# Plugin API

Public types a consumer plugin uses. Signatures are from `src/Progenitor.Core` (Dev ops from
`src/Progenitor.Dev`). Narrative and traps: [`modules.md`](modules.md). Terms:
[`glossary.md`](glossary.md). Worked example:
[`samples/Progenitor.Sample`](../samples/Progenitor.Sample).

**Status** is the same vocabulary the roadmap and `modules.md` use, and each row's status is the one
the module doc's own evidence supports:

| Status | Means |
|---|---|
| **verified live** | Exercised in the running game and cited in the evidence log. |
| **landed** | Committed and tested, not yet verified live (or the owed live check is still open). |
| **experimental** | Deliberately incomplete, Dev-only, or a documented subset of what it names. |
| **parked** | Works, and further work on it is stopped by decision. |

**Only the rows in the tables below are contract.** `public` in the source is not the boundary: the
four assemblies (Core, Bootstrap, Dev and the test library Progenitor.Testing) declare **1001** public types, including types outside the contract tables. Those types are
implementation that is public for a stated reason — the test project compiles it, Bootstrap and
Core must agree on a shape across an assembly boundary they are forbidden to reference, the Dev
bridge needs it, or a generator emitted it — and it carries no compatibility promise. Every one of
them is listed, with that reason, in
[Appendix: framework-internal public types](#appendix-framework-internal-public-types), so a reader
can tell contract from implementation without reading `src/`. `ApiSurfaceTests` fails if a public
type appears in neither place.

**Example** is the fourth column of every table below. It names the worked example that exercises
that row — a file under [`samples/Progenitor.Sample`](../samples/Progenitor.Sample) plus the command
or keybind that drives it, `unit test` when only `tests/Progenitor.Tests` reaches it, or **none**
when nothing in the repository does. It exists because *verified live* and *has a working example*
are different claims and this file used to conflate them — `ctx.Hooks` and `ctx.Chat` were both
marked verified live on the strength of framework spikes and Dev ops, with no consumer plugin
calling either, which is a state this column makes visible instead of arguable. It also makes the
public-repo gate ("working examples of all the Progenitor functionality",
[`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md) M2.10) countable rather than a judgment call:

```
grep -c '| \*\*none\*\*' docs/api.md      # documented members with no worked example
```

Register through `PluginContext` (`ctx`) so unload reverses the work. `ProgenitorPlugin<TContext>`
(`TContext : PluginContext, new()`), `[ProgenitorSide(Side)]`, `[BepInDependency("progenitor")]`.
`[NetworkCompatibility(level, versionRule)]` is applied after `OnLoad` (`DeclareSelf` is idempotent).
`ProgenitorPlugin.WhenWorldReady(ctx, world => …)` runs now or once on the matching `WorldReady`.
`Worlds.Find` / `Server` (`"Server"`) / `Client` (`"Client_0"`).

## The `ctx.*` facades

Every facade on `PluginContext`, and nothing that is not one. `Ui` is **not** a `ctx` property —
call `Progenitor.Ui.Ui` statically and pass `owner` + `pluginId`.

| Facade | Status | What it is | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Log` | verified live | This plugin's BepInEx `ManualLogSource`, named after the plugin. | `Plugin.cs` (every module logs through it) |
| `ctx.Config` | verified live | Typed binds over this plugin's `GUID.cfg`. Refuses the shared `progenitor.cfg`; `synced: true` throws here. | `SampleUi.cs` (3 local binds behind `.ui demo`) |
| `ctx.Bind(section, key, default, description, synced, acceptableValues)` / `ctx.Bind(section, key, default, ConfigDescription, synced)` | verified live | The synced bind. Tracks `ConfigSync.Register`; BCL primitive / `string` / BCL enum only. `acceptableValues` (an `AcceptableValueRange`/`List`, or a full `ConfigDescription`) works on a synced entry: bounds stay local metadata and a synced value outside them is clamped on the client with a log line. | `Plugin.cs` (synced `Sample.EmberStack`, read by `.order`) |
| `ctx.Events` | verified live | Event-bus subscriptions, recorded for undo. | `Plugin.cs` (`WorldReady`, `PlayerConnected` -> `.joins`) |
| `ctx.Hooks` | verified live | ECS system Before/After hooks, recorded for undo. | `SampleHooks.cs` (`.hooks`, `.hooks skip`) |
| `ctx.Systems` | verified live | Named virtual systems — callbacks, not injected `SystemBase`. | `Plugin.cs` (`sample.users`) |
| `ctx.Commands` | verified live | Chat-command registrations (`[Command]`, prefix `.`). | `Plugin.cs` -> `SampleCommands.cs` (15 commands) |
| `ctx.Chat` | verified live | Server chat send bound to this plugin's world. 0 / false on the client. Sends take effect at once and in order; several from one handler or event (for example `ServantsReturned`) all arrive, with no need to defer them. A Progenitor client's chat view took only the newest line of a same-frame burst once the game's 50-entry list was full; fixed 2026-10-17, game-free tested, not live-verified ([new lines](chat.md#chat-view-as-built-chat4-to-chat9)). | `SampleChat.cs` (`.shout`, `.whisper`) |
| `ctx.Content` | verified live | Cloned item / buff / recipe definitions and ordered prefab editors. | `Plugin.cs` (`.ember`), `Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` (`DefineBuff`, `.rested`), `SampleRecipe.cs` (`sample.bench.crystal-thread`, offline) |
| `ctx.Save` | verified live | Per-save JSON bag (`IModStore`). Server: beside the world save. Client: a no-op store. | `Plugin.cs` (`.joins`) |
| `ctx.ServerSave` | verified live | Server-level JSON bag (`IModStore`) that survives wipes and world restores. Server: `<persistentDataPath>\progenitor-server`. Client: a no-op store. [Where should my data go?](modules.md#where-should-my-data-go) | `Plugin.cs` (the all-time join book), `SampleStore.cs` (`.store server`) |
| `ctx.Net` | verified live | Custom events to a user, all users, or the server. | `SampleFx.cs`, `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleMods.cs` (server to client); `SampleNet.cs` (the F8 client to server round trip); `SampleRifts.cs` (a server-authoritative state packet on join, on change and on a heartbeat) |
| `ctx.Handshake` | verified live | Mod-version rows; a peer missing a required mod is kicked with a message. | `Plugin.cs` (`DeclareSelf`), `SampleHandshake.cs` (`Require`) |
| `ctx.Features` (`PluginFeatures`) | verified live (2026-10-09, see [Networking](#networking)) | Features this mod offers the other side: `Offer(name, level = 1)` returns a tracked handle; `Offered`. Unload withdraws them. [Feature negotiation](modules.md#feature-negotiation-and-versioned-messages-mod-compatibility-c1c2). | `samples/Progenitor.CompatTest` (test plugin) |
| `ctx.Peer` (`PluginPeer`) / `ctx.Peers` (`PluginPeers`) | verified live (2026-10-09) | Client: the joined server's features and mod versions (`Known`, `Has`, `ModVersion`, `CoreVersion`, `Changed`). Server: `Peers.Of(user)` per connected client, `Peers.Changed(Entity)`. | `samples/Progenitor.CompatTest` (test plugin) |
| `ctx.Messages` (`PluginMessages`) | verified live (2026-10-09) | Versioned, tolerant messages on Core's `'PrMs'` envelope: `On<T>`, `SendToServer`, `SendTo`, `SendToAll`, plus the hand-written `MessageWriter` / `MessageReader` overloads. Sends are gated on the peer's capability and feature table. | `samples/Progenitor.CompatTest` (test plugin) |
| `ctx.Requests` (`PluginRequests`) / `ctx.Subscriptions` (`PluginSubscriptions`) | landed (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | Typed request and reply (`Handle`, `HandleDeferred`, `Send`, `SendAsync`) and snapshot-then-delta subscriptions to keyed server state (`Topic`, `Subscribe`), both chunked above one packet on Core's `'PrXc'`. Tracked: unload removes handlers and topics and ends requests and subscriptions. [Requests, large messages and subscriptions](modules.md#requests-large-messages-and-subscriptions). | `SampleExchange.cs` (`.request`, `.subscribe`); unit tests `ExchangeEngineTests`, `ExchangeWireTests` |
| `ctx.Castles` | verified live | Server castle plot / tile / building reads and handles on this plugin's world. | `SampleCastle.cs` (`.castle`) |
| `ctx.MaterialSource` (`PluginMaterialSource`; alias `ctx.Stations.MaterialSource`) | experimental, opt-in; Build, Craft, Repair and Upgrade live-verified on the test rig 2026-10-14 (other screens, a claim staying vanilla and the client's equipped-repair path not); CharacterCraft live-verified on the test rig 2026-10-14 (the client's own craft click not) | The one build, craft, Character window craft, repair and castle heart upgrade material provider: register, invalidate, read what is armed. The native seams are opt-in (`[Materials] NativeSeams`, client and server restart). [Material sources](#material-sources). | `samples/Progenitor.SharedResourcesTest` (test plugin); unit tests `MaterialServiceTests`, `MaterialTransactionTests`, `MaterialViewSyncTests` |
| `ctx.Inventory` (`PluginInventory`) | landed | Server inventories of any kind: read, count, free space, add, remove, a total-conserving transfer, watches, stable container ids. Client throws. [Inventory](#inventory). | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items chests`, `.items move`) |
| `ctx.Clans` (`PluginClans`) | landed | Server clans: a user's clan, its members and its castles. Client throws. [Clans](#clans). | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items clan`) |
| `ctx.Servants` (`PluginServants`) | experimental | Server castle servants and hunts: the throne's list, the heart's hunt record, server sends and resends with the game's checks, the `ServantsReturned` event, success chances, a one-provider throne list swap, and per-user filters for the game's hunt chat lines (`SuppressGameNotices`). Client throws, except `MissionDisplayName` (client only) and `MissionToken`. [Servants](#servants). | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants`) |
| `ctx.Units` | verified live | Server unit spawn and steering. Client returns disabled handles. | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat`) |
| `ctx.Items` | verified live (grant, equip); landed (catalog) | Server inventory grant and equip (client throws; defaults stack, accept a partial add, do not equip, leave durability), and a read-only item catalog on both sides. [Items](#items). | `SampleItems.cs` (`.items grant`, `.items equip`); unit test `ItemGrantPolicyTests`; live `give` / `equip` on the isolated server (the wrapper sample itself is not live-verified) |
| `ctx.Stations` (`PluginStations`) | landed, not live-verified | Server castle recipe stations: a station's recipes (inputs, outputs, duration, enabled), the refinement station's own per-recipe switch, a pure whole-craft count, and current progress. Also braziers: read a burning structure's switch, burn state and fuel, and set its switch. Every member except `CraftsPossible` throws on the client. [Stations](#stations), [modules.md](modules.md#stations). | `SampleStations.cs` (`.stations`), `SampleBraziers.cs` (`.brazier`); unit tests `StationLogicTests`, `SortBrazierPositionTests` |
| `ctx.Abilities` (`PluginAbilities`) | landed; `SetSlot` live-verified, `ForceCast`/`NoCooldowns` exercised by the necro sample | Server ability control: `ForceCast` through `ServerGameManager.ForceCastAbilityGroup` (starts groups that are not on the bar), and `NoCooldowns` holds that keep a character's abilities off cooldown with full charges, and `SetSlot`, which puts a chosen group on a bar slot. Holds are tracked. Client throws. [Abilities](#abilities), [modules.md](modules.md#abilities). | unit tests `AbilityCooldownPolicyTests`, `AbilitySlotPolicyTests`; sample `Progenitor.NecroArmyExample` |
| `ctx.Time` (`PluginTime`) | landed, not live-verified | Server time of day: read it (`GetTimeOfDay`), move it and let it run (`SetTimeOfDay`), or freeze it (`HoldTimeOfDay`) for filming. Connected clients' lighting follows through the game's replicated day/night fields; the game clock `ServerTime` and every timer are untouched. Overrides are tracked and stack. Also world speed: `Freeze` / `SlowMotion` stop or slow the whole simulation (and the game clock) on the server and every client through the game's replicated `TimeScale`. On the client only `GetTimeOfDay` works (the client's own clock); the rest throws. [Time of day](#time-of-day), [World speed](#world-speed), [modules.md](modules.md#time-of-day). | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time`), `SampleSpeed.cs` (`.speed`); unit tests `TimeOfDayMathTests`, `TimeScalePolicyTests` |
| `ctx.Ai` (`PluginAi`) | landed, not live-verified | Server boss AI: describe/observe, cast-option edits, health gates, phase thresholds, private behavior-tree copies, factions, named variants with a persistence marker. Every mutation returns a restoring handle and is tracked. Client throws. [Ai](#ai), [ai.md](ai.md). | `samples/Progenitor.BossVariantsExample` (`.bossvariants spawn`); unit test `AiCodecTests` |
| `ctx.Debug` (`PluginDebug`) `Protect` / `KeepAlive` / `Release` | landed | `Protect` applies Core's debug buff (`progenitor.debug`, the [`.debug`](modules.md#debug-tools-debug) buff) when that clone is registered, otherwise `Admin_Invulnerable_Buff` and the stealth no-aggro buff), sets `Immortal`, and takes the kill-path guard. `KeepAlive` sets `Immortal`, the kill-path guard, and invulnerability (`Admin_Invulnerable_Buff`, unless a debug buff that stays already covers it). It takes off the no-drain and stealth buffs only if `ctx.Debug` put them on and no other holder still asks for `Protect`, and forces `Aggroable` on only when no foe-ignoring buff stays. `Immortal` alone does not stop the downed screen. `Release` drops this plugin's guard; what is left converges on the strongest request the remaining debug holders (another plugin's `ctx.Debug` or the bridge `protect` op) still make, and with none left the buffs and an `Immortal` grant this framework made come off; an unrelated `ctx.GodMode` guard does not keep them. **Ownership (2026-10-07):** `ctx.Debug` only ever removes a buff it applied itself, and never one Core's `.debug` holds a grant for; it does not clear `Immortal` while Core's `.debug` holds a grant. A kept buff shows as `IgnoredByFoes` and `KeptOtherOwnersBuff`. Buff removal is deferred to the next frame; `Ok`, `IgnoredByFoes` and `Invulnerable` describe the state after it lands and `Pending` says one is in flight. The decisions are `DebugProtectionPolicy` (game-free tested). Every character a plugin still holds is released on unload, for every reason. The record of whether `Immortal` was already on is process memory keyed by world epoch, so after a server restart or a Core reload `Release` leaves `Immortal` as it finds it. Plugins are released before a Core reload unloads them. Returns `DebugProtectionResult` (`Ok`, `Immortal`, `Guarded`, `IgnoredByFoes`, `Invulnerable`, `ImmortalGranted`, `Pending`, `KeptOtherOwnersBuff`). Client throws. | `SampleDebugProtect.cs` (`.protect on` is `Protect`, `.protect alive` is `KeepAlive`, `.protect off` is `Release`). Source-checked and game-free tested; not live-verified. The bridge `protect` op is the same protection with `mode` `alive` = `KeepAlive`. |
| `ctx.Buffs` (`PluginBuffs`) | landed (port) | Server timed-buff application for buffs this plugin defined: apply, query, refresh, remove, with an owned handle and structured outcomes. Client calls are refused with an actionable reason. Live-qualified 2026-09-14 on the pre-port branch. On main (2026-10-02): apply, refusal, expiry, removal, short refresh and a Core swap with live buffs verified live; the drain number, HUD and plugin-only swap not yet (see [Buffs](#buffs)). | `samples/Progenitor.TimedBuffExample` (`.rested`, `.unrested`, `.restedstatus`), `samples/Exercise.TimedBuff` (`.adrenaline`, `.adrenalinestatus`, `.calmdown`) |
| `ctx.Effects` | verified live / parked | Client vanilla-asset borrow **and** server-authored beams. Beams work and are parked (see below). | `SampleFx.cs` (`.fx`), `SampleBeam.cs` (`.beam`) |
| `ctx.Camera` (`PluginCamera`) | landed, not live-verified | Client camera for cinematic shots: `SetMode`, orbit and free shots written every frame with player camera input suppressed and lighting anchored on the shot's focus, `Reset`. Server throws. Unload (every reason) and Core uninstall reset the owner to top-down. The mode switch was verified live via scratch; this implementation is unit-tested only. [Camera](#camera), [modules.md](modules.md#camera). | `SampleCamera.cs` (`.shot`); `samples/Progenitor.NecroArmyExample/NecroCamera.cs` (`FreeShotLookAt`, `Reset`); unit test `CameraTests`. The Sample is source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. |
| `ctx.Players` (`PluginPlayers`) | landed, not live-verified | Server player directory: who is online, and lookup by display name or platform id. An ambiguous name returns every candidate. Session entity refs stay valid only while `IsFresh`. Where each online character stands (`OnlinePositions`, one read per player per call). `State` (experimental) reads dead, downed, PvP combat and form. Read-only. [Players directory](#players-directory). | `SamplePlayerDirectory.cs` (`.players`, `.players where`, `.players state`) |
| `ctx.Triggers` (`PluginTriggers`) | verified live | Server area triggers. When a connected player enters or leaves a circle or an axis-aligned rectangle, both with a finite height, run an action (for example `ctx.Units.Spawn`). The first time that player is seen, the watch only records which side they are on. The client returns an inactive handle. Tracked. Live on the isolated test server, 2026-10-08: circle leave, rectangle leave, and a rat spawned on return. A point 4 m up was snapped back to the ground, so one floor above stays the game-free test. [Triggers](#triggers). | `SampleTriggers.cs` (`.triggers on`); unit tests `AreaTriggerTests`, `SampleTriggerTests` |
| `ctx.Models` (`PluginModels`) | landed; load verified live | Client presentation prefabs: stream in a game model that is not loaded (for example `HYB_NPCDracula_Evolved`) by its entity-scene section, hold it, and make inert owned copies. `ModelCatalog` reads the name table `tools/catalog/model-sections.mjs` writes. Server loads fail with `server-side`. Unload (every reason) and Core uninstall destroy copies and release holds. [modules.md](modules.md#models). | unit test `ModelsTests`; `samples/Progenitor.FilmExample` |
| `ctx.Film` (`PluginFilm`) | landed; verified live | Client film cameras and lock-step capture. Server calls throw. Unload stops captures and releases cameras. [Camera](#camera). | `samples/Progenitor.FilmExample` |
| `ctx.Menu` (`PluginMenu`) | landed; `OpenPage` source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified | Tracked Mods-menu registration for this plugin. Registers the plugin cfg as a settings source. `OpenPage(title)` opens the Mods menu at this plugin's custom page through the game's menu. [Ui](#ui). | `SampleMenu.cs` (`ctx.Menu.Register`; Shift+F9 calls `ctx.Menu.OpenPage`); unit test `ModPageOpenTests` |
| `ctx.Input` (`PluginInput`) | landed, not live-verified | Client input leases for the local player: aim at a point or unit, hover a unit, walk, press synced buttons, one owner per channel, refused unless preempting, paused while the user uses a channel unless `UserPolicy.Override`. Every lease ends on unload (every reason), Core swap, world change, disconnect, character change and the Ctrl+Shift+F12 release key. Server acquire returns `ServerSide`. The underlying writes were verified live in the necro sample and the Dev bot driver (2026-09-24/25); this implementation is unit-tested only. [Input](#input), [input.md](input.md). | `SampleInput.cs` (`.input`); unit test `InputTests` |
| `ctx.Keybinds` | verified live | Client keybinds backed by a `[Keybinds]` entry in this plugin's `GUID.cfg`; also registers this plugin as a settings source. A player exercised the Sample's F9 and Ctrl+F9 binds and the F10 settings opener on 2026-09-04 (evidence 161, 163, 164, 167, 173; `README.md` "the UI toolkit met a player", `roadmap.md:50`) — this row said *landed, live check owed* until job FDOC10 reconciled it with those two. | `SampleUi.cs` (F9 and Ctrl+F9), `SampleLogPanel.cs` (the Ctrl+F9 panel) |
| `ctx.Settings` (`PluginSettings`) | landed (SETTINGS1 stage 2) — source-checked and game-free tested, **not live-verified** | The layout of this plugin's settings on its Mods page: `Page(Action<SettingsPageBuilder>)`, bound to its config entries (the entries stay the source of truth; writes go through the settings catalog's validated path). Registers the plugin's cfg as a settings source, like `ctx.Menu`. Tracked: every unload reason drops the page, its delegates and the admin-edit registrations a `scope: Server` row made, and closes the page if it is on show. Once per plugin. [Ui](#ui), [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#the-settings-page-builder-stage-2). | `SampleMenu.cs` (Mods workspace, Progenitor Sample page); unit test `ModSettingsStage2Tests` |
| `ctx.Dialogs` (`PluginDialogs`) | landed (DIALOGS1) — source-checked and game-free tested, **not live-verified** | Ready-made modal dialogs in the framework's own dialog layout: `Confirm`, `Notice`, `Choose`, `Prompt`, each returning a handle that closes it. One on screen at a time, later ones queued (FIFO); Enter is the main action, Escape the dismissal; the footer names the mod and its version unless `ShowModVersion` / `showModVersion` turns it off. Client only (the server logs once and returns a do-nothing handle). Tracked: every unload reason drops this plugin's dialogs, shown and queued, without running a callback. Named `Dialogs`, not `Ui`, so it cannot hide the static `Ui` class inside a context subclass. [Ui](#ui), [ui.md](ui.md#dialogs). | `SampleDialogs.cs` (Mods workspace → Progenitor Sample → Dialogs); unit test `DialogTests` |
| `ctx.Hud` (`PluginHud`) | experimental (P7, P7b) — source-checked and game-free tested; `Notify` (the game's banner, the framework band it falls back to, the band's notice kinds), `PickInWorld` (one-shot and `Repeat`, Escape precedence) and `Tip` live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); `Locate` and `TryFindByNetworkId` **not live-verified**; not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller) | `Notify(text, detail = "", UiNoticeKind kind = Info, seconds = 0, prefix = true)` → `IUiNotice` on the game's announcement banner (queued; a framework band where there is none), shown as `<Mod Name>: text` unless `prefix: false` or the text already opens with `Name:`, `[Name]` or `(Name)` (WINDOWS1, game-free tested, not live-verified); `Locate(Entity target, label = "", seconds = 10)` / `Locate(float3 position, …)` / `Locate(float3 position, label, seconds, arrivalDistance, showOnMaps)` → `IUiLocatePing` (a marker 1.6 m above the target and an edge arrow; the label reads `<label> · <meters>`, the real 3D distance from the local character to the target; `position` is world meters with `y` up, as `Translation`; `arrivalDistance` > 0 ends the ping within that 3D distance and is never displayed); `TryFindByNetworkId(index, generation, type, out Entity, out string)` (the client entity for network id words the server sent); `PickInWorld(Action<UiWorldPickResult> onPicked, Action? onCancelled = null, UiWorldPickOptions? options = null)` → `IUiWorldPick` (the next interact on a container or station, its panel kept closed, Escape cancels; P7b: `UiWorldPickOptions.Repeat` keeps picking until Escape or `Cancel()` finishes it, `IUiWorldPick.PickCount` counts the targets). P7b tip cards: `Tip(id, title, body, UiTipOptions? options = null)` → `IUiTip` (a one-time card in the game's popup look; never over a modal; once per player), `DefineTip(id, title, body)`, `Tips` → `IReadOnlyList<UiTipInfo>`, `IsTipSeen(id)`, `ShowAllTips()`, `ResetTips()`, `ResetTip(id)`. Client only; tracked on every unload reason. [ui.md](ui.md#hud-notifications-locate-and-pick-in-world), [tip cards](ui.md#tip-cards). | `ControlsSample/Plugin.cs` (`ControlsHud`, the Widgets page's HUD and Layout tabs); unit tests `UiNoticeTests`, `UiLocateTests`, `UiWorldPickTests`, `UiWorldPickRepeatTests`, `UiTipsTests` |
| `ctx.Windows` (`PluginWindows`) | landed (WINDOWS1) — game-free tested, not live-verified | Standard windows: `Create(UiWindowPreset preset, string title, UiWindowOptions? options = null)` → `IUiWindow` (not shown yet; `Basic`, `Header` or `Tabbed`, each fixing the frame art, title and spacing, the version stamp `<plugin name> <version> · Progenitor <version>`, the input mode and Escape); `Open` (the plugin's windows not yet disposed); `VersionStamp` (its default stamp line). Client only (a disabled window on the server). Tracked: every unload reason disposes every window. | `ctx.Patches` | landed | Tagged Harmony patches with an unload-safe unpatch, routed through Bootstrap's slot pool. The only patching path that keeps a plugin hot-swappable. | `SamplePatchDemo.cs` (`.patchdemo`) |
| `ctx.GodMode` | experimental | Mark entities the game's unguarded kill paths may not kill. Server-only. | `SampleGodModeDemo.cs` (`.godmode`) |
| `ctx.WorldState` | landed | The world-load epoch: `LoadEpoch`, `Loads`, `IsStale`, `Describe`. | `SampleEpoch.cs` (`.epoch`) |
| `ctx.UnsafeHarmony` | landed | This plugin's raw Harmony id, and an **opt-out of hot reload** (job FHARM7; was `ctx.Harmony`). Reading it warns once per plugin and marks the plugin non-hot-swappable — the reloader then refuses to swap it and asks for a restart. Unload still calls `UnpatchSelf`, which does not release the ALC. | **none**, deliberately. `SamplePatchDemo.cs` (`.patchdemo unsafe`) explains the property *without reading it* (the read is the opt-out), and unit test: `SamplePatchDemoTests.cs` (`No_Sample_source_file_reads_ctx_UnsafeHarmony`) keeps every Sample file that way |
| `ctx.World` | verified live | The side's ECS `World`, or null before bootstrap. | `Plugin.cs` and others (17 reads) |
| `ctx.Side` / `IsServer` / `IsClient` | verified live | Process role. `Side` is never `Both`. | `Plugin.cs` (`if (ctx.IsServer)`) |
| `ctx.Guid` / `Name` / `Version` | verified live | BepInEx metadata. | `SampleFx.cs`, `SampleUi.cs` (`ctx.Guid`); `SampleMods.cs` (`.mods about`: all three together, next to the handshake row they feed) |
| `ctx.Owner` | verified live | The owner key for every registration — the BepInEx plugin instance. | `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleFx.cs` (owner on every static call) |
| `ctx.Track(IDisposable)` | verified live | Anything the wrappers do not cover: native detours, extra Harmony ids, file handles. | `Plugin.cs` |
| `ctx.IsRetired` | landed (source-checked only; not unit-tested, not live-verified) | True once unload starts reversing the context's registrations, after `OnUnload` has returned (calls inside `OnUnload` still work and are swept). From then on `ctx.Requests.Send` ends at once as `NotSent` with `NotSent = SendResult.Invalid` and `Unloading` set, `ctx.Subscriptions.Subscribe` returns an ended subscription without a callback, and `ctx.Hud` returns inert handles, because a registration made then would never be swept and would pin the plugin's load context. A `ctx.Track` after it disposes the handle at once. | none. The guards have no game-free seam (`PluginContext` is not in the test project); `ExchangeEngineTests` covers only the engine's `Unloading` mark on an unload cancellation |

## Plugin lifecycle

| Type / member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ProgenitorPlugin<TContext>.OnLoad(TContext ctx)` | verified live | Abstract. Runs on the matching side after the context is attached. | `Plugin.cs` |
| `ProgenitorPlugin<TContext>.OnUnload(TContext ctx)` | verified live | Optional cleanup. Unchanged; still called by default. | `Plugin.cs` (`OnUnload`) |
| `ProgenitorPlugin<TContext>.OnUnload(TContext ctx, PluginUnloadReason reason)` | landed | Same hook with the reason. The default body calls the parameterless overload, so exactly one of the two runs. | `Plugin.cs` |
| `PluginUnloadReason` `Swap` / `Removal` / `Shutdown` / `Unknown` / `Suspend` | landed (`Suspend`: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | Core generation change / the DLL is gone / the process is exiting / no reason established / paused for the server this client joined ([per-server mod pause](mods-menu.md#per-server-mod-pause); the plugin comes back when the client leaves). Only `Removal` sweeps. `Suspend` needs a Bootstrap that carries slot code 4; an older one never sends it. | `SampleLog.cs` (`UnloadMessage`: `KeepsState`, so `Suspend` keeps); Core's debug buff (`DebugBuff.ShouldSweep`, `Removal` only); unit test: `PluginUnloadReasonTests.cs` (`A_suspend_is_carried_through_the_slot_and_keeps_state`) |
| `PluginUnloadReasonPolicy.KeepsState(reason)` | landed | `reason != Removal`, i.e. the table above in one call. | `Plugin.cs` via `SampleLog.UnloadMessage` |

The `Reload: <name> unload reason=<r> (<source>)` line. | unit test: `PluginUnloadReasonTests.cs` (`A_signalled_reason_wins_over_every_other_source`); Core: `Reload/PluginReloader.cs` |
| `PluginUnloadScope.Push(reason)` → `IDisposable` / `.Current` | landed | How a reloader signals the reason for the unload it is running. | unit test: `PluginUnloadReasonTests.cs` (`The_slot_is_scoped_to_one_unload_and_restores_the_previous_reason`); Core: `Reload/PluginReloader.cs` |

## Events

Typed bus; Core owns each game hook. Higher `priority` first; `before`/`after` plugin ids;
`ctx.Events.On` passes `ctx.Guid` as `pluginId`.

When an event type's last subscription goes (dispose, `RemoveAll`, the unload sweep), the bus drops that type, so a plugin that declares its own event type is not pinned after unload (offline-tested with a collectible assembly, not live-verified). | `SampleCommands.cs` (`.order` -> `DescribeOrder`) |
| `ICancellable` | verified live | Later handlers still run; `IsCancelled` is set. | `SampleEvents.cs` — `.events cancel on`, then a chat line starting `!!`: the canceler at priority 400 cancels, and `SampleChat.cs`'s observer at -100 still runs and logs `handled=True` |

| `PlayerDisconnected` (`Reason`) | verified live | Server, prefix of disconnect. | `SampleEvents.cs` — `.events` (a player leaving raises it) |

Removing more than one needs the previewed count; no refund; a piece that may hold items is skipped. Same admin rule. [modules.md](modules.md#build-mode-debug-build) | built-in - `DetachedPiecesTests` (unit test) |

## Content

`ctx.Content.DefineItem(id).CloneOf(guid|name).MaxAmount.Named.Described.Icon.Register()` — `id` is ASCII
1–63, hashed with `PrefabGUID.CalculateGuidHash`. `ctx.Content.DefineRecipe` is the workstation
recipe builder (`RecipeDefinition` / `RecipeDefinitionBuilder` / `RecipeStationKind` /
`RecipeItemRef` / `RecipeStationRef` / `RecipeApplyPlan`); see [recipes.md](recipes.md).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PrefabRegistry.DefineItem` / `ItemDefinitionBuilder` | verified live | Clone an item prefab; client sync `PrfD`. `.Wearable` stores worn graphics on the definition (not a wire field). | `Plugin.cs` (`.ember`); `src/Progenitor.Core/Content/GuideOutfit.cs` (built-in; assets in `samples/Progenitor.RobeSet`) (`.Wearable`) |
| `ItemDefinitionBuilder.Icon(path)` | compile-verified; live UI pending | Client PNG under the plugin assets folder replaces the cloned `ManagedItemData.Icon`. Uses the process-owned sprite cache across reloads; omit to retain the donor icon. Custom plugins require matching client assets; no icon wire field. Guide icons are embedded in Core. | `src/Progenitor.Core/Content/GuideOutfit.cs` (built-in; assets in `samples/Progenitor.RobeSet`) |
| `ctx.Models.CaptureRigReference` / `RigReference.Capture` / `RigReferenceSnapshot.Save` / `PoseSurfaceSnapshot` | compile-verified; live capture pending | Explicit client/main-thread full readable reference export: hierarchy, rest/pose matrices, topology, UVs, normals, weights and baked vertices. Never overwrites a capture. [Blender workflow](../tools/rig-reference/README.md). | `RigReferenceTests`; `tools/rig-reference` |
| `ctx.Content.DefineItem(...).StatBonuses(new ItemStatBonuses(maxHealth, physicalPower, spellPower))` | under development; limited live stat/reconnect checks | Adds bounded main-stat bonuses to the donor on a registered clone. Rebuilds authored rows without stacking; cannot combine with ArmorBaseline. Server-only data packs synchronize definitions and bonuses to compatible clients. | [Data-only packs](content-packs.md); bone-sword pack |
| `ctx.Content.DefineItem(...).ArmorBaseline(displayedLevel, removeBonuses, removeSet)` | verified on isolated Guide items | Sets displayed level (1–100), optionally clears the set association and inherited item stat buffer. Preserves base equip buff. Apply the same recipe on both sides; re-equip existing items to refresh totals. | `src/Progenitor.Core/Content/GuideOutfit.cs` (built-in; assets in `samples/Progenitor.RobeSet`) |
| `ctx.Content.DefineItem(...).BloodBound()` / `.Indestructible()` | source-checked, built, game-free tested; not live-verified | `BloodBound` sets `ItemCategory.BloodBound` on the clone and its item lookup row, which the death drop reads, so the item stays with the player on death, in the bag too. With the server's blood-bound equipment setting off the game strips that bit from Equippable items only while registering its own prefabs, before clones exist (traced in native code, not live-verified); the server re-checks the clone and its row on every connect and re-sets the bit with a warning if it was cleared. `Indestructible` sets `Durability.LossType` None, the take-damage factor 0, `DestroyItemWhenBroken` false and full durability on the clone, and clears `ItemCategory.LoseDurabilityOnDeath`, so neither combat nor death wears the item. The server rearms existing instances, worn or not, at registration and at the first player connect of each world load. Register the same recipe on both sides; not a wire field. An option dropped from a re-registered id reverts the clone to the donor's rules; existing instances keep theirs. Live recipe: [Guide robes](../samples/Progenitor.RobeSet/README.md#blood-bound-and-indestructible). | `src/Progenitor.Core/Content/GuideOutfit.cs` (all four Guide items); `ItemKeepRulesTests` |
| `WearableGraphics.FromAuthoredJson(slot, json, hideNames)` | compile-verified; live authored loading pending | Loads original garment JSON exported from Blender; retains authored skin/UVs/normals and cloth/gold/void surfaces. Bone paths and rest transforms must match; one rest skeleton per asset; explicit alternatives use `FromAuthoredJsonVariants`. No cloth simulation. | `WearableAuthoredTests`; `tools/rig-reference/test_export.py` |
| `WearableGraphics` / `WearableSketch` / `WearableLooks` / `WearableSlot` / `WearableSurface` / `WearablePaletteMode` | landed | Sketch a worn mesh for an armor or weapon slot. The client fits original geometry to authored rig/body references and transfers compatible skin weights. Model names are diagnostic only. Grants keep `ItemData.Entity` on the clone. Geometry tests pass; female coverage and shape were confirmed by eye in tested combat moves. Male and broader animation qualification remain open. [wearables.md](wearables.md) | `src/Progenitor.Core/Content/GuideOutfit.cs` (built-in; assets in `samples/Progenitor.RobeSet`); `WearableMeshTests` |
| `PrefabRegistry.DefineBuff` / `BuffDefinitionBuilder` (`Icon`, `RefreshLiveInstances`) | verified live | Clone a buff on the item packet path; the icon ships in the plugin's `assets/` folder. | `Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` (`DefineBuff`); `Icon` + `RefreshLiveInstances`: Core's own debug buff (`src/Progenitor.Core/Debugging/DebugBuff.cs`, `.debug on`, icon embedded in Core) |
| `ctx.Content.DefineRecipe` / `RecipeDefinitionBuilder` | landed | Clone a recipe prefab onto a vanilla workstation. Offline builder, apply plan and unregister ledger; not live-verified. `Named`/`Described` are refused (the output item carries the name). A registered definition's `Inputs`, `Stations` and plan steps are read-only copies (offline-tested). | `SampleRecipe.cs` (`sample.bench.crystal-thread`; `sample.ember.craft` is a secondary custom-item variant not registered from `OnLoad`) |
| `content/*.json` files (`ContentFiles`, schema 1) | evolving | Define cloned **items** from JSON next to the plugin, no compiler: `id`, `cloneOf` (name or GUID hash), `name`, `description`, `stack`, `statBonuses`; the same `DefineItem` builder, read after `OnLoad`, owned by the plugin. Unknown field / wrong schema / 63-byte overrun refuses that file whole, naming file, item index and field; an id the plugin already defines in C# refuses the file. `docs/content-files.md` | `samples/Progenitor.Sample/content/items.json` (`sample.ash`, "Ash Shard") |
| `PrefabRegistry.UnregisterAll(owner)` / `Definitions` / `Describe` | verified live | Soft unapply (`PrfU`) plus a key-row tombstone (`PrfK`). | `SampleContent.cs` — `.content` (`Describe` + `Definitions`), `.content define` then `.content drop` (`UnregisterAll` under its own owner; watch `tombstones=` go up) |
| `ctx.Content.Edit(guid, editor, priority, before, after)` | evolving | Ordered pass on both sides; the **server's** result wins — after a pass the server ships the touched components as `PrfE` and the client re-applies that layer over its own editors (job FEDW1; server round trip and two-process check pending). Still needs `ServerMustHave` + `Exact\|Minor` (phase 2 relaxes it). Removal (Dispose / unload) restores the components that were written, server layer included (value writes only, not structural changes). A server layer is connection-scoped: when the client's connection ends it is withdrawn and the client's own values come back, and a new client world re-runs local editors on its fresh entities (FSCOPE1; live-accepted 2026-09-28 for one client process switching A→B→A on the isolated test server, on the `fix/client-server-switch-scope` build; the port to main is offline-tested only). | `Plugin.cs` (a deliberate Default/Late conflict) |
| `PrefabEditHandle` `Has/Read/TryRead/Write<T>`, `GetComponent/SetComponent<T>` | verified live | `ItemData` is not C# `unmanaged`: use `GetComponent`/`SetComponent`. No `PrefabGUID` / Prefab-tag retarget. | `Plugin.cs` (`Has` / `GetComponent` / `SetComponent`); `SampleContent.cs`: `.content define`'s editor does the whole guarded shape in order, `TryRead<PrefabGUID>` (identity check), then `Has<DestroyData>` -> `TryRead<DestroyData>` -> `Write(in destroy)` (`DestroyReason` Default -> Consume, the one plain-byte component the Crystal clone carries) -> `Read<DestroyData>()` read-back. Removing the editor restores the vanilla value from Core's baseline (FEDIT1); `DestroyData` is not on the `PrfE` allowlist, so the edit does not travel and Core says so once |
| `PrefabEditPriority` `Early=-1000` / `Default=0` / `Late=1000` | verified live | Last writer of a component wins; the conflict line names both plugins. | `Plugin.cs` |
| `Invalidate` / `InvalidateEdits(owner)` (extensions in `PluginContentEdits`) | verified live | Re-run after captured inputs (a synced entry) change. | `Plugin.cs` |

Both halves of the old "open by design" clause are closed as of 2026-09-05. Removing an editor
(Dispose, plugin unload, Unrequire sweep, Core uninstall) restores the components it wrote from a
lazily captured baseline and re-runs the remaining editors (job FEDIT1); and edited values **are**
on the wire — after a server pass the touched components ship as a `PrfE` packet and the client
applies them as the final layer over its own editors (jobs FEDW1–FEDW3, design
`docs/edits-on-the-wire.md`), so both sides no longer have to run the same editors.

Still open: structural edits through `handle.EntityManager` (component add/remove) are not tracked
and so not restorable; `ServerMustHave` + `Exact|Minor` is still required until phase 2 relaxes it
for wire-safe edits; and the two-process live check on a real client is owed.

## Config

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PluginConfig.Bind` / `File` | verified live | This plugin's `GUID.cfg`. Refuses the shared file; `synced: true` throws. A `ConfigDescription` overload exists, but prefer `ctx.Bind`. | `SampleUi.cs` (3 local binds) |
| `PluginContext.Bind(..., synced, acceptableValues)` | verified live | Tracks `ConfigSync.Register`; BCL primitive / `string` / BCL enum only. Takes an `AcceptableValue*` or a `ConfigDescription`, synced entries included. | `Plugin.cs` (synced), `SampleUi.cs` (the bounds forms) |
| `ConfigHelper.Bind` / `Watch` / `Reloaded` / `BindEnum` / `ReadEnum` / `DropWatchedFile` | verified live | Disk watch (1 s debounce, main thread). Same BCL gate on the shared file. `Reloaded` is one static event for **every** watched file: filter on `ConfigFilePath`. It is raised only when at least one registered instance reloaded, and is handed the newest one that did; when every `Reload` threw, Core logs an error, raises nothing and retries once per file notification (offline-tested, not live-verified). `Bind` starts a watch whose lease it discards, so only Core's `UninstallAll` stops that watcher. The watch reloads the **live** `ConfigFile` instances for a path (BepInEx makes one per plugin instance; every registered instance is reloaded so an undropped dead one agrees with the disk instead of overwriting it, `DropWatchedFile` drops yours at unload, FCFG12), never a dead instance whose write-back would overwrite the operator's edit (FCFG11, evidence 222). | `SampleConfig.cs`: `.cfg status` (`Bind<bool>` + `BindEnum`/`ReadEnum` values), `.cfg watch on|off` (`Watch` lease + `Reloaded` subscribe/unsubscribe; edit the `.cfg` by hand and grep `Sample config: reload #`), `.cfg read` (manual `ConfigFile.Reload()`, which does *not* raise `Reloaded`); `SampleConfig.Detach` (`DropWatchedFile`) |
| `ConfigHelper.SharedFileName` | verified live | `"progenitor.cfg"`: a Core or plugin type bound here pins a collectible generation. | `SampleConfig.cs` (`.cfg status` prints it beside the plugin's own file) |
| `ConfigSync.Register` / `ThrowIfNotSyncable` / `EventId` | verified live | Snapshot after handshake; the client locks the entry until disconnect. | indirect, through `ctx.Bind` (`Plugin.cs`) |

The shared file also carries operator switches that are **not** plugin API and are owned by
Bootstrap: `[Reload] WatchCore`, `[Compat] RewriteMode`, and `[SaveSafety]` (`DropUnknownPrefabs`,
`FailLoadInstead`, `MaxDropEntities`, `MaxDropPercent`, `MinPrefabLookupCount`). A plugin should not
bind or depend on them.

## Persistence

Server JSON beside the world save (`…/Saves/v4/<save>/progenitor/<modGuid>.json`). Client `ctx.Save`
is a no-op. Unload does not delete the file. Data that must survive wipes and world restores goes in
`ServerSave` instead (`<persistentDataPath>\progenitor-server\<modGuid>.json`); see
[Where should my data go?](modules.md#where-should-my-data-go).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IModStore` `Get<T>(key, fallback)` / `Set<T>` / `Remove` / `Keys` | verified live | `System.Text.Json`; a pending store merges until the folder is known. | `Plugin.cs` — `.joins` (`Get` / `Set`); `SampleStore.cs` — `.store keys` (`Keys`), `.store reset` (`Remove`) |
| `IModStore.WhenReady(callback)` | tests only | Runs once the store is backed by the save folder, on the main thread, dropped on unload. **A read-modify-write belongs here**: on a pending store `Get` reads empty and the overlay writes that answer over the file (R58 finding 2). | `SampleStore.cs` — `ArmLoadCounter`, the load counter `.store` reports |
| `ModSave.Store(guid)` / `SaveAll` / `CurrentSaveDirectory` / `RemoveAll(owner)` | verified live | Flushed on `TriggerSave` and on disconnect. `RemoveAll` drops that plugin's `WhenReady` callbacks. | `SampleStore.cs`: `.store static` (`Store(guid)`, a `ReferenceEquals` against `ctx.Save`), `.store dir` (`CurrentSaveDirectory` + the `SavePaths.StoreFile` path), `.store flush` (`SaveAll`, then the file read back off disk) and `.store reset` (`SaveAll` after a `Remove`); `SampleStore.Detach` (`RemoveAll(owner)`, count logged) |

Written on the same triggers as `ModSave` (`ModSave.SaveAll` flushes both) and on `ServerSave.SaveAll`; only changed stores are written. Root: `-persistentDataPath`, else the game's value, else the root above the world save folder. `RemoveAll` is called by the reloader sweep. | `SampleStore.cs`: `.store server` (`CurrentDirectory`); unit test: `ServerSaveTests.cs` |
| `ServerSavePaths` (`FolderName`, `StoreFolder`, `StoreFile`, `RootFromSaveFolder`, `IsInside`) | game-free tested | The `progenitor-server` layout. | `ServerSaveTests.cs` |

## Networking

Unmanaged struct payloads. Max `CustomEvents.MaxPayloadBytes` (15 KiB), one custom event per packet,
a distinctive `eventId`.

**Event ids are process-wide and `0x5072____` ("Pr\*\*") is reserved for Progenitor.** Core's fourteen
protocol ids all live in that range, and `OnReceive` / `OnReceiveFromClient` **refuse** a plugin
registration inside it, and refuse an id a *different* assembly already owns rather than replacing
it. Neither throws: the handle removes nothing and one error line names both parties. Derive ids
from your own four-letter tag (`modules.md#networking`).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CustomEvents.SendToUser` / `PluginNet.SendToUser` + `OnReceive` (server → one client) | verified live | Two overloads each: an explicit `World`, or `ctx.World` (returns `false` when the world is not ready). Unload drops **that** handler, not the event id. | `SampleCommands.cs`, `SampleMods.cs`, `SampleUi.cs` (send); `SampleFx.cs`, `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleMods.cs` (receive); `SampleRifts.cs` (`SmRf` on the handshake-ok edge, and `.rifts send` to the caller alone) |
| `CustomEvents.SendToAll` / `PluginNet.SendToAll` (server → every connected client) | verified live | Returns the recipient count. Same two overloads. Core itself sends `PrfD` / `PrfU` / `PrfK` through it (`PrefabRegistry.cs:2181,2199,2221`), which is what a content hot reload with a player connected exercised. | `SampleNet.cs` (the server answers a ping by notifying every client); `SampleRifts.cs` (the rift schedule on change and on a 60 s heartbeat, with a 2 s floor) |
| `CustomEvents.SendToServer` / `PluginNet.SendToServer`, and `OnReceiveFromClient` (client → server) | verified live | The receive handler also gets `Entity userEntity` and `User user`, so the server can validate the sender. The handshake reply is this path, which is why the mismatch kick works. (The manifest package request `PrmR` that also used it was retired 2026-10-05.) | `SampleNet.cs` (F8 keybind -> `SendToServer`, server `OnReceiveFromClient` validates the sender) |
| `CustomEvents.TryRead<T>` / `MaxPayloadBytes` | verified live | 15 KiB, one custom event per packet, `T : unmanaged`; `TryRead` length-checks before it copies. **The length is `sizeof(T)`, which is the CLR size including trailing padding — not what the fields add up to** (`SampleRifts.RiftSchedule` is 52 bytes of fields and 56 on the wire). | `SampleFx.cs`, `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleMods.cs`; `SampleRifts.cs` (a pinned `PayloadBytes` and a wrong-length refusal line) |
| `CustomEvents.IsReservedEventId` / `ReservedEventIdPrefix` (`0x5072`) | landed | The reserved-range predicate the two receive registrations refuse on, plus the one-owner-per-id rule beside it (ownership is the handler's assembly simple name, so a hot reload is the same owner). | `SampleNet.cs`: `.net reserved <id>` runs the predicate on an id typed in chat, and Attach runs it over `SmNp` / `SmNr` every boot so "outside the range" is checked, not asserted |
| `Handshake.Require/Provide/DeclareSelf` + level/rule overloads | verified live | 16 slots; `DeclareSelf` is side-agnostic. | `Plugin.cs` (`DeclareSelf`), `SampleHandshake.cs` (`Require`): `.hs` prints every row this plugin declared and by which call; both explicit overloads (`Require`/`Provide` with level+rule) on a `NoNeedForSync` and a `ClientOnly` companion guid |
| `NetworkCompatibilityLevel` `ServerMustHave` / `NoNeedForSync` / `ClientOnly` / `None` | verified live | Reverse-evaluated when declared. `None` is presence-only. | `Plugin.cs` |
| `[Counterpart(CounterpartNeed)]` (`CounterpartAttribute`: `WithoutCounterpart`, `Features`, `ClientExtras`; `CounterpartNeed` `Required` / `Limited` / `Optional`) | landed (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | How much of the mod works without its counterpart on the other side. `Limited` / `Optional` turn a `ServerMustHave` row into `NoNeedForSync` (version rule kept), so the mod is never refused or paused for a server that lacks the counterpart; its page then says `Server part not on this server · limited` and grays its `needsServerPart` rows. Rules: [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#counterparts). | `samples/Progenitor.SampleCompanion/Plugin.cs`; the reference pair [`Progenitor.SampleServerLed`](../samples/Progenitor.SampleServerLed/README.md) (`Optional` + `ClientExtras`) and [`Progenitor.SampleClientLed`](../samples/Progenitor.SampleClientLed/README.md) (`Limited`) |
| `[ModAbout(markdown)]` (`ModAboutAttribute`: `Markdown`); embedded `README.md` / `<AssemblyName>.README.md` resource; `<dll>.README.md` beside the DLL | landed (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | The mod page's **About** text from the plugin itself, drawn by the README renderer with every `<` escaped. Order: the README of a listing whose package and current version this side's ledger row or a package `manifest.json` names, then the embedded resource (exactly `README.md`, else exactly `<AssemblyName>.README.md`), then `<dll>.README.md` / `<dll>.md` beside the DLL (in a folder of the mod's own also `README.md`, then the package folder's `README.md` and manifest `description`), then `[ModAbout]`, then the `ctx.Menu.Register` description or `[assembly: AssemblyDescription]`, then `No description available.` A package verified at another version keeps its author, store page and sides but not its README; a listing matched only by GUID or name is not shown. ## Chat

Server send that skips `ChatMessageSystem`. Recipients are `ConnectedUser` only (not offline).

The game's client chat box, which these lines reach, has these defaults: it fades 14 seconds after the last message, shows at most 50 lines across all channels, draws player text inside `<noparse>` (no formatting, no links), and lists server notices, admin announcements and mod replies as the same System type. Progenitor's client improvements to that box are described in [chat.md](chat.md); its link clicks and mod-owned links have the client API below ([chat links for mods](chat.md#chat-links-for-mods)).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Chat.Broadcast(text, type=System)` / `SendTo(user, text, type)` | verified live | Includes the speaker. Truncates at 509 UTF-8. World and speaker overloads at `Chat.cs:40,48,93,99`. | **none** — `SampleChat.cs` uses the `ctx` forms |
| `PluginChat.Broadcast` / `SendTo` / `Add(IDisposable)` | verified live | Bound to `ctx.World`; 0 / false on the client. `PluginContext.cs:1160,1171,1184`. | `SampleChat.cs` (`.shout` -> `Broadcast`, `.whisper` -> `SendTo`) |
| `ctx.Chat.OnLinkClicked(ChatLinkHandler handler)` → `IDisposable`; `ChatLinkHandler` (`bool (ChatLinkClick click)`) | source-checked, built and game-free tested (`ChatLinkTests`), **not live-verified** | Client. Runs when the player clicks a link in Progenitor's chat (web, mod package, location, item and mod links), before Progenitor's own action; returning true marks it handled and skips later handlers and the default dialog. Order: a mod link's owner first, then these handlers in registration order. A throwing handler counts as not handled and is logged once. Tracked: removed on unload. Never fires on a server. | `SampleChatLinks.cs` (logs every click, returns false) |
| `ctx.Chat.OnModLink(owner, ChatLinkHandler handler, acceptPlayerLinks = false)` → `IDisposable` | source-checked, built and game-free tested (`ChatLinkTests`), **not live-verified** | Client. Claims the mod links `[mod:owner:…]`: they are drawn as clickable text and their clicks reach this handler first. Links on a player's line stay as written unless `acceptPlayerLinks`, and then the handler must check `click.Source`. `Source = Server` means no player typed the line, not that your mod wrote it: the joined server and any of its mods can send any owner's links, so validate the payload and never open a URL, path or command from it directly. One plugin per owner (another plugin's claim is refused with a logged warning and gets a handle that does nothing; the same plugin claiming again replaces its handler). Tracked: removed on unload; Core's unload drops any left. | `SampleChatLinks.cs` (`progenitor.sample` links show a notice) |
| `ChatLinks.ModLink(owner, type, payload, text)` / `ctx.Chat.ModLink(…)` → `string`; `ChatLinks.Defuse(text)` | source-checked, built and game-free tested (`ChatLinkTests`), **not live-verified** | Either side. The token `[mod:owner:type:payload\|text]` with `\`, `\|` and `]` escaped; owner and type 1 to 48 and 1 to 32 of `a-z 0-9 _ . -` (lowercased), payload at most 160 characters without control characters or angle brackets (otherwise `ArgumentException`), text cleaned to at most 80 characters. Send it in a line of its own size: a reply longer than one chat line is split by bytes and can cut a token. `Defuse(text)`, for a server line that repeats what a player typed: every `[` becomes `(` and every `]` becomes `)`, and text starting with `mod:`, `item:` or `loc:` gets a leading space, so neither the text nor a `[` the template puts right before it can form a token. The shown text drops control and Unicode format characters (bidirectional overrides included). | `SampleChatLinks.cs` (`.chat link [text]`) |
| `ChatLinkClick` (`Kind`, `Target`, `Text`, `Owner`, `LinkType`, `Payload`, `ItemPrefab`, `Source`, `IsFromServer`, `Sender`, `FromSelf`, `Channel`, `Line`, `IsRestored`); `ChatLinkKind` (`Web`, `Package` = a Thunderstore `[mod:Namespace-Name]` link, `Location`, `Item`, `Mod` = a mod-owned `[mod:owner:type:…]` link); `ChatLinkSource` (`Player`, `Server`); `ChatChannel` | source-checked, built and game-free tested, **not live-verified** | What a handler gets. `Source` is `Server` only for a System or lore line whose sender network id is empty, read from the game's chat event, never from the text ([provenance](chat.md#who-wrote-a-line)). | `SampleChatLinks.cs` |
| `PluginChat.Broadcast(text, bool prefix, type = System)` / `SendTo(user, text, bool prefix, type = System)`; the message prefix | landed (WINDOWS1) — game-free tested, not live-verified | Both the old and the new forms send `<Mod Name>: text` by default (not twice); `prefix: false` sends the text as given. A text over one chat line (500 UTF-8 bytes with the name) goes as several lines, the name on the first, instead of being cut; `Broadcast` returns the first line's recipients, `SendTo` true when every line was sent. `Chat.Broadcast` / `Chat.SendTo` (no plugin) are unchanged. [Message prefix](modules.md#message-prefix). | unit test `ModMessagePrefixTests` |

## Localization

`ProgenitorPlugin` calls `LoadForPlugin` before `OnLoad` (`<dir>/<AssemblyName>/localization`, then
`<dir>/localization`). Stem `en.json` → English.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Register(key, text, language, owner)` → `IDisposable` | verified live | The client writes the game table; the server records. | `SampleLocalization.cs` — registered in `OnLoad` on both sides, reported by `.loc` |
| `LoadDirectory` / `LoadForPlugin` / `RemoveAll` | verified live | File load; English fallback. | `localization/en.json` (auto-loaded; its 4 keys drive the item and buff names) |
| `KeyFor` / `TryGetGameString` / `ApplyName` / `ApplyDescription` / `IsRegistered` | verified live | `<id>.desc` when `Described()` was omitted. | `SampleLocalization.cs`: `.loc` (`KeyFor`, `TryGetGameString`, `IsRegistered`; the client's read-back line is the only place the game table answers), `.loc key <id>` (the same three for any key, printing the derived `AssetGuid`), `.loc apply <id> <text>` (`Register` the pair, then `ApplyName` / `ApplyDescription` on the Ember Shard's cloned `ManagedItemData`; the server registers and says why the managed half is client-only) |

## Castles

Server snapshot plus handles. Reads live-verified 2026-09-04 on a claimed `CastleHeart` (territory
13). Server-only (the client returns disabled handles and one Debug line) and main-thread-only;
missing components never throw.

|---|---|---|---|

| `Castles.All` / `ForUser` / `AllForUser` / `ForTerritory` / `ForHeart` / `At` | verified live | Static forms take a `CastleId`. `ctx.Castles.All()` / `ForUser(Entity)` / `AllForUser(Entity)` / `ForTerritory(int)` / `At(CastleVec3)` / `At(Entity)` are the bound forms (`PluginContext.cs:1098-1136`), alongside the raw-row `Plots` / `Tiles` / `Buildings` / `PlotOf` on the row above; `ForHeart` is static-only. Never null. Clan-aware. `ForTerritory` is the reload-safe lookup. | `SampleCastle.cs`: `.castle all` (`All`, plus `AllForUser` and `At` on the trailing line), `.castle` / `.castle tiles` / `.castle canplace` (`ForUser`), `.castle territory <n>` (`ForTerritory`, and `ForHeart` shown beside it as the session-scoped lookup) |

The value `MaterialContext.StandingTerritory` carries outside a game call that names its heart, and `At(character)` resolves to the same castle. | unit tests `CastlesTests` (upper floor, stale `TilePosition`, off-plot, agreement). ## World

The world-load epoch. Stamp it next to any `Entity` kept past the current frame: a save reload
rebuilds the world from index 0 at version 1, so `EntityManager.Exists` on a retained `index:version`
can answer true while naming a different entity.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.WorldState.LoadEpoch` | landed | The epoch the world is in — an opaque, generation-stamped token. Never 0 or negative. Do not do arithmetic on it. | `SampleCastle.cs`, `SampleEpoch.cs` (`.epoch`) |
| `ctx.WorldState.IsStale(stampedEpoch)` / `.Loads` / `.Describe()` | landed | True when the world was rebuilt since, or when the stamp came from an earlier Core generation. `Describe()` = `world: epoch=2 loads=1`. | `SampleEpoch.cs` (`.epoch`) |
| `WorldLoaded` (`Epoch`, `PreviousEpoch`, `Source`) | landed | On the normal `EventBus`, once per increment. | `SampleEvents.cs` — `.events` |
| `WorldEpoch.Current` / `.Advance(source)` / `.IsStale` / `.FormatStale` | landed | The game-free counter behind all of the above. `Advance` is **Core-only**: it publishes `WorldLoaded`, makes every `ICastleHandle` stale and drops every god-mode guard from the old epoch, so its callers are `WorldLoadHook` (server), `ClientWorldEpoch` (client) and `ResetForTests`. A plugin never calls it. | `SampleEpoch.cs`: `.epoch`'s third line prints `Current` and `FormatStale(stamped, current)` off the statics beside the `ctx.WorldState` route; `Advance` has **no example on purpose**, and the reply says why |

Server seam: a Core postfix on `LoadPersistenceSystemV2.CopyDataFromPrefabs`. Client seam: the epoch
moves once when the client world is created and once when it ends (`ClientWorld.Created` /
`ClientWorld.Ended`).

## Time of day

Server only, except `GetTimeOfDay`, which on the client reads the client's own clock; every other
client call throws. Main thread. Hours are `double` in [0, 24): 0 is midnight, 12 is
noon, 23.5 is 23:30. See [modules.md](modules.md#time-of-day) for what is written and why.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Time.GetTimeOfDay()` → `TimeOfDayReading?` | landed, not live-verified | `Day`, `Hour`, `IsDay`, `DayLengthSeconds`, `SunriseHour`, `SunsetHour`, `WorldClockStopped` (the game's `DayNightCycleDisabled`), `Override` (`TimeOfDayOverrideKind`: `None`, `Shift`, `Hold`), `BloodMoon` (a blood moon night now), `NextBloodMoonDay` (the game's `DayNightCycle.NextBloodMoonDay`). `ToString()` = `day 3 23:30 night (held; sunrise 09:00, sunset 17:00, day 1080s)`, with `blood moon night` in place of `night` during one. Null (logged) when the world is not ready. **On the client** (landed, not live-verified): the client world's `DayNightCycle`, which follows the server's replicated time, so it is the hour the player's sun shows; `Override` is always `None` there and `WorldClockStopped` is the replicated flag (false when the client has none). One singleton read per call. | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time status`); client: `Progenitor.PanelsExample` (logged with each sort) |
| `ctx.Time.SetTimeOfDay(double hour)` → `TimeOfDayHandle` | landed, not live-verified | Move the clock to `hour` on the current game day and keep it running from there until disposed or unload; then the world's own time returns. The day number never changes, so an earlier hour moves the clock back within the day. Out-of-range hour throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. Refused (handle with `IsLive` false, `RefusedReason` set, logged) until the current world has finished loading, and when a singleton is missing or the first write throws. | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time set 23.5`) |
| `ctx.Time.HoldTimeOfDay(double hour)` → `TimeOfDayHandle` | landed, not live-verified | Freeze the clock at `hour` until disposed or unload. Written once; the game stops advancing it, so the sun does not step back. Same refusals as `SetTimeOfDay`. Never holds a blood moon night: when the hour on the current day is one, the same hour one day earlier is held (never later). Same as `HoldTimeOfDay(hour, false)`; kept as its own overload for binary compatibility. | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time hold 0`) |
| `ctx.Time.HoldTimeOfDay(double hour, bool bloodMoon = false)` → `TimeOfDayHandle` | landed, not live-verified | `bloodMoon` true holds the blood moon night at `hour`: day `NextBloodMoonDay` for an evening hour, the day after for a pre-dawn hour. Refused for a daytime hour or while `NextBloodMoonDay` is 0. The schedule itself is never written. | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time hold 1 bloodmoon`) |
| `ctx.Time.HasOverride` | landed, not live-verified | True while this plugin has a live `SetTimeOfDay` / `HoldTimeOfDay`. | `SampleTime.cs` |
| `ctx.Time.ResumeWorldClock()` → `bool` | landed, not live-verified | Clear `DayNightCycleDisabled` when no override from any plugin is live: recovery for a world saved during an override (the game saves that setting) or stopped by an admin. False (logged) otherwise. | `SampleTime.cs` (`.time resume`) |
| `TimeOfDayReading` / `TimeOfDayOverrideKind` | landed | The reading returned above and the override kind it reports. | `SampleTime.cs` |
| `TimeOfDayHandle` (`IsLive`, `RefusedReason`, `Kind`, `Hour`, `Dispose`) | landed, not live-verified | One override. Check `IsLive` after taking it; dispose to release. Tracked by the plugin context and dropped from the tracked list once released by any path. | `SampleTime.cs` (takes the new handle before disposing the old) |

Overrides from every plugin stack: the newest governs, releasing it hands the clock to the previous
one, releasing the last restores `DayNightCycleDisabled` to its value before the first. Released on
dispose, plugin unload, a new world epoch and Core uninstall. Overrides do not survive a world load:
take them from a `WorldLoaded` handler or later. The prior flag value is kept in a plain per-save
marker (`TimeOfDayPolicy`) so a world saved during an override is restored after a restart.

## World speed

Server only; client calls throw. Main thread. Writes the game's networked `ProjectM.TimeScale.Value`,
which the game copies into `UnityEngine.Time.timeScale` on the server and every connected client, so
the whole simulation stops or slows for every player. See [modules.md](modules.md#world-speed) for
what stops, what keeps running, and the camera caveats. Live-checked 2026-10-02 on the isolated
server: the `TimeScale` singleton exists, and writing `Value` = 0.5 through the bridge's generic
`set` replicated to the Steam client (its singleton read 0.5), then 1 was restored. The `ctx.Time`
members and the bridge `time` op themselves have not run in the game yet.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Time.Freeze()` → `TimeScaleHandle` | landed, not live-verified | World speed 0 until disposed or unload; then the prior speed (normally 1) returns. Refused (handle with `IsLive` false, `RefusedReason` set, logged) until the world has finished loading, when the `TimeScale` singleton is missing, or when the first write throws. | `SampleSpeed.cs` (`.speed freeze`) |
| `ctx.Time.SlowMotion(float scale)` → `TimeScaleHandle` | landed, not live-verified | World speed `scale` in [0.001, 1]; anything else throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. Clients receive 3 decimals. Same refusals as `Freeze`. | `SampleSpeed.cs` (`.speed slow 0.25`) |
| `ctx.Time.GetTimeScale()` → `TimeScaleReading?` | landed, not live-verified | `Scale`, `Paused` (the game's `IsPaused`, never written), `EngineScale` (`UnityEngine.Time.timeScale` here; lags a write by up to a frame), `Override`. `ToString()` = `0.25x (SlowMotion; engine 0.25, not paused)`. | `SampleSpeed.cs` (`.speed status`) |
| `ctx.Time.HasTimeScaleOverride` | landed, not live-verified | True while this plugin has a live `Freeze` / `SlowMotion`. | — |
| `TimeScaleHandle` (`IsLive`, `RefusedReason`, `Kind`, `Scale`, `Dispose`) | landed, not live-verified | One lease. Tracked by the plugin context and untracked once released by any path. | `SampleSpeed.cs` |
| `TimeScaleReading` / `TimeScaleOverrideKind` (`None`, `Freeze`, `SlowMotion`) | landed | The reading above and the lease kind it reports. | `SampleSpeed.cs` |
| `TimeScalePolicy` | landed | Game-free rules: `MinSlowMotion`/`MaxSlowMotion`, `RequireSlowMotion`, `OnTheWire` (the replicated 3-decimal value), `NeedsWrite`, the per-save marker (`MarkerStore` `progenitor.core.timescale`, `MarkerKey`, `Encode`/`Decode`/`Decide`/`PriorForFirstTake`), `Format`. Public so the test project compiles it. | unit test `TimeScalePolicyTests` |

Leases from every plugin stack like time-of-day overrides: **the newest live lease governs** (last
in wins), releasing it hands the world to the lease before it, the last release writes back the value
from before the first, and the value is re-asserted every frame (overriding an admin's debug-menu
speed). Released on dispose, plugin unload (every reason), a new world epoch and Core uninstall. A
per-save marker restores the prior speed after a restart if a world was saved mid-freeze.

The Dev bridge `time` op (`status`, `speed {value}`, `freeze`, `resume`, `release`; MCP
`progenitor_time`) holds one lease of its own through `TimeScaleBridge`, owned by
`progenitor.dev bridge`; `resume` / `release` drop it. See [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#time).

The caller disposes `Build`. | `SampleBeam.cs` (`Entities`); `SampleEcs.cs` (`.ecs users`: `Build` twice, both flag values, each disposed in a `finally`) |

| `RawWriteGuard.EnsureTypedWrite` / `EnsureOverlayMemcpy` | landed | A `TypeSize`-0 raw byte write is refused. | unit test |
| `BuffSweep.Sweep` / `DestroyBuffs` / `PruneDanglingRows` / `DanglingRowsDescending` / `FormatPruneLog` / `Peek` / `ScanBuffer` / `AllAbsent` / `ClassifyBufferRows` / `ContainsId` / `BoundLength` / `LengthAboveCap` / `CopyEntities` / `SelectExisting` / `FromPass` / `DecideFinalizeStep` / `ShouldDestroyBuff` / `ShouldAddDestroyTag` / `FormatLog` / `FormatIncompleteUnitWarning` / `FormatPlayerRefuseLog` / `FormatDeferTimeoutWarning` / `FormatStuckReason` / `RefuseAsPlayerOrUser` / `DestroyTagDeferFrames` / `MaxCycles` | landed | DIAG5 two-phase helper: `Sweep` copies `BuffBuffer` and calls `DestroyUtility.Destroy(TryRemoveBuff)` alone on each still-existing row (skip already-tagged); does not strip `Attach` and does not destroy the unit. `DestroyBuffs` is the same destroy on a listed id set. Neither adds `DestroyTag` to a buff: the game's `TagNewDestroyedEntity` skips an already-tagged entity, and a directly tagged buff left its `BuffBuffer` row behind (live 2026-09-07); units that then outlived their despawn were saved with rows naming no entity (film save, 2026-09-27). `PruneDanglingRows` removes every row naming no entity once the listed buffs are gone (typed `GetBuffer<BuffBuffer>` + `RemoveAt`, highest index first, after re-reading the length); the Units despawn poll calls it before it destroys the unit or gives up. Verified live on the test server (server side, 2026-09-28): with this change a despawned unit's rows are removed by the game even when a Core swap drops the despawn poll (4 stranded units, 0 rows; the previous Core left 5 dangling rows on each and 20 `destroyed without being detached` errors). The prune itself was not exercised live, because the game had already cleaned every row. A reported length above 256 is refused before any raw read. `DecideFinalizeStep` maps `{existingLeft, unitExists, newLiveLeft, cycle}` to `waiting-buffs` / `buffs` / `tagged` / `stuck`. `AllAbsent` is listed ids gone, not an empty `BuffBuffer`. Stale rows (entity `Exists=false`) are ignored for `tagged`; a live row not in the listed set is `buffs` + `destroy-buffs` for another cycle, capped at `MaxCycles` (4), then `stuck` without tagging. The unit is never `DestroyUtility.Destroy`'d before `tagged`. Units despawn refuses `PlayerCharacter`/`User` and bounds waiting-buffs at 120 frames. The Unity Error `destroyed without being detached` is DetachSystem logging a live detach and is not a failure (internal diagnoses DIAG5 and DIAG2). | unit test: `BuffSweepTests.cs`; Core: `UnityUnitRuntime` despawn; Dev: `entity` destroy / finalize |
| `BuffSweep.DestroyUnitReplicated(em, unit, reason, label)` / `UnitDestroyFallbackFrames` / `ShouldTagUnitFallback` / `FormatUnitFallbackWarning` | landed | The unit's final removal step: `DestroyUtility.Destroy` alone, polled once a frame; `DestroyTag` only if the unit still exists after 120 frames, with a warning. Live 2026-09-24: an immediate `DestroyTag` removed entities on the server only (a loot drop and 397 of 400 Frostmaws stayed on the client); `Destroy` alone removed them on both sides within 2 s. The fallback itself is not live-verified. | unit test: `BuffSweepTests.cs`; Core: `UnityUnitRuntime` despawn; Dev: `entity` finalize / force |
| `BuffSweep.EntityId` (`Index` / `Version` / `IsNull`) | landed | One `BuffBuffer` row's Entity, copied out before any destroy runs. | unit test: `BuffSweepTests.cs` |
| `BuffSweep.Result` (`Found` / `Destroyed` / `Skipped` / `Stale` / `Complete` / `Reason` / `DestroyedIds` / `StaleIds` / `BuffIds`) | landed | How many buffer rows were walked, issued a destroy, skipped, or were already gone (`Stale` / `StaleIds`), the still-existing buff ids (`BuffIds` aliases `DestroyedIds`), and whether the pass is complete enough for a later-frame unit `DestroyTag`. Incomplete reasons include `length <n> above cap`, unread buffer, short copy, row skip other than already-absent, and a swallowed exception. | unit test: `BuffSweepTests.cs` |
| `BuffSweep.FinalizeStep` (`Phase` / `Action` / `Reason`) | landed | DIAG5 helper result: next finalize phase (`waiting-buffs` / `buffs` / `tagged` / `stuck`) and the action to take (`none` / `destroy-buffs` / `destroy-unit`). `Reason` is set on `stuck` (`cycle cap (4) exceeded after N destroy cycle(s)`). | unit test: `BuffSweepTests.cs` |
| `MainThread.Post` / `PostDelayed` / `Run` / `IsMainThread` | verified live | For any worker-thread plugin work; pass `ctx.Owner`. | `SampleFx.cs`, `SampleUi.cs` |
| `PlayerTeleport.Teleport(em, character, float3 destination, out why)` → method or null; `GameMethod` / `WriteMethod` | MAPTP1 (2026-10-02), source-checked and built, **not live-verified** through this helper (the game call is the one the Dev `teleport` op runs live) | Server, main thread. Moves a character with the game's `TeleportUtilityServer.Teleport`; if that throws, writes `Translation` and `LastTranslation` through `UnitSpawnPlacement`'s guarded writes. Never touches `SpawnTransform`. Returns which ran. No read-back: the Dev op keeps its one-second read-back and richer fallback. | Core's debug tools (`src/Progenitor.Core/Debugging/DebugMap.cs`, `.debug tp`); no plugin example since the map powers moved into Core |
| `GroundProbe.TryOpen(world, out why)` → `GroundProbe`; `TryGetHeight(x, probeY, z, out height)`; `TryIsWalkable(world, x, y, z, out walkable)`; `MinHeight`, `AgentRadius`, `TerrainSource` | MAPTP1 (2026-10-02). Height: the call the Dev `teleport` op's floor snap has used live, now shared (Dev uses this). **Walkable: not live-verified** | The game's tile world opened once for many probes (`Progenitor.Terrain`). `TryGetHeight` is `TileWorld.TryGetGameplayHeight` (bilinear): the highest walkable floor at or below the probe height, false for no tile data, NaN or below -40 m. `TryIsWalkable` asks the game's `PathfindingUtility.TryFindValidTile` with a ground humanoid's terrain preferences whether the tile is standable (catches walls, rocks and deep water); a by-ref interop call no feature has made live, so keep it behind a default-off setting. | Core's debug tools (`DebugMap.cs`); Dev `teleport` |

Avoid `GetSingleton<T>`, `NativeList<T>.Add` and `HasComponent<T>` on game structs unless the
instantiation is known. `User` is not C# `unmanaged` — use `GetComponentData<User>`. `TypeSize` often
reads 0, so the toolkit uses `sizeof(T)`.

**Stale combat music (server, automatic; not an API).** The game sets a player's `CombatMusicListener_Shared.UnitPrefabGuid` when a unit with music (a V Blood) starts targeting them, only while it is 0, and clears it when a unit of the same prefab stops targeting them. A boss that leaves without that "stopped targeting" event (seen after teleporting near a boss and away) leaves the music playing out of combat for good, and no later boss fight can replace it. Core's server checks connected players every 2 s (`CombatMusicRepairServer`, rule `CombatMusicRepair`): music whose prefab no unit in the player's `InverseAggroBufferElement` rows carries is set to what the game's own "stopped" step would leave (the first targeting unit with active music, else 0, `PlayOutro` false), after two checks in a row agree. A fight in progress is never touched. Status: source-checked against the game's own music code, built and unit-tested (`CombatMusicRepairTests`); the stale value was cleared by hand with the bridge on 2026-10-07, the automatic check is not yet observed live.

## Items

Server inventory and equip. Client calls throw. `ctx.Items.Grant` adds through
`ServerGameManager.TryAddInventoryItem`. `ctx.Items.Equip` sends `EquipItemEvent` for a bag slot.
The debug bridge `give` and `equip` ops call the same methods.

A player character has no `InventoryBuffer`. The bag is the entity
`InventoryInstanceElement.ExternalInventoryEntity` points at (one element, the player's 35-slot
bag). Grant and Equip read that entity. `TryAddInventoryItem` still takes the character.

`whenFull` is `allowPartial` (default: a short stack succeeds, a completely full bag fails and
adds nothing) or `requireComplete` (any shortfall fails). A partial add is not taken back out:
`Added` is how many stayed. `whenPresent` is `stack` (default: add again; the game merges
stackable items and equipment gets a second copy), `refuse` (do nothing if a copy is in the bag
or worn), or `equipExisting` (wear the bag copy, or stop if it is already worn, and do not add
another). `equip: true` wears the new stack. `durability: none` sets `LossType` to `None`, the
take-damage factor to 0, and `DestroyItemWhenBroken` false, including on a piece that was already
worn. Amount must be 1..10000 on `Grant`; the bridge `give` op still clamps a bad amount to that
range before the call.

| `ctx.Items.Equip(Entity, int slot)` | verified live | Wear the stack already in that bag slot. An empty or missing slot is `Ok` false. | `SampleItems.cs` (`.items equip`); live `equip` of slots 3-9 (bridge op, not this sample) |

| `WhenAlreadyPresent` | verified live | `Stack`, `Refuse`, `EquipExisting`. | unit test `ItemGrantPolicyTests` |
| `ItemDurability` | verified live | `Leave`, `Indestructible`. | live `Durability.LossType` None on the tier-9 set |

Both sides. Built on the first call of a world load, rebuilt on a new world-load epoch, when a custom item is applied or removed, or when Core writes an item's `ItemData` (plugin and server edits, keep rules); other calls return the same list. Empty, and not cached, before the world's prefabs exist; on the client a build made before the local character exists is not cached either (uncached builds repeat at most once per frame). Describes the last build; `Built` false with zero counts once the world is gone. Does not build. | `SampleItemCatalog.cs` (summary line) |

### Item catalog

`ctx.Items.Catalog()` lists item prefabs: prefab entities carrying `ItemData` whose GUID is in
`GameDataSystem.ItemHashLookupMap`, the table the game's inventory code reads stack sizes from
(a custom item applied in this process is listed even if the table misses it). Stack size,
category and type come from the prefab's own `ItemData`. Live item copies in inventories are not prefabs and are not listed. Custom items registered with
`DefineItem` or a data pack appear once their clone is applied on that side. Narrative and the
live acceptance procedure: [modules.md](modules.md#item-catalog).

| Field | Server | Client |
|---|---|---|
| `PrefabGuid`, `PrefabName`, `MaxAmount`, `ItemCategory`, `ItemType` | yes | yes |
| `DisplayName`, vanilla item | null: the dedicated server ships no localization tables | the game's localized name in the current language (`ManagedItemData.Name`), the resolver chat item links use |
| `DisplayName`, custom item | its `Named(...)` text | the game string it registered, else its `Named(...)` text |
