**Demand qualification.** `Demand` is a server, main-thread read using the existing queue layout; it does not enqueue, cancel, or pay for crafts. `IsKnown` currently means zero reserved inputs: an empty workstation queue, or refinement in `Idle` with no current recipe. A nonempty queue, active or ambiguous refinement, missing station/queue, or failed read sets `ProtectAllInputs` and leaves `IsKnown` false. `ReservedInputs` is currently empty even for unknown demand: never interpret that list without checking `IsKnown`. Exact paid-item reservations and effective recipe costs are unavailable; `StationRecipe.Inputs` remains base costs, without floor bonuses or server cost adjustments. Consumers must retain all inputs while demand is unknown, and may reclaim only after known empty/inactive state. Workstation crafting demand is paid through the material-source path; this API does not justify prefill.

## Inventory

Server inventories of any kind: chests, station input and output, castle hearts (fuel),
servants and player bags. Every write goes through the game's own server inventory code and is
read back; every count in a result is a read-back. Server only, main thread; client calls throw
`InvalidOperationException`. Narrative, rules and the live acceptance:
[modules.md, Inventory](modules.md#inventory). Built and game-free tested; **not live-verified**.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Inventory.Of(Entity owner, InventoryPart part = Main)` / `InputOf(Entity)` / `OutputOf(Entity)` → `InventoryRef` | landed | Session handle for one inventory (owner + instance + world epoch). `InventoryPart`: `Main` / `Input` (instance 0), `Output` (instance 1, refinement stations). A reference from before a save reload is refused as stale. | `SampleInventory.cs` (container tokens `#n`, `bag`, `heart`, `/out`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.Instances(Entity)` | landed | How many inventories the owner has (1 chest or heart, 2 refinement station, 0 none). | none (diagnostic) |
| `ctx.Inventory.TryIsServantContainer(Entity owner, out bool isServantContainer, out string reason)` | experimental (source-checked, compile-verified; not live-verified) | Native `ServantCoffinstation` or `ServantEquipment` component presence identifies special servant owners independently of names. Return false means unknown, not ordinary storage. Does not follow the coffin's connected servant or filter gear out of inventory reads. | consumer default-eligibility policy |
| `ctx.Inventory.DescribeAcceptance(InventoryRef)` → `InventoryAcceptance` | experimental (source-checked, built, game-free tested; not live-verified) | Read-only native `RestrictedInventory` rule, independent of free space or furniture names. `Ok`, `Reason`, `Restriction.Item` (prefab GUID), `Restriction.Category` (native category mask), `IsOpen`, and `Allows(int item, long itemCategory)`. `Allows` requires actual `ItemData` category flags, not application category names; it does not check capacity or item-table membership. Failed/stale reads have `Ok=false`, `IsOpen=false`, and reject `Allows`. | unit tests `InventoryAcceptanceTests` |
| `ctx.Inventory.Read(InventoryRef)` → `InventorySnapshot` / `ReadInto(InventoryRef, InventorySlot[])` | landed | Slots in buffer order: `InventorySlot` (`Slot`, `Item`, `Amount`, `MaxStack`, `StackOverride`, `HasItemEntity`), plus the container's `InventoryRestriction` (`Item`, `Category`). `ReadInto` fills a caller array and allocates nothing. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items slots`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.Count` / `CountMany` / `FreeSpace` | landed | Totals; free space honors stack size, slot overrides and `RestrictedInventory` (exact for chests, stations and hearts, an upper bound for a player bag). | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items count`, `.items free`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.TryAdd` / `TryRemove` → `InventoryResult` | landed | Partial allowed; the result says how much and why not. Removal never asks for more than the inventory holds, so worn equipment is never touched. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items add`, `.items remove`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.Transfer(from, to, item, amount)` → `InventoryResult` | landed | Conserves the total of interchangeable items; for any other item a step that does not conserve the total is detected and the transfer stops (`Inconsistent`) without creating or deleting a copy (by design; not yet live-verified): the game's own slot move per source slot, read back after each step. Units the game equips onto the target's owner on arrival count as moved (`Equipped`). An interchangeable item (stackable, no item entity) is restored exactly after a step that does not conserve it; for anything else the transfer stops with `Inconsistent` and an error line rather than recreate or delete a copy. Two references to the same inventory (a character and its bag entity) are refused. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items move`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.TransferAll(from, to, Func<int,bool>? filter)` → `InventoryTransferAllResult` | landed | One `Transfer` per item type the filter accepts. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items moveall`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.TransferSlot(from, int slotIndex, to, int amount)` → `InventoryResult` | experimental (game-free tested, not live-verified) | Up to `amount` of whatever one source slot holds, through `Transfer`'s own path restricted to that slot: the same conservation check, restore step, `Inconsistent` stop and `Equipped` accounting. Refused: amount below 1, slot out of range, the same inventory on both sides; an empty slot is `Nothing` with `Item` 0. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items moveslot`); unit tests `InventorySlotTransferTests` |
| `InventorySlotFilter` (`All`, `SkipActionBar`, `SkipSlots(first, count)`, `ActionBarSlots` = 8, `Allows(slot)`, `IsAll`, `First`, `Count`) with `ctx.Inventory.Transfer(from, to, item, amount, InventorySlotFilter)` / `TransferAll(from, to, InventorySlotFilter, Func<int,bool>? filter = null)` | experimental (game-free tested, not live-verified) | Which **source** slots an item-based move may draw from; the target is never filtered. A player's action bar is bag slots 0–7 (`SkipActionBar`), so a quick stash is `TransferAll(bag, chest, InventorySlotFilter.SkipActionBar)`; plain `Transfer` still takes from the bar. Result counts stay whole-container; `TransferAll.Left` is what the allowed slots held minus what moved; the restore step can put units back into any slot, the bar included. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items moveall <from> <to> keepbar`); unit tests `InventorySlotTransferTests` |

| `ctx.Inventory.Watch(InventoryRef, Action<InventoryChange>, TimeSpan? interval)` → `IInventoryWatch` | landed | Fingerprint poll (default 500 ms, minimum 100 ms, at most 1024 watches per server) plus an immediate check after a `ctx.Inventory` write. `InventoryChange` (`Container`, `Kind`: `InventoryChangeKind.Changed` / `Gone`, fingerprints). Tracked; `Dispose` / `Poke`. With `[Inventory] WatchUsesChangeStamps = true` (server, default `false`, experimental) a due check whose `ChangeStamp` is unchanged skips the fingerprint, except every 8th. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items watch`) |
| `ctx.Inventory.ChangeStamp(InventoryRef)` → `long` / `ChangeStamps(IReadOnlyList<InventoryRef>, Span<long>)` | experimental (source-checked, built, game-free tested; not live-verified) | A stamp of the slot contents, compared for **equality only**: equal means unchanged, unequal means possibly changed (false positives only: per-chunk granularity, read-write accesses, chunk moves, first sight, any failed read). Slots only, not item-entity state or the restriction. Never 0. `ChangeStamps` is one batch and throws `ArgumentException` on a short span. Server, main thread, never from inside a native inventory hook; client calls throw. [modules.md](modules.md#change-stamps). | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items stamp`); unit tests `InventoryStampTests` |
| `ctx.Inventory.Clock` → `long` / `AnyChangedSince(IReadOnlyList<InventoryRef>, long since)` | experimental (source-checked, built, game-free tested; not live-verified) | `Clock`: a 64-bit change clock that only grows, also across Core reloads and world loads. `AnyChangedSince`: true when any container may have changed at or after `since`, under the same false-positives-only rule; refreshes every container in the list. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items stamp`); unit tests `InventoryStampTests` |
| `ContainerId` / `ctx.Inventory.IdOf(Entity)` / `Status(ContainerId)` → `ContainerIdStatus` / `TryFind(ContainerId, out Entity, out string)` | landed | Stable id of a placed container, station or heart: survives restarts, never reused (`Unknown`, `Live`, `Unbound`, `Gone`). Text form `c-` + 32 hex. | `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items id`, `c-…` tokens) |
| `ctx.Inventory.TryResolveNetworkId(int index, int generation, int type, out ContainerId, out Entity owner, out string reason)` / `TryGetNetworkId(ContainerId, out int index, out int generation, out int type[, out string reason])` | experimental (game-free tested, not live-verified) | A client's `NetworkId` words (`VanillaPanelTarget`, `UiWorldPickResult`) to the server entity and stable id of the placed container, station or heart, and back. Untrusted input: malformed, unknown, stale and non-container ids give false with a reason, never a throw. [ui.md](ui.md#resolving-a-target-on-the-server). | `samples/Progenitor.PanelsExample` (`Locate` button); `SampleInventory.cs` (`.items resolve`, `.items net`); unit tests `NetworkIdResolverTests` |

**Container preview, client side.** `ctx.Inventory.Preview` is the one client facade of `ctx.Inventory`:
the game's own container window for a chest or brazier somewhere else, read-only, for the castle's owner
and clan. Core owns the server half (the authorization and the shared streaming lease); no mod can widen it.
Narrative, the seams and the live plan: [modules.md, Container preview](modules.md#container-preview).
Source-checked, built and game-free tested; **not live-verified** (experiments E1 to E5 are open).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Inventory.Preview` (`PluginInventoryPreview`) `.Open(ContainerId, ContainerPreviewOptions? options = null)` → `IContainerPreview` / `.Current` | experimental (game-free tested, not live-verified) | Client, main thread. Asks the server, waits for the container and its inventory to reach the client, then opens the game's container window for it with no interaction behind it. The server allows it only when its Remote chest preview setting does (`[ContainerPreview] RemotePreview`: `off`, `pve` (the default: PvE servers only) or `on`, admin-edited in the Mods menu under Progenitor, On this server) and only for an owner or clan member (`ctx.Castles.RelationTo` `Owner` / `ClanMember`; `Allied` and strangers are refused) of a plain container's castle: an inventory and no menu of its own (not a heart, a crafting, refining, research or salvage station, a forge, fusion forge, blood mixer, jewel crafting station, stable, arena or route station, a vermin nest or tomb, a prison, a servant coffin or a servant; clan membership is the whole access rule, the game has no per-chest permission inside a clan). Read-only is the game's own server rule: every item event the window sends needs an interaction with the container, and the preview grants none. For the lease the server also puts the user in the chest's inventory sync lists (`SyncToUserBuffer` of the inventory and its slot items; one copy added, exactly that copy removed at the end; taken out around world saves), because the chest's inventory reaches only the users listed there (E1, live 2026-10-17); sync is not permission. Not live-verified (E1b). One preview at a time per client; a new `Open` closes the running one (`Replaced`). Throws on the server, off the main thread, with no client world or with bad options. | Dev bridge `container_preview`; unit tests `ContainerPreviewTests` |
| `ContainerPreviewOptions` (record) | experimental | `HidePlayerInventory` (default true: the bag panel next to the container is hidden so a mod window can dock there), `KeepOpen` (until closed; the client renews the server lease every 60 s for 180 s), `Seconds` (default 30, more than 0, at most `MaxSeconds` 600; ignored with `KeepOpen`), `ReplicationTimeoutSeconds` (default 5, at most 30), `DropItemEvents` (default true: this client's item events naming the previewed container are dropped before they are sent so a drag does not visibly fail; a courtesy, not the guarantee). | unit tests `ContainerPreviewTests` |
| `IContainerPreview` (`Container`, `State`, `CloseReason`, `Reason`, `Closed` event, `Dispose`) / `ContainerPreviewState` / `ContainerPreviewClosed` (`Container`, `Reason`, `Message`) | experimental | `State`: `Requesting` (asked the server), `Waiting` (agreed; replication or the window pending), `Open`, `Closed`. `Reason` is the refusal or close sentence, player-facing, null until closed. `Closed` is raised once on the main thread for every reason but `Unloaded`. | unit tests `ContainerPreviewTests` |
| `ContainerPreviewCloseReason` | experimental | `None`, `Disposed`, `Escape` (topmost: after framework dialogs, key captures and the focused chat box), `WindowClosed` (the game closed the window or opened another menu), `Refused`, `NoAnswer` (8 s), `ReplicationTimeout`, `OpenFailed` (the window did not appear in 2 s, or another game menu stayed open), `Timeout`, `LeaseEnded` (the server let go: moved more than 10 m, access changed, the setting was turned off, the container is gone, another view took the streaming origin), `TargetGone`, `WorldChanged`, `Replaced`, `Unloaded`, `Failed`. | unit tests `ContainerPreviewTests` |

**The vanilla sort, client side.** `ctx.Inventory` itself is server only apart from `Preview`; this event
is the other client half of inventories. Subscribe from a client plugin with `ctx.Events.On<InventorySortUsed>`.
Narrative and the live acceptance: [modules.md](modules.md#vanilla-sort-event-client).

The same request seen twice, or a repeat within 50 ms, is raised once. No input is read and nothing is patched. Costs nothing while unsubscribed. | `Progenitor.PanelsExample` (logs each sort and a double sort); unit test `SortBrazierPositionTests` |

## Servants

Castle servants and their hunts on the server. **Experimental**: source-checked against the
game's metadata and code and game-free tested (`ServantLogicTests`); nothing has run in the game.
Server only, main thread; client calls throw `InvalidOperationException`. Ids are session ids
(`CastleId`) stamped with the world-load epoch; persist the heart's `ContainerId` or the
territory index, never an entity. Narrative, game facts and the live acceptance:
[modules.md, Servants](modules.md#servants).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Servants` (`PluginServants`) `List(Entity throneOrHeart)` → `ServantInfo` | experimental | The servants the castle's throne lists, in the game's order and with the state it shows: `Servant`, `Coffin`, `Heart`, `Name`, `State` (`ServantState`: `Dead`, `Injured`, `AwayOnHunt`, `HasItems`, `Free`), `InjuryEndsUtc`, `Injury`, `HuntProficiency`, `LootFactor`, `GearLevel`, `Expertise`, `Perk0`/`Perk1`, `Inventory` (`InventoryRef`), `ItemStacks`, `Epoch`. | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants list`) |
| `ctx.Servants.Missions(Entity throneOrHeart)` → `ServantMission` | experimental | The heart's hunt record: `MissionGuid`, `MissionName`, `Zone` (`MissionZone`), `DataSlot` (the hunt length choice), `StartedUtc`, `LengthSeconds`, `EndsUtc`, `Servants`, `Chances` (`ServantChance` per servant, live), `MaxSuccess` (the hunt's cap, null when vanilla), `Row`, `Epoch`. | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants hunts`); bridge `servants list` (`hunts[].chances`) |
| `ctx.Servants.Chance(ServantChanceRequest)` → `ServantChanceResult` | experimental (source-checked, not live-verified) | A prospective send's success chances, per servant, as the game rolls them at the return and the throne's hunt menu shows them. Request: `Heart`, `Servants` (1–3), `MissionGuid`, `DataSlot`, `MaxSuccess` (preview only), `User`, `CheckDiscovered`, `Epoch`. Result: `Ok` (chances read), `Refusal`/`Reason`/`WouldSend` (what `Send` would say now, no writes), `Heart`, `MissionGuid`, `DataSlot`, `Chances`. | bridge `servants chance`; unit test `ServantChanceTests` |
| `ServantChance` | experimental (source-checked, not live-verified) | One servant on one hunt: `Servant`, `Vanilla` (0..1, the throne's %), `MaxSuccess` (the hunt's cap; null: no cap applied), `Applied` (`min(Vanilla, MaxSuccess)`, else `Vanilla`), `Proficiency`, `PartyBonus` (the throne's "+n"), `MissionDifficulty`, `PerkReduction`, `EffectiveDifficulty`, `PerkLootBonus`, `SuccessRateBonus`. Formula: `x = Proficiency + PartyBonus - EffectiveDifficulty`; `x >= 0` is 1, else `1 - x²/15000 + SuccessRateBonus` clamped. Also on `ServantSendResult.Chances` and `ReturnedServant.Chance`. | unit test `ServantChanceTests` (the game's formula) |
| `ctx.Servants.MissionDisplayName(int missionGuid)` → `string?` | experimental (source-checked, not live-verified) | **Client only**: the mission's localized name (`ManagedMissionData.Name` through the game's string table, as the game's hunt notices print it, for example "Fishing Lake"); null on the server, for a non-mission id, or before game data loads. | none |
| `ctx.Servants.MissionToken(int missionGuid)` → `string` | experimental (source-checked, not live-verified) | Either side: the chat token `[mission:<guid>]`. Progenitor clients draw it as the mission's name (like `[item:id]`, [chat.md](chat.md)); vanilla clients show it as written, so send it only to Progenitor players. | unit test `ServantChanceTests` (token parsing and rendering) |
| `ctx.Servants.ZonesOf(int missionGuid)` / `MissionOf(MissionZone)` | experimental | Map zone(s) of a servant mission and back. `MissionZone` is the game's `MapZoneId` (`ChunkX`, `ChunkY`, `ZoneId` = zone index + 1). | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants send … x,y#zone …`) |
| `ctx.Servants.Send(ServantSendRequest)` / `Send(Entity heart, IReadOnlyList<Entity> servants, int missionGuid, int dataSlot, Entity user = default, bool checkDiscovered = true)` → `ServantSendResult` | experimental | A server send with the writes the game's throne send makes (one `ActiveServantMission` row, the mission buff on each servant), validated with the game's checks and refused with `ServantSendRefusal` + `Reason`. Never synthesizes the client's throne event. Raises the game's "Hunt in X started." notice (`ServantMissionStartedEvent`) to the connected users allied with the castle, as the throne's send does (`Announce`, default true; source-checked, not live-verified). | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants send`); unit test `ServantLogicTests` (the checks and the row) |
| `ServantSendRequest.MaxSuccess` / `ctx.Servants.Resend(ServantsReturned, float? maxSuccess)` (the success cap) | experimental (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | A cap 0..1 on each servant's success chance for the hunt. At the return, when a servant's vanilla chance `c` is above the cap, Progenitor forces the game's own roll to fail with probability `1 - cap/c` (that servant's `ServantPower.GearLevel` reads -10000 for that one update only), so the servant succeeds with probability exactly `cap`. A forced failure is the game's failure: the servant dies (revivable at its coffin), brings no loot, allies get the "was killed" line. **A capped hunt can kill a servant the game would have brought home; that is the intended trade-off.** A cap at or above `c` changes nothing. Kept in Progenitor's server store (`progenitor-server/progenitor.servants.json`, keyed by the world's id, mission and start), so it holds across a Core swap and a restart. Only the hunt the game finishes in an update (the first due row per castle) is ever written, never while the game's save is in flight (that hunt waits an update instead). A capped send before the stores are loaded is refused (`WriteFailed`). Read back as `ServantChance.MaxSuccess`/`Applied` and `MaxSuccess` on `ServantMission`/`ServantSendResult`; `ReturnedServant.CapForcedFailure`. Out of range throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. | bridge `servants send` with `maxSuccess`; unit test `ServantCapTests` |
| `ctx.Servants.CapStatus` → `ServantCapStatus` | experimental (source-checked, not live-verified) | `Ready` (cap book loaded), `Caps`, `ForcedFailures`, `Restores`, `LateRestores` (must stay 0; each one is logged as an error), `Outstanding` (0 outside the update), `Held`, `ReleasedUncapped`. | bridge `servants caps` |
| `ctx.Servants.Resend(ServantsReturned)` → `ServantSendResult` | experimental | The unhurt servants of a finished hunt again: same heart, mission and length choice. Refuses a servant still carrying loot, and a return from another world load. | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants again`, `.servants watch resend`) |
| `ServantsReturned` (event, `ctx.Events.On<ServantsReturned>`) | experimental | After the game finished a hunt: `Heart`, `HeartId`, `Territory`, `MissionGuid`, `MissionName`, `Zone`, `DataSlot`, `LengthSeconds`, `StartedUtc`, `ReturnedUtc`, `Servants` (`ReturnedServant`: `Servant`, `Coffin`, `Name`, `Outcome` = `ServantOutcome.Returned`/`Injured`/`Dead`, `InjuryEndsUtc`, `Inventory`, `Loot` (`ServantLoot`), `LootIsHaul`, `Chance` (the `ServantChance` read just before the game rolled it), `CapForcedFailure` (the cap forced this roll to fail)), `TotalLoot`, `Epoch`. `Dead` only on positive evidence (entity gone, `Dead` or `DestroyTag`, or a revivable coffin; never `DestroyData`). Watched only while subscribed. | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants watch on`) |
| `ServantsSent` (event) | experimental | After a send Progenitor made: `Result`, `By` (plugin GUID, or `progenitor.throne` for a routed throne send). The game's own sends are not announced. | none (read by `SampleServants.cs` only through the reply) |
| `ServantSendRequest` / `ServantSendResult` / `ServantSendRefusal` | experimental | Request: `Heart`, `Servants` (1–3), `MissionGuid`, `DataSlot`, `User` (whose discovery is checked; default the heart's owner), `CheckDiscovered`, `Epoch`, `Announce`, `MaxSuccess` (0..1, the success cap; null vanilla). Result: `Ok`, `Refusal`, `Reason`, `Heart`, `MissionGuid`, `Zone`, `DataSlot`, `LengthSeconds`, `StartedUtc`, `Servants`, `BuffApplied`, `Chances`, `MaxSuccess`, `Announced`. | unit test `ServantLogicTests` |
| `ctx.Servants.RegisterListProvider(IServantListProvider)` → `IDisposable` / `ListStatus` → `ServantListStatus` | experimental | One provider, player-scoped: `HeartsFor(ServantListRequest)` (`User`, `Character`, `Heart`, `Territory`) returns the hearts whose servants the throne lists for that player, or null for the game's list. A throne send naming servants of another heart is performed by Progenitor on their own heart. Tracked; a second provider throws naming the owner. | `SampleServants.cs` (`.servants swap on`); unit test `ServantLogicTests` (registry) |
| `ctx.Servants.SuppressGameNotices(Func<Entity, ServantNoticeKind, bool> suppress)` → `IDisposable`; `ServantNoticeKind` (`Started`, `Returned`, `Killed`) | experimental (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) | Drops the game's hunt chat lines (System lines the client prints from the server's hunt events) for chosen users. Asked once per line and recipient with the user entity: `Started` ("Hunt in X started.", the throne's send and Progenitor's `Send`/`Resend`), `Returned` ("… has returned from a successful Hunt in X", one per servant) and `Killed` ("… was killed during the Hunt in X", one per servant), including hunts that finished while the server was down. True drops that line for that user only; the other recipients (the clan) still get it. Filters of every plugin combine with OR; one that throws keeps the line. Only the line goes: the hunt, loot and `ServantsReturned` are unchanged. Server only; can be registered before the world loads; tracked. `NoticeFilterActive` (bool) is false when the hook failed to install; registration then warns and the lines go out unfiltered. | unit test `ServantNoticeTests` |

## Material sources

Milestone P3, **experimental**: one provider decides which containers pay for building (B menu, the
game's shared-inventory mode), workstation crafting, crafting from the Character window on a plot, item
repair and castle heart upgrades, and the totals the player sees follow the same provider (build menu,
workstation, Character window crafting and castle heart window; a repair cost in an item tooltip keeps
the game's own count). The native availability and payment seams are opt-in Bootstrap seams, **off by default**:
`[Materials] NativeSeams = true` in `progenitor.cfg` on the client and the server, then restart both
(a Bootstrap change). Without them a registration is accepted and changes nothing in the game, and
`Status` says so. Experimental and opt-in; `Repair` and `Upgrade` were live-verified on the isolated test rig on 2026-10-14 (cases 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20 and 22, the last partial: the chest half); not verified: claim staying vanilla (case 21) and the client's own path of an equipped-item repair. Build and Craft were live-verified on the isolated test rig on 2026-10-14 for craft, build, the per-item fallback, the reserve, provider faults, a shortfall between check and payment, disable, the client resend, unload and a server Core swap; other screens reading the same count are not live-verified ([modules.md](modules.md#native-seams-bootstrap)).
Narrative, guarantees, seam facts and limits: [modules.md, Material sources](modules.md#material-sources).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.MaterialSource.Register(IMaterialSourceProvider)` → `IDisposable` | experimental | One provider at a time; a second registration throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the current owner. Tracked: unload releases it; dispose unregisters earlier. Server only, main thread. | `Progenitor.SharedResourcesTest` (`.srtest enable`); unit test `MaterialSourceRegistryTests` |
| `ctx.MaterialSource.Invalidate(Entity user)` / `InvalidateAll()` | experimental | Mark one player's (or every player's) displayed totals stale; `InvalidateAll` also re-reads `Operations`. Payments never use stale data: each operation asks the provider at the game's own check. Server only. | `Progenitor.SharedResourcesTest`; unit test `MaterialSourceRegistryTests` |
| `ctx.MaterialSource.Status` → `MaterialSourceStatus` | experimental | `Registered`, `Owner`, `HooksInstalled` + `HooksReason`, `Requested`, `Wired` (requested and supported, empty while the seams are off), `Revision`, `Payments`, `Views` (per-player view packets the server sent; **changed meaning on 2026-10-14**: it used to count views computed, including unchanged recomputes and the check's composed rows, so older dashboards and logs read higher), `Recomputes` and `Skips` (per-player view refreshes recomputed or reused; see [refresh cost](modules.md#refresh-cost)), `Faults` (provider calls that threw; the log is capped at 20 lines per Core load, the counter is not), `Shortfalls` (operations a cost line of which the pool could not pay in full at payment; the game completes them as it does its own short removals). `ToString()` is one `materials:` log line. The counters reset on every provider registration. Both sides. | `.srtest status`; unit tests `MaterialSourceRegistryTests`, `MaterialTransactionTests` |
| `IMaterialSourceProvider` (`Operations`, `Revision(in MaterialContext)`, `Candidates(in MaterialContext)`, `Sources(in MaterialContext, int item)` → `ItemSources?`) | experimental | Provider contract. `Sources` returns the item's containers in spend order (`ItemSources.SpendOrder`, `ContainerId`) and the pool reserve (`PoolReserve`, kept once across the pool). `null` = the game's own rules pay and show that item; an empty `Candidates` list = this context is not governed. Asked once per item per game operation, at the game's own check, before any count is read, never between the check and the payment. Server main thread: fast, no item moves, no throws (a throw means no remote sources, never the game's storage). | `samples/Progenitor.SharedResourcesTest/MaterialProvider.cs`; unit tests (test doubles) |
| `ItemSources(IReadOnlyList<ContainerId> spendOrder, int poolReserve = 0)` | experimental | One item's answer. Duplicates and unknown or removed ids are ignored; a negative reserve counts as 0. | unit test `MaterialPlannerTests` |
| `MaterialOperation` / `MaterialOperations.Supported` / `IsSupported` / `RequiresStandingPlot` | experimental | `Build`, `Craft`, `Repair`, `Upgrade`, `Research`, `Other`, `CharacterCraft` (= 6). `CharacterCraft`, crafting from the Character window, is experimental, opt-in, game-free tested and live-verified on the isolated test rig on 2026-10-14 (build b6f997d61, run by another session with its own provider): wired, the crafting list showed pool totals on a plot, a bridge-sent craft logged one check and one paid line, `payments` rose and the item was crafted, and off a plot there was no check or paid line and nothing was crafted; not verified: the client's own craft click, a short craft, and the later changes (capability gate, reuse without provider calls, character placement by `Translation`): governed only while the character stands on a plot (`RequiresStandingPlot`; off a plot the game's own rules), the same check-and-pay transaction as `Craft` with no station, and a client view in the Character window's crafting list. Wired: `Build`, `Craft` (live-verified), `CharacterCraft`, `Repair` (both repair events) and `Upgrade` (castle heart level upgrades; claim, raid and destroy costs stay vanilla), the last two experimental, opt-in, live-verified on the isolated test rig on 2026-10-14 (cases 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20 and 22, the last partial: the chest half); not verified: claim staying vanilla (case 21) and the client's own path of an equipped-item repair; `Research` and `Other` stay vanilla. An ingredient a provider governs with no sources counts as zero, even when the plot's own shared storage holds it ([modules.md](modules.md#provider-contract)). | unit tests `MaterialSourceRegistryTests`, `MaterialTransactionTests`, `MaterialUpgradeFilterTests`; `Progenitor.SharedResourcesTest` (repair and upgrade cases 15–22) |
| `MaterialContext` | experimental | `User`, `Character` (`CastleId`, session ids), `WorldEpoch`, `Operation`, `StandingTerritory` (-1 = no plot), `Station` (`ContainerId` of the structure the operation runs at: the workstation for `Craft` and for a `Repair` at a station, the castle heart for `Upgrade`, none for `Build`, for `CharacterCraft` and for a repair with no workstation window open). | unit tests `MaterialServiceTests`, `MaterialTransactionTests` |
| `MaterialPaid` (event) / `MaterialDebit` | experimental | Published on the server for each cost line the pool paid any part of, after the game system finished the operation (same frame): `Context`, `Transaction` (shared by every line of one operation), `Item`, `Requested`, `Paid` (less than `Requested` after a shortfall), `Debits` (container, amount, in the order taken). | unit test `MaterialTransactionTests` |

## Buffs

Apply, query and remove a **timed** buff this plugin defined with `ctx.Content.DefineBuff`. Server
only; a client call is refused with a sentence, not an exception. Narrative, the supported stat
modifiers and the live procedure: [`buffs.md`](buffs.md).

Every call and every handle method runs on the game main thread. `ctx.Buffs` `Track`s each
application, so plugin unload removes the buffs this plugin applied. The reloader sweeps buffs off
their wearers (`Buffs.SweepForUnload`) **before** it unregisters the plugin's prefab definitions,
and Core uninstall finishes every live application before `Units` tears down.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Buffs` (`PluginBuffs`) `Apply(BuffRequest)` / `Apply(id, Entity, seconds)` / `ApplyOrRefresh(id, Entity, seconds)` / `Stats(id, params BuffStat[])` / `Find(id, Entity)` / `Active()` / `RemoveAll()` | landed (port) | **These seven are the whole facade** (`PluginContextBuffs.cs`). `Apply` returns a tracked `IBuffApplication`; a refusal is a handle in `Refused` with a reason, never a throw. Unload removes the applications this plugin made **silently** — no `OnStateChanged` — because `ctx.UndoAll()` runs while this assembly's handlers are still attached and going away. Put cleanup in `OnUnload`, not in that handler. | `samples/Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` (`.rested`) |
| `IBuffApplication` `State` / `IsActive` / `Instance` / `Target` / `BuffId` / `PluginId` / `Refusal` / `RefusalReason` / `RequestedDurationSeconds` / `LastRefreshRefusal` / `LastRefreshRefusalReason` / `OnStateChanged` / `Refresh(seconds)` / `Remove()` / `Dispose()` | landed (port) | Owns **only this application's** instance. `Refresh(seconds)` leaves **`seconds` remaining from now**. `State` and `Refusal` always describe the **original apply**, so on the `ApplyOrRefresh` route they stay `Active`/`None` even when the refresh was refused: read `LastRefreshRefusal` (`None` when the last refresh took, or was never attempted) to tell those apart. `Remove` == `Dispose`; repeated removal, removal after expiry, and removal after a world reload are all no-ops. Cancelling before confirmation keeps watching long enough to destroy an instance that arrives late. | `samples/Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` (`.unrested`, `.restedstatus`) |
| `BuffApplicationState` `Refused` / `Requested` / `Active` / `Ended` / `Removed` / `TargetLost` / `NotConfirmed` | landed (port) | **A request is not an active buff**: `ApplyBuff` queues an event and the game builds the instance on a later update. `Ended` is one observation covering expiry, wearer death and a foreign destroy — this path cannot tell them apart. | unit test: `BuffLifecycleTests.cs` |
| `BuffRequest.For(id, target)` / `WithDuration(float\|TimeSpan)` / `WithPrefabDuration()` / `WithRefresh(BuffRefreshPolicy)` / `KeepPrefabDuration` | landed (port) | Duration is written onto the confirmed instance's `LifeTime.Duration`; `EndAction` is never written, so the cloned vanilla base decides what happens at zero. | `samples/Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` |
| `BuffRefreshPolicy` `Reject` (default) / `Refresh` | landed (port) | A second apply by the same owner is refused, or rewrites the live one's duration and returns **that** application. **`Refresh(seconds)` leaves `seconds` remaining from now.** The game counts elapsed time in a separate `Age` component (the server's `LifeTimeSystem` takes `RefRW<Age>` + `RefRO<LifeTime>`) while `LifeTime.Duration` is the **total** lifetime, so the API reads `Age` and writes `elapsed + seconds`. It never writes `Age`, which is not networked and has no snapshot type — zeroing it server-side would desynchronize the client's own timer. A refresh is **refused, never clamped or guessed**, when the timing cannot be read, when `elapsed + seconds` would leave the `SyncRangedFloat(0, 10000)` range, or when `LifeTime.EndAction` is `RemoveBuffStack` (there `Duration` is one stack *period*, not a lifetime) or a value this build does not recognise. Until this landed, `Refresh` wrote the argument straight into `Duration`, so a refresh shorter than the time already elapsed ended the buff at once — observed live 2026-09-13. **Stacking is not exposed**: `Stacks` is always 1 because per-prefab stack semantics are unverified for a clone. | unit test: `BuffLifecycleTests.cs` |
| `BuffRefusal` / `BuffRefusalText.For(refusal, detail)` / `Line(...)` | landed (port) | Nineteen refusals, each with one actionable sentence: wrong side, no world, unregistered id, registered-but-not-applied clone, missing target, target with no `BuffBuffer`, already owned, already active, bad duration, module unavailable, apply path unavailable, untimed clone, an instance already on the target that no application created, a cancel still in flight, and the five a remaining-time refresh can hit — timing unreadable, total out of range, an end action whose `Duration` is not a lifetime, a write that did not take, and a handle that is not `Active`. | unit test: `BuffLifecycleTests.cs` (`Every_refusal_has_an_actionable_sentence`) |
| `BuffEntityId` (`Index`, `Version`, `IsNone`, `None`, `From(Entity)`, `ToEntity()`) | landed (port) | Entity identity without a `Unity.Entities` dependency, so the lifecycle is tested without the game. Never persist one. | unit test: `BuffLifecycleTests.cs` |
| `BuffApplicationPolicy` `ConfirmFrames` / `MaxDurationSeconds` / `IsDurationUsable` / `IsTerminal` / `IsLive` / `EndedMeaning` | landed (port) | The public half of the decision table. `ConfirmFrames` is 8, the same measured window as `PrefabLiveRefresh.RestoreDurationFrames`. The conflict and poll enums are `internal`. | unit test: `BuffStatPolicyTests.cs` |
| `BuffStat.BloodDrain(op, value, priority, scope)` / `BuffStat.Of(kind, op, value, priority, scope)` / `BuffStat.Suppress(kind)` / `IsUsable(out reason)` / `Scope` / `IsSuppression`, `BuffStatKind`, `BuffStatOp`, `BloodDrainScope`, `BuffStatSupport.IsSupported` / `Reason` / `ComponentName` / `MaySuppress` / `SuppressionComponents` / `NeverSuppressed` | landed (port) | The supported stat-modifier set. **Blood drain is the only modifier this build will write**; `BuffStatKind.MovementSpeed` is refused for writes with the missing evidence named. Writes go onto **this plugin's own clone** through `ctx.Content.Edit`. `BloodDrainScope.Active` is the default and writes the active drain only; `ActiveAndIdle` writes both and registers **two modifications against the same value**, which compounds under `Add`/`Multiply` — confirmed live 2026-09-13, where one `Multiply 0.1` produced 0.01x. `Active` yielding exactly **one** registered modification was observed on the pre-port branch on 2026-09-14 (drain 0.0005 = 0.005 x 0.1); the tests pin the write set, not the game's registry. `Suppress(kind)` removes what the cloned base carries for that kind from your own clone, prefab-only — supported for `MovementSpeed` even though *writing* it is refused, because removing a component needs none of the unknown value semantics. `Suppress(BloodDrain)` is refused rather than aliased to `BloodDrain(Set, 0f)`. | `samples/Progenitor.TimedBuffExample/Plugin.cs` (`ctx.Buffs.Stats`) |
| `BuffDrainWrite` (offsets, `ComponentSize`, `NeverWritten`, `TypeOf`, `For`) / `BuffFieldWrite` | landed (port) | The game-free `ModifyBloodDrainBuff` layout and the decision about which fields a stat writes, extracted out of the raw-pointer path so both are testable. `NeverWritten` pins the three `ModificationId` fields and `IgnoreIdleDrainWhileActive` as untouchable — the game issues the ids on the instance so its own destroy system can reverse the contribution. | unit test: `BuffDrainWriteTests.cs` |
| `Buffs.Apply(request, owner?, pluginId?)` / `Buffs.Find(id, target)` / `Buffs.Active(owner)` / `Buffs.RemoveAll(owner)` | landed (port) | The static forms. `RemoveAll(owner)` removes **only that owner's** applications by reference; the assembly-wide teardown is the reloader's `internal SweepForUnload(owner, pluginId)`, the same split as `Units`. `Find` returns an application **whoever owns it**, so subscribing to another plugin's is supported — and that sweep drops the handlers your assembly wrote on a stranger's application while leaving the application itself running, so your ALC is not pinned by one. | unit test: `BuffLifecycleTests.cs` |

**Verification of this port.** The rows above are source-checked, compile-verified (Core, both
samples, Debug and Release) and game-free tested (`BuffLifecycleTests`, `BuffStatPolicyTests`,
`BuffDrainWriteTests`, `ApiValidationBuffsTests`, and the reloader-order pin in
`PluginReloaderTests`) on current `main`. They are **not verified live on main**. The same buff code
passed live qualification on 2026-09-14 on the pre-port branch (isolated pair: apply/expire against
a recorded drain baseline, two modifications for `ActiveAndIdle` and one for `Active`, movement
untouched, overlap, manual removal, refresh short and long, and plugin unload with a live buff with
the server surviving on the same PID). That run predates the port, so it does not qualify this
integration; `buffs.md` "Later live procedure" is the sequence that would. `RefreshTotalOutOfRange`,
`RefreshWriteFailed`, `RefreshNotActive` and `RefreshEndActionUnsupported` were never reachable live
through the samples and remain offline-only.

`Buffs` is **not** a `SkipCoreModules` token: that list is mirrored by Bootstrap and pinned equal by
`HarmonySlotHandoffTests`, so adding it would be a Bootstrap change. The module always installs.

## Abilities

Server only; client calls throw. Main thread. See [modules.md](modules.md#abilities) for the
live facts and the difference between the two cast paths.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Abilities.ForceCast(Entity character, int abilityGroupGuid)` / `ForceCast(…, out string why)` | landed, not live-verified | `ServerGameManager.ForceCastAbilityGroup`. Starts a group that is not on the character's bar (the underlying call was observed live 2026-09-25; this wrapper was not). Returns false when the world, `ServerScriptMapper` or character is missing, the character has no `AbilityBar_Shared`, or the group is 0. True means the call returned, not that a cast started. Aimed abilities use `EntityInput.AimPosition` (a player's mouse). | unit test `AbilityCooldownPolicyTests` (refusal text) |
| `ctx.Abilities.NoCooldowns(Entity character)` → `IDisposable` | landed, not live-verified | Every ~0.1 s zero `AbilityCooldownState` on each entity `EntityOwner`-owned by the character and fill `AbilityChargesState.CurrentCharges` to `AbilityChargesData.MaxCharges`. Charges are never lowered. Owned lists rebuilt on take, on a held character's cast-counter advance and 0.5 s after; a failed rebuild keeps the old lists. Holds nest; released on dispose, plugin unload, character destroyed, a new world epoch, Core uninstall. Nothing is restored. Refusal returns an inert handle and logs. | unit test `AbilityCooldownPolicyTests` |
| `ctx.Abilities.IsNoCooldowns(Entity character)` | landed, not live-verified | True while this plugin holds `NoCooldowns` on the character. | unit test `AbilityCooldownPolicyTests` (hold table) |
| `ctx.Abilities.SetSlot(Entity character, int slot, int abilityGroupGuid)` → `IDisposable` | landed; the recipe live-verified 2026-09-25, this build not yet | Put a group on bar slot 0-8 of a **player** character until the handle is disposed or the plugin unloads; the slot then goes back. A Progenitor-owned clone of `Buff_VBlood_Ability_Replace` carrying one `ReplaceAbilityOnSlotBuff` element (priority 100), applied to the character; the game's `ReplaceAbilityOnSlotSystem` swaps it. One clone per plugin, slot and group (`progenitor.abilityslot.<plugin>.<slot>.<group>`). Slots are per character; on a player (measured): 0 primary, 1/4 weapon Q/E, 2 Space, 5 R, 6 C, 7 T. Two of one plugin's handles for a slot and group share one buff, re-applied if something else removed it. Bad slot or group 0 throws; a missing world, or a gone or non-player character, returns an inert handle and logs. **Known limit:** the swap is a buff, and buffs are saved with the character; if the server dies without unloading the plugin after an autosave, the swap stays on the character after restart with no owner, until the plugin slots the same group again (which adopts it) or the buff is removed; if the plugin is removed or renamed before the restart, the saved buff names a clone prefab that no longer exists, which the save guard for unknown buff prefabs has to drop (see modules.md). Two plugins slotting different groups onto one slot of one character both apply at priority 100, and which one shows is not defined. Live (with the earlier per-slot clone id, before the per-plugin id, the re-apply rule and the player-only check): Corrupted Skull, Chains of Death and Unstable Arachnid shown on R/C/T of a player's bar (server slots read back, client bar captured), then released back to the previous spells. | unit test `AbilitySlotPolicyTests`; live (NecroArmyExample `.arena setup` / `.arena reset`) |
| `AbilityCast.Cast(world, character, group, out why)` | verified live | Writes `AbilityBar_Shared.ForceCastGroup`. **True means the request was written, not that a cast started.** A group not on the character's bar is consumed without starting (live 2026-09-25). | `SampleFrostDash.cs` (`.frostdash dash`) |

## Units

Server spawn and steering. The client returns a disabled handle. `ctx.Units` `Track`s handles. Never
a bare marker entity; never a raw `PathRequest`.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Units` (`PluginUnits`) `Spawn(string\|int, UnitVec3, behaviourTree?)` / `Follow(IUnitHandle, Entity)` / `Engage(IUnitHandle, Entity)` / `RemoveAll()` | landed | Name or GUID hash; `UnitVec3` position. `Engage` requests Combat and sets `AggroConsumer.AggroTarget`. It does not clear `Immortal` and it does not post Follow. | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat`, `.rat follow`); boss arena run uses the debug `engage` op, which calls `Units.Engage` |
| `UnitEngageResult` | landed | `CombatRequested`, `AggroWritten`, `Ok`. | returned by `Engage` |
| `ctx.Units.Spawn(string, UnitVec3, UnitSpawnOptions)` / `ctx.Units.SetFaction(IUnitHandle, string faction)` | landed, not live-verified | `UnitSpawnOptions` (`Faction`, `BehaviourTree`, `HitboxScale`, `MoveSpeedMultiplier`, `Look`, `Stats`, `Aura`, `HideHealthBar`, `TeamOf`, `Permanent`, `DropLoot`, `Entrance`; the last ten in the next rows): writes the unit's `FactionReference` (the faction prefab GUID; the game derives `Team.FactionIndex` from it at spawn, live) and its `Team.FactionIndex` (the blob's `Index`) on the spawn tick, and checks both again at 1 s and 5 s. An unknown faction throws before anything spawns. Every `ctx.Units.Spawn` also logs one warning per plugin generation when the plugin's live units first exceed `AiLimits.RecommendedActiveUnits`. Several copies of a boss whose faction is not self-friendly (Frostmaw) damage each other; this is never changed silently ([ai.md](ai.md#defaults-a-spawned-boss-brings-with-it)). | `samples/Progenitor.BossVariantsExample` (`.bossvariants spawn`) |
| `ctx.Units.SetHitboxScale(IUnitHandle, float)` / `GetHitboxScale(IUnitHandle)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.HitboxScale` | verified live | Server-side hitbox of a spawned unit, 0.25 to 4; 1 restores the vanilla collider. The unit's `PhysicsCollider` is pointed at a private copy of its compound collider whose Hittable capsule or sphere is scaled with its bottom kept on the ground, with the BVH refitted. Copies are shared per (collider, scale) and never freed. Any other collider shape is refused. Visual size is separate: `SetLook` (below) is client-side and never changes the hitbox. A saved unit loads with its vanilla hitbox. [modules.md](modules.md#hitbox-scale-and-move-speed). | unit test `UnitHitboxSpeedTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetMoveSpeed(IUnitHandle, float multiplier)` → `bool` / `GetMoveSpeed(IUnitHandle)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.MoveSpeedMultiplier` | verified live | Scales `Movement.Speed` (chase speed) and the four `AiMoveSpeeds` values, 0.1 to 4 x vanilla; 1 restores. Applied as a ratio to the current value so the game's temporary speed effects are kept, and tracked per handle so calls never compound. Returns false while the unit's speed reads 0 (retried at 1 s and 5 s). Both components are saved with the world, so a unit restored from a save keeps the speed. [modules.md](modules.md#hitbox-scale-and-move-speed). | unit test `UnitHitboxSpeedTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetStats(IUnitHandle, UnitStatOverrides)` → `bool` / `GetStats(IUnitHandle)` → `UnitStatOverrides`; `UnitSpawnOptions.Stats`; `UnitStatOverrides` (`Level`, `MaxHealth`, `PhysicalPower`, `SpellPower`, `IsEmpty`, `Refusal()`, `Validate()`, `Clone()`, range constants) | verified live (spawn option, 2026-09-25) | Absolute level (1 to 120, `UnitLevel.Level`), max health (above 0, at most 10,000,000, `Health.MaxHealth`) and physical and spell power (0 to 10,000, `UnitStats`) of a spawned unit; a null field is left as it is and out-of-range values throw. Setting max health fills current health to it and is refused on a dead or downed unit (never a revive). Level feeds the game's level-difference damage scaling and does not recompute power or health; `HideLevel` is not touched. A fresh unit reads max health 0 until the game's spawn computation fills it, so the write waits for that (returns false) and is made by checks at 0.25 s, 1 s and 5 s, which also write a value again if the game resets it. Temporary modifiers active at a later call are kept (ratio for power and health, offset for level). All three components are saved with the world. Vanilla limitation: clients receive current health clamped to 100,000, so a unit above that shows a bar that is too short (display only; see [modules.md](modules.md#unit-stats)). Measured defaults are listed in [modules.md](modules.md#unit-stats). Unit-tested, not live-verified. | unit test `UnitStatsTests` |
| `ctx.Units.GetHealth(IUnitHandle)` → `UnitHealth` (`Current`, `Max`, `IsDead`, `IsDowned`, `IsStanding`) | landed | Current health (`Health.Value`), max health (`Health.MaxHealth`) and dead (`Health.IsDead` or a `Dead` component) of a unit this plugin spawned, read through the guarded path the stat code uses. `IsDowned` is health at or below 0 without being dead, which is how a downed V Blood reads; a unit spawned this frame reads max 0 until the game fills it, and is neither downed nor standing. Server-only; throws on a disposed handle, a gone unit or no `Health`. Used by the necro army sample to stop its spell barrage when Dracula falls. | sample `Progenitor.NecroArmyExample` |
| `ctx.Units.SetBlood(Entity character, string bloodType, float quality[, out string why])` / `SetBlood(…, int bloodTypeGuid, …)` → `bool` | landed, live-verified 2026-09-25 (event recipe) | Give a player character a blood type at 0-100 % quality with a full pool, through the game's own admin "consume blood" event (`ConsumeBloodAdminEvent`, raised on the server as the admin console would; `VariousMigratedDebugEventsSystem` applies it next update and it is saved with the character). The type is the prefab name with or without `BloodType_` (`"Draculin"`). Types in this build include Brute (804798592), Corruption (-1382693416), Creature (524822543), Draculin (1328126535), Rogue (-1620185637), Warrior (-516976528), Worker (-1776904174) and None (frail, 447918373). True means queued. False with a reason for a non-player, quality outside 0-100, or a name or GUID that is not a `BloodType_` prefab. Verified on an admin character; the handler's behavior for a non-admin target is unverified. Server-only (throws on the client). Live: the recipe itself (Worker 4 % to Draculin 100 %) was run from a snippet; the wrapper was not run live because the test server was down when it was written. | unit test `UnitBloodAndBreakablesTests`; sample `Progenitor.NecroArmyExample` `.arena setup` |
| `ctx.Units.ClearBreakables(UnitVec3 center, float radius, Func<int,bool>? keep = null)` → `int` | landed, live-verified 2026-09-25 (recipe) | Destroy every spawn-chain-placed tile model (`SpawnChainChild`; in Dracula's throne hall these were the breakable banners, braziers, vases and pillars, but a chain-placed gate or interactable would go too, so check the area or use `keep`) within `radius` on the x-z plane and 3 m (`UnitBreakablesPolicy.HeightBand`) of the center's height; units are never touched. `DestroyUtility.Destroy`, which replicates to clients. The spawn chains stay, so the area's reset restores them; some chains restore within seconds (Dracula's four arena pillars came back in about 20 s), so `keep(prefabGuid)` returning true leaves a prop. Returns the count. Server-only (throws on the client). Live: the same destroy pass removed 42 props from Dracula's throne hall (8 more by a first pass); the wrapper was not run live because the test server was down when it was written. | unit test `UnitBloodAndBreakablesTests`; sample `Progenitor.NecroArmyExample` `.arena setup` |
| `ctx.Units.SetPet(IUnitHandle, Entity owner)` / `SetPet(IUnitHandle, Entity owner, PetStance)` / `SetPetStance(IUnitHandle, PetStance)` / `GetPetStance(IUnitHandle)` → `PetStance?` / `PetAttack(IUnitHandle, Entity target)` / `HoldAt(IUnitHandle, UnitVec3)` / `Hold(IUnitHandle, bool)` / `IsHeld(IUnitHandle)` / `ClearPet(IUnitHandle)` / `IsPet(IUnitHandle)`; `PetStance` (`Follow`, `Hold`, `Attack`) | verified live (`Follow` through the two-argument `SetPet`); `Hold` and `Attack` landed, not live-verified | **Follow** (companion mode; the two-argument `SetPet` is kept as its own overload for binary compatibility): every 0.5 s the unit follows the owner while it has no live aggro target, and on a live target that is not the owner it clears `Follower.Followed` and posts Combat, returning to the owner when the target is gone. Walk speed is kept equal to run speed. Plain `Follow` holds the unit in the game's Follow state, so a following unit never fights. **Hold**, for pets and any other spawned unit: every 0.5 s `AggroConsumer.Active` (@0x50) is written 0; `AggroTarget` (@0x30), `AlertTarget` (@0x3C) and `Follower.Followed` are cleared; Idle is posted from Alert, Combat, Return, Follow or Relocate_CombatArea; and the unit is walked back with the runtime GoTo write (no handle events) when more than 2 m from its hold position. That it then acquires no aggro and does not fight back is not live-verified. Release (stance change, `PetAttack`, `Hold(false)`, `ClearPet`, unload) writes `Active` back to 1 only when the hold saved 1, `Active` still reads 0, and no `DisableAggroBuff` `TargetDontAttackOthers` buff is on the unit. A world save keeps a held unit's `Active` 0; after a crash without unload nothing releases it. **Attack**: the `Engage` write (aggro target, `Active` forced to 1) plus Combat every step until the target is dead or gone, then Follow. Dead units drop out. Every call throws off the main thread ([modules.md](modules.md#pet-stances-and-hold)). | research harness (Dracula pet test, 2026-09-25) for Follow; unit test `PetStanceTests` |
| `UnitSpawnOptions.Permanent` / `DropLoot` / `Entrance`; `UnitEntrance` (`None`, `RiseFromGround`) | landed, not live-verified | On the spawn tick: `Permanent` writes a unit's `LifeTime` to `{-1, None}` (the death knight ships `{6, Kill}`); `DropLoot = false` removes `DropTableBuffer`, `DropTableOnDeath`, `DropTableOnDestroy` and the `DropTable` tag (the death knight drops `DT_Unit_Dunley_Militia_Rank02`); `Entrance = RiseFromGround` applies `Buff_SpawnBuff_Skeleton_EarthCrawl` (1820474368). An undefined `Entrance` throws before anything spawns. The lifetime and drop changes are on the unit, so a world save keeps them. Unit-tested, not live-verified ([modules.md](modules.md#spawn-options-permanent-droploot-entrance)). | unit test `PetStanceTests` |
| `UnitSpawnOptions.LifetimeSeconds` / `IUnitHandle.TrySetLifetime(float)` | landed, not live-verified | Ends the unit `seconds` of server running time after the call (0.05 s to 24 h; server downtime does not count). Adds `Age` when the unit has none and writes `LifeTime { Age + seconds, Destroy }` on the entity: the game's `LifeTimeSystem` only sees entities with `Age`, and a prefab without it never expired before. The stamp is saved with the unit. Plugin unload despawns owned units at once. After a Core swap, a world load or a restart, Core's lifetime sweep (at install, 5 s after each load, then every 5 s) despawns any API-spawned unit more than 2 s past its end and adds `Age` to one saved without it. Units without the API-spawn marker, `Permanent` units and vanilla `Kill` lifetimes are never touched ([modules.md](modules.md#lifetime-across-reload-and-restart)). | unit test `UnitExpiryTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetLook(IUnitHandle, UnitLook)` / `GetLook(IUnitHandle)` → `UnitLook` / `ClearLook(IUnitHandle)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.Look`; `UnitLook` (`VisualScale`, `Tint`, `GlowIntensity`, `Overlay`, `OverlayStrength`, `Validate()`, `IsVanilla`, `Vanilla`, bound constants) | verified live | How a spawned unit looks on Progenitor clients. `VisualScale` is relative to the creature's natural model size (hard 0.1 to 5, 0.25 to 3 recommended); `Tint` multiplies the texture (`EffectColor`, channels 0 to 1, alpha ignored); `GlowIntensity` multiplies the tint in HDR (1 none, 4 to 6 recommended, hard cap 8); `Overlay` + `OverlayStrength` (0 to 1) is a flat fill on the hit-flash property. Out-of-range values throw. The server sends the look on Core event `'PruL'` (on set, clear, join and despawn); the client applies it to the model's `localScale` and `MaterialPropertyBlock` and reapplies it when the model is rebuilt. Unmodded clients see vanilla; the hitbox is unchanged. Bounds measured live; this implementation is unit-tested, not live-verified. [modules.md](modules.md#unit-look-size-tint-glow-overlay). | unit test `UnitLookTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetLook(Entity, UnitLook)` / `GetLook(Entity)` → `UnitLook` / `ClearLook(Entity)` | landed, not live-verified | The same look on any networked entity with a model, such as a player character, by entity instead of handle. Same table, wire and client applier (keyed by `NetworkId`). Plugin-owned: cleared on plugin unload, when the entity is destroyed, on a world load, and when a player character's user disconnects (set it again on `PlayerConnected` if wanted). Throws `InvalidOperationException` for an entity without a `Normal` `NetworkId`, a missing entity, or a player character whose user is offline. A player's model is naturally scale 1 (live, 2026-09-25). A reconnecting viewer gets the look from the join sync; a new model instance (shapeshift, streaming) is rescaled from its own natural size. Unit-tested, not live-verified. [modules.md](modules.md#look-for-any-character). | unit test `UnitLookTests` |
| `ctx.Units.JoinTeam(IUnitHandle, Entity character)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.TeamOf` | landed, not live-verified | Copies the character's `Team.Value` and `TeamReference` (a `ModifiableEntity`) onto a spawned unit, on the spawn tick for the option, checked again at 1 s and 5 s; keeps the unit's own `Team.FactionIndex`. Game default (live, 2026-09-25): API units sit on team 2 (`Team.UNIT_TEAM`, the unit-team entity) even with `Faction_Players`, while a player is on their own team (11 in the test), so the player's projectiles hit them. With the player's team written (scratch), the player's skulls passed through the army and it still fought Dracula; its client bars turned allied. Separate from ally-safe hits. Throws for `Entity.Null`, the unit itself, a missing character, either side lacking `Team`/`TeamReference`, or while the unit wears a `ModifyTeamBuff` buff (the modification registry would restore the old team when it ends). Not restored. [modules.md](modules.md#join-a-characters-team). | unit test `UnitTeamTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetHealthBarHidden(IUnitHandle, bool)` → `bool` / `IsHealthBarHidden(IUnitHandle)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.HideHealthBar` | landed, not live-verified | Hides a spawned unit's health bar and name plate on Progenitor clients with a Core-owned inert buff (`progenitor.core.hide-hud`, a stripped `Buff_General_Haste` clone) carrying `HideTargetHUD { Type = -1 }` on the client's copy only (the server prefab is left without it; an unidentified server reader instantiates `BuffUtility.HasBuff<HideTargetHUD>`); the client's `GetCharacterHUDSystem` skips the HUD of a unit wearing such a buff. `false` shows it again (the buff is destroyed). Returns false, changing nothing, while the clone is not verified. Checked again at 1 s and 5 s; saved with the unit; plugin unload shows the bar again on units it hid. What a client without Progenitor shows is not verified. Unit-tested, not live-verified. [modules.md](modules.md#hide-a-units-health-bar). | unit test `InertCloneRulesTests` |
| `ctx.Units.SetAura(IUnitHandle, UnitAura)` → `bool` / `GetAura(IUnitHandle)` / `GetAura(Entity)` → `UnitAura` / `ClearAura(IUnitHandle)`; `UnitSpawnOptions.Aura`; `UnitAura` (`None`, `Elite`) | landed, not live-verified | A server-applied aura on a spawned unit. `Elite` is the elite-affix purple ring and glow: a Core-owned `DefineBuff` clone of `Shared_Affix_LevelAura_Self` with the fixed id `progenitor.core.aura.elite`, stripped on the server prefab so it carries none of the vanilla buff's gameplay (the +8 levels to units within 15 m). A clone Core has not verified inert is never applied: `SetAura` returns false and logs one warning. The game draws the ring natively on every client that has the clone; Progenitor clients build it from the registry, and a client without Progenitor is not verified. One aura per unit: another replaces it, `None` clears. `ClearAura` destroys the buff instance. `GetAura` reads the unit's buffs. The buff is saved with the unit, so a restored unit keeps its aura; the `Entity` overload reads it without a handle. Unlike `SetLook`, no Progenitor client code draws it. Mechanism verified live (the clone shows the ring on a client); this implementation unit-tested, not live-verified. [modules.md](modules.md#unit-aura). | unit test `UnitAuraTests` |
| `ctx.Units.ClearArea(float3, float, ClearAreaOptions?)` → `ClearAreaResult`; `ClearAreaOptions` (`Scope`, `IncludeUnits`, `IncludeSummons`, `IncludeDrops`, `IncludeUnattributedDrops`, `MaxPasses`, `OnComplete`); `ClearAreaScope` (`Owned`, `All`) | landed, not live-verified | Removes units in a radius over several passes (bosses keep summoning while they are removed), through the replicated removal path. `Owned` (default) removes this plugin's units and their summons; drops only when attributable or with `IncludeUnattributedDrops`. `All` removes every non-player NPC unit and drop in the radius. Players, anything a player owns, servants, castle pieces and unowned mounts are never removed. Every despawn also sweeps the unit's summons. | `samples/Progenitor.BossVariantsExample` (`.bossvariants clear`) |
| `IUnitHandle` `Follow(UnitId)` / `GoTo(UnitVec3)` / `Despawn()` / `Position` / `Entity` / `Enabled` / `Prefab` / `PluginId` / `IsDisposed` / `State` | verified live | Follow and GoTo are exclusive and clear each other's steering state. `Despawn` == `Dispose`; double-dispose is a no-op. Despawn's final step is the replicated `DestroyUtility.Destroy` (`BuffSweep.DestroyUnitReplicated`; the change from an immediate `DestroyTag` is not yet re-verified live through `ctx.Units`). | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat follow` -> `Follow`, `.rat go` -> `GoTo`, `.rat off` -> `Despawn`) |
| `Units.Spawn(string\|int, UnitVec3, owner?, pluginId?, behaviourTree?)` / `Units.RemoveAll(owner)` | verified live | The only static forms (`Units.cs:54,73,83`). There is **no** static `Units.Follow` and **no** static `Units.GoTo` — steer through the handle. `RemoveAll(owner)` despawns **only that owner's** units and does nothing else (`Units.cs:102`), so it is safe for retiring one subsystem; the assembly-wide teardown is the reloader's own `SweepForUnload(owner, pluginId)` (`Units.cs:123`), which is `internal` and has no public form (FUNIT6, R59 finding 4). | `SampleUnits.cs` (the game-free `Units.Spawn` path) |
| `IUnitHandle.Behaviour` — `Play(params UnitStep[])` / `Stop()` / `IsPlaying` / `StepIndex` / `StepCount` | landed, not live-verified | Hookable steps over a **vanilla** tree. Progenitor never compiles a behavior-tree blob. One step at a time from the Units frame poll. | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat patrol`) |
| `UnitStep.Idle/GoTo/Follow/Face/Wait/Animate/Then` | landed, not live-verified | GoTo ends on Arrived and fails on Unreachable; Wait and Animate on a module timer (never a BT `Delay` node); the rest on the tick that started them. A failed step stops the sequence; a refused `Animate` is `Skipped` and it carries on. | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat patrol`: Idle, Face, Wait, GoTo, Then, Wait, Animate, Follow, all seven factories). `Idle` is step 1 and appears nowhere else: it is the one position the sequencer accepts without doubling its own forced post, and an Idle between steps sends a unit that has already walked to `Return` |
| `Spawn(..., behaviourTree:)` | landed, not live-verified | Selects a vanilla tree on the **spawn tick** only (`BehaviourTreeBindingSystem_Spawn` is in `ReactToSpawnGroup`). A GUID whose prefab has no `BehaviourTree` is refused with one log line; the unit keeps its own tree. | `SampleUnits.cs` (`.rat patrol` passes `BEH_Farmlands_Animal_Standard`) |
| `UnitStepCompleted` / `UnitSequenceCompleted` | landed, not live-verified | On `EventBus` with the owning handle. Despawn and Core uninstall stop the sequencer silently and publish neither. **Plugin unload is the exception**, and it is the reloader's `Units.SweepForUnload(owner, pluginId)` that makes it — not `Units.RemoveAll(owner)`, which despawns that owner's units and publishes nothing (`Units.cs:102,123`; FUNIT6, R59 finding 4). On that sweep a still-live sequence owned by the unloading plugin (the same owner object, any owner whose type its assembly declares, or the `pluginId` the handle was spawned with) ends with a published `Stopped`, and anyone else's keeps playing with the unloading assembly's `Then` steps reported `Skipped` ("callback dropped") and nothing published. The units the sweep despawns for its own owner take the silent Despawn route. | `SampleUnitEvents.cs` — `.rat patrol` then `.rat status` (one line per step; `.rat stop` reports `Stopped`, `.rat off` deliberately reports nothing) |
| `Animate` on the server | refused by design | `AnimationStateBuffer` is a Client-world buffer. The step is held for its duration and reported `Skipped`; the real route is client-side and belongs to Effects / Buffs. | `SampleUnits.cs` (the step the Sample expects to be `Skipped`) |

## Triggers

`ctx.Triggers` watches connected players. When one crosses into or out of a circle or an axis-aligned rectangle, the action runs on the server simulation thread and may call `ctx.Units.Spawn`. Other units do not fire it, so a creature spawned in the area does not trip the watch. Register on the game main thread (`OnLoad`, a command, an event, or `MainThread.Post`).

Both shapes have a finite height, so a watch on one floor of a castle does not see the floor above or below. A circle is a vertical cylinder: `Radius` on x and z around `Center`, and `Height` meters tall centered on `Center`'s height (the top and bottom faces are included). A rectangle is an axis-aligned box from `Min` to `Max` on x, y, and z; the corner order does not matter, and each axis must have a positive size. There is no shape that ignores height.

Leave fires only after the player is `AreaTriggerOptions.LeaveSkin` (0.5 m) past every face, including up and down, so standing on the rim does not chatter. Enter uses the registered volume. A crossing that both starts and ends outside the volume in one simulation tick is not seen.

The first time a player's position is seen, the watch records which side they are on and fires nothing. A plugin reload, a world load, or a reconnect while they are already inside does not fire enter. Disconnecting while inside fires leave and forgets them; connecting again only records a side until they cross. A tick with no readable position changes nothing. A player scan that throws changes nothing: it is not treated as everyone leaving.

`Watch` reads the options once. Dispose the `AreaTrigger` to stop that watch. Unload disposes every watch this plugin created. The client returns an inactive handle and logs one line, so shared `OnLoad` code can register on both sides.

```csharp
using Progenitor.Triggers;

ctx.Triggers.Watch(new AreaTriggerOptions
{
    Center = new UnitVec3(-2300f, 17f, -1200f),
    Radius = 8f,
    Height = 4f,
    OnEnter = hit => ctx.Units.Spawn("CHAR_Undead_SkeletonSoldier_Armored_Dunley", hit.Position),
    OnLeave = hit => ctx.Log.LogInfo(hit.CharacterName + " left " + hit.Center),
});

ctx.Triggers.Watch(new AreaTriggerOptions
{
    Shape = AreaTriggerShape.Rectangle,
    Min = new UnitVec3(-2312f, 15f, -1212f),
    Max = new UnitVec3(-2288f, 19f, -1188f),
    OnEnter = hit => ctx.Units.Spawn("CHAR_Critter_Rat", hit.Center),
});
```

`OnEnter(center, radius, height, action)` and `OnLeave(center, radius, height, action)` watch a circle. `OnEnter(min, max, action)` and `OnLeave(min, max, action)` watch a rectangle. Spawn at `hit.Center` when the creature should appear in the middle of the area rather than on the player. `hit.PlatformId` is `User.PlatformId`. For a circle, `hit.Min` and `hit.Max` are the cylinder's bounding box. For a rectangle, `hit.Radius` is zero.

The Sample command is `.triggers on` (`samples/Progenitor.Sample/SampleTriggers.cs`). It arms a 6 m circle, 4 m tall, and a 16 m by 4 m by 16 m rectangle where you stand. Entering the circle spawns `CHAR_Critter_Rat`. Leaving the rectangle logs `Sample triggers:`. You are already inside, so step out and back in. `.triggers off` disposes both. Admin only.

Live on the isolated test server, 2026-10-08, as the test player: the first sight did not fire; 7 m east logged only the circle leave; 9.2 m east logged only the rectangle leave; returning spawned `CHAR_Critter_Rat`; `.triggers off` and a second `off` disposed both. A teleport 4 m up was snapped back to the ground, so one floor above was not held live.

Status: source-checked, game-free tested (`AreaTriggerTests`, `SampleTriggerTests`), and the sample enter/leave/spawn path verified live. One floor above stays the game-free test. [modules.md](modules.md#triggers).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|

Throws the text from `AreaTriggerOptions.Refusal` when the volume or the actions are unusable. Off the main thread it throws `InvalidOperationException`. | `SampleTriggers.cs` (`.triggers on`); unit test `AreaTriggerTests` |
| `ctx.Triggers.OnEnter(UnitVec3, float, float, Action<AreaTriggerHit>)` / `OnLeave(UnitVec3, float, float, Action<AreaTriggerHit>)` → `AreaTrigger` | landed, not live-verified | Enter-only or leave-only circle. The last `float` is the full height, centered on the position. A null action throws. The sample uses `Watch`. | unit test `AreaTriggerTests` |

## Ai

Server boss AI. Client calls throw. Every mutation returns an `IDisposable` that restores exactly what
it changed and is tracked by the context, so unload restores it too. Contract, safety rules (the
Burst abort a shared-blob swap causes), defaults and persistence: [ai.md](ai.md). The mechanisms were
proven live by hand on Frostmaw; **this code has not run in the game**.

| `AiProfile` / `AiObservation` / `AiCastOptionGroup` / `AiCastOptionRow` / `AiAbilitySlot` / `AiPhaseScript` / `AiFactionInfo` / `AiCastHistoryEntry` | landed | Read-only results. `AiCastOptionRow.CooldownText` reads the runtime pair (`Min < 0` = exactly `Max`). `AiFactionInfo.SelfFriendly` is `(Friendly & Membership) != 0`. | unit test `AiCodecTests` (decoded from captured Frostmaw bytes) |
| `AiCastOptions` `Read(boss)` / `Edit(boss, abilityGroup, CastOptionEdit, priorityGroup?)` | landed, not live-verified | **Prefab-wide** row edit for every row that casts the ability group (short name, full name or GUID; never a row index). Dispose restores each field unless it was changed again since. | unit test `AiCodecTests` |
| `CastOptionEdit` / `CastAggroFilter` / `CastLineOfSight` / `CastAngle` | landed | `Weight`, `CooldownSeconds` (exact), `RangeMin`, `RangeMax`, `LineOfSight`, `AggroFilter`, `Angle`; enum values are the game's. `Validate()` refuses empty, negative and inverted edits. | unit test `AiCodecTests` |
| `AiConditions` `SetHealthGate(boss, abilityGroup, fraction, priorityGroup?)` | landed, not live-verified | Private condition copy with the boss-health gate at `fraction`, row repointed; the original blob is never written. Refused without a HealthFactor gate. Prefab-wide. | Frenzied variant (`HealthGate`) |
| `AiPhases` `Get(unit)` / `SetThreshold(unit, fraction, buff?)` | landed, not live-verified | Per-instance `Script_ApplyBuffUnderHealthThreshold` threshold (re-arms above it). Other phase scripts are reported, not edited. | Frenzied variant (`PhaseThreshold`) |
| `AiTrees` `CreateVariant(boss)` / `Apply(unit, TreeVariant, experimental)` / `Revert(unit)` | **experimental** (Sentinel verified live on one build) | Private tree copy on its own Prefab-tagged tree entity; the unit moves through `ExpectedTreeEntity`, the job's own abort path. Never repoints a shared tree blob. Refused before the unit runs a tree. The private tree reference is not saved: a saved variant unit reloads with both tree references null and aborted the server when a player came near (INC-20260924-02); Core's post-load tree repair writes both to the prefab tree. Revert or remove variant units before any save ([ai.md](ai.md#persistence)). | Sentinel variant; live: 0 casts vs vanilla 64 |

| `AiCastOptions.CreateVariant(boss, name, experimental: true)` / `Assign(unit, CastOptionsVariant)`; `CastOptionsVariant` `Read` / `Edit` / `SetHealthGate` / `Guid` | **experimental** | Per-instance copy of the CO prefab chain (a `LinkedEntityGroup` root is instantiated as a group and its remap verified), Prefab-tagged, registered with the content registry's clone rows (entity, asset, conversion state), adopted after a Core swap. Unverified live; see [ai.md](ai.md#per-instance-cast-options-experimental). | Frenzied variant |

A marker that did not land is a warning and `MarkerProblem`. Unload reverts and keeps the marker. | `samples/Progenitor.BossVariantsExample` |
| `AiAllySafety` (`ctx.Ai.AllySafety`) `Exempt(prefab)` / `Protect(prefab)` / `IsProtected(prefab)` / `Describe()` → `AllySafetyReport` (`AllySafetyEntry`, `AllySafetyState`) / `Enabled` | verified live | On by default on the server, for units spawned through the API only: every damaging hit slot whose condition has no team check (or no condition) is pointed at a private copy that first asks whether the caster (or its minion master) wears Core's API-spawn marker, and only then requires `NOT IsAlly(Target)`. So API-spawned units no longer damage allies, and vanilla units and bosses run the original condition unchanged. Environmental hazards, player abilities and hits that also heal are exempt by rule. `Exempt` restores the original while held; `Protect` guards a prefab the rules skip. `[Ai] AllySafeHits` turns the default off. Uninstall restores the originals. Verified live: unmarked Bishops still damage each other, API-spawned ones do not, and the sample’s Frenzied Ice Beam spares its group; summon coverage, save/restart and Core hot swap are not verified ([ai.md](ai.md#ally-safe-damaging-hits)). | `samples/Progenitor.BossVariantsExample` (Frenzied Ice Beam) |

## Access — experimental

Typed accessors for game members the interop stubs do not hand you directly, generated from a text
declaration file by `tools/accessgen`. Static class, not on `ctx`: the members are compile-time, so
there is nothing per-plugin to hold. `using Progenitor.Access;`. Declaration grammar, the plugin
workflow and the limits: [`modules.md`](modules.md#access).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |

| `access.txt` `access v1` / `assembly` / `type` / `field` / `method` | landed | The declaration file. `field T f : X` annotates the type; `field T f static` is an IL2CPP static; `method T M(A, B)` names one overload. | `Access/access.txt` |
| `Access.<Type>.<field><V>(instance)` / `(instance, value)` | landed | Get / set, `V : unmanaged`, through `il2cpp_field_get_value` / `_set_value`. No offset is ever written down. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access`) |
| `Access.<Type>.<field>Object(instance)` / `<field>Offset()` | landed | A reference-typed field as a raw object pointer; `il2cpp_field_get_offset` as a diagnostic. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs`: bare `.access` (and `.access field`), the reference-typed field as a raw pointer, and the runtime offset printed next to the dump's `0x100` |
| `Access.<Type>.<Method>()` / `<Method>Pointer()` | landed | The interop stub's `MethodBase` (a Harmony target) and the compiled function pointer (a native detour target). Not a call. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access method`): through **Core's** generated facade, `Access.DeathUtilities.Kill()` and `KillPointer()`; the reply says nothing is invoked |
| `Access.<Type>.Type` | landed | The interop stub `System.Type`. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access type`): the Sample's own facade and Core's, side by side |
| `Access.Reset()` / `Access.Describe()` | landed | Drop the per-generation resolution cache; one diagnostic line. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access`) |
| `AccessException` | landed | `Access: <Type>.<member> not found on this build; the address library/resilience report knows which build`. | `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access missing`): asks `AccessResolver` for a member that exists on no build and prints the sentence; a resolver that *answers* is reported as a bug, not a pass |
| `AccessMemberAttribute` | landed | Assembly-level row per declaration, read by `Resilience.MetadataScan` so declared members appear in the resilience report. | `Access/Access.Generated.cs` |

Generic instantiations, Burst job data, obfuscated names and unmodeled Unity engine internals are
not declarable; the address library covers those.

## Effects

Two surfaces on one facade. **Client borrow**: the server returns a disabled handle.
**Server-authored beams**: a real networked game entity every client draws, so the *client* returns a
disabled handle. `ctx.Effects` `Track`s both (`progenitor-effects-<guid>`). Presets and the raw-write
guards: [`effects.md`](effects.md).

| `HostKind` Overlay / RenderTexture | verified live | Overlay = UI; RT = world particles and sequences. | `SampleFx.cs` |
