| `PluginEffects.ServerBeam / Beams / ServerBeamLink / ServerBeamMark` | parked | Server. `BeamPreset`, `EffectVec3` endpoints, seconds; `Beams` adds count and spacing. Verified live (beams replicate and track their endpoints), and further beam work is **parked** by decision — the one item left open is endpoints following their entities for the link sequence. | `SampleBeam.cs` (`.beam`, `.beam link`, `.beam mark`) |
| `IServerBeamHandle` `Track` / `Blast` / `Dispose` / `OnDisposed` | parked | Main-thread dispose. `Enabled` false on the client. | `SampleBeam.cs` (`.beam`) |
| `Assets.Borrow/Host/Find/RemoveAll`, `ServerBeam.Spawn/Beams/Link/Mark/RemoveAll` | verified live | Static forms; pass `owner` + plugin GUID. Swept on unload. | `SampleFx.cs` (the game-free `Assets.*` path) |
| `AssetAcquisition.Acquire` / `ScanResidency` / `Preview` / `Find` / `RemoveAll` | experimental | Client. Owned mount / load / preview / release of one initially-unloaded catalog asset in Progenitor's `PGbrowser` namespace. Native calls run only when `AssetAcquisitionOptIn.Granted`; otherwise Acquire returns a refused handle and the loader is never asked to mount. Dispose unloads and unmounts only what this process mounted. Never `RTC`, never `ReleaseObjectAsync` of a game id. This cut admits the lantern identity only. Not verified live. [`assets-acquisition.md`](assets-acquisition.md). | unit test |
| `IAssetAcquisitionHandle` | experimental | One owned mount/load of a content-archive object. Dispose unloads and unmounts only what this handle acquired. Double-dispose is a no-op. Track on `PluginContext` so plugin unload releases it. | unit test |
| `AssetAcquisitionRequest` | experimental | Game-free acquire arguments (`ArchiveId`, `PathId`, `BudgetBytes`, `TimeoutMs`). Budget is checked before any mount (`RefuseBeforeMount`): unknown identities are `not-in-index` unless they are the lantern (or the G8 negative, which exceeds a lantern-sized budget). `Lantern()` is the factory this cut admits. | unit test |
| `AssetAcquisitionStatus` | experimental | Owned handle state: `Queued` / `Loading` / `Ready` / `Error` / `Refused`. Not the game's `LoadingStatus`. | unit test |
| `AssetAcquisitionOptIn` | experimental | Process gate for the first `ContentNamespace` / `ArchiveFileInterface` / `ContentLoadInterface` call. Default closed. Both Dev config `Acquisition.AllowCatalogLoad` and `PROGENITOR_CATALOG_ACQUIRE=1` must be set; neither alone is enough. | unit test |
| `AssetAcquisitionReasons` | experimental | Fixed refusal vocabulary for catalog acquisition: `not-in-index`, `budget-exceeded`, `missing-dependency`, `timeout`, `load-error`, `unsupported-class`, `namespace-rejected`, `mount-refused`, `icall-unresolved`, `opt-in-required`, `client-only`, `already-resident`, `stale-handle`, `wait-unload-unsupported`. | unit test |
| `AssetResidencyReport` | experimental | Read-only residency snapshot from `ScanResidency`. Does not mount and does not require the acquire opt-in. `AbortIdentity` is true when `NameCount` &gt; 0 or `RtcMentionsTarget` (a baseline scan already sees this name, or RTC holds the archive). | unit test |
| `AssetPreviewResult` | experimental | Preview outcome. `Unsupported` names a missing Effects seam when Host was refused. | unit test |
| `CatalogAssetIdentity` | experimental | Disk identity of the first initially-unloaded visual candidate (`Part_LanternGlow_PaleBlue01`), the `PGbrowser` namespace name, and parsers for `(archiveId, pathID)`. Source-checked against `catalog-inventory.json`; not verified live. | unit test |

**What a plugin can and cannot get today.** It can spawn a replicated beam between two points or two
entities, pick a preset, set a duration and a count, re-point it every tick, mark a single entity,
and dispose it (or have unload dispose it). It cannot author a *custom* sequence — the route is
clone-and-swap of a vanilla sequence's asset references, not a shipped `AssetBundle`, which is
stripped on this build — and it cannot hand the link sequence entities that carry their own
endpoints. **Wire note:** `Assets.RemoveAll` and `ServerBeam.RemoveAll` changed `void` → `int`, so a
plugin compiled against an earlier Core throws `MissingMethodException` and must be rebuilt; the beam
marker values also moved, and both sides must run the same Core.

## Camera

Client only; every member throws in a server context. Angles are degrees (yaw 0 = +Z, positive pitch
looks down). A shot is rewritten every frame until `Reset`, `SetMode`, another shot, another plugin
taking the camera, a client world change, or unload. Contract, timing, input handling and the live
acceptance procedure: [modules.md](modules.md#camera).

**Asset importing — under development and incomplete.** `ImportGlb`, `ImportPackage` and authored animation playback are experimental client-visual APIs, not a complete third-party armor, character or creature import pipeline. Scoped example verification does not establish general asset compatibility or production readiness. Gameplay binding, native-rig retargeting, multiplayer package delivery and full item lifecycle recovery remain incomplete. See [import limitations](player-animation-import.md) and [item management](item-removal.md).

Play starts at zero, optionally blends from the displayed pose, and requires positive finite speed. Seek clamps and preserves playback state; non-looping clips hold the endpoint. Stop restores authored rest transforms and clears the clip. Main thread only. No native-player retargeting, gameplay casting or networking. | [Animation import](player-animation-import.md); `GltfAnimationSamplerTests` |

Every preview ends on Dispose, Escape when it is topmost, damage, death, the character moving more than 1.5 m, a client world change, another shot taking the camera, and unload. [Camera preview](modules.md#camera-preview). | `SampleCamera.cs` (`.shot preview [hold]`); unit test `CameraPreviewHoldTests` |

| `CameraPreviewEndReason` / `CameraPreviewOptions` | landed | `None`, `Disposed`, `Timeout`, `HoldLimit`, `Escape`, `Damaged`, `Died`, `Moved`, `WorldChanged`, `CameraTaken`, `Replaced`, `Unloaded` (no `ended` call), `Failed`. | unit test `CameraPreviewHoldTests` |
| `ctx.Camera.Observe(Entity user, Entity target, float seconds = 8)` / `Observe(Entity user, Entity target, CameraObserveOptions options)` | landed; timed lease verified live, renewal not live-verified | Server streaming origin at an authorized target for one user, without moving the real character. The first overload still returns `IDisposable` (the object is a `CameraObservation`). `CameraObserveOptions` `Seconds` (more than 0, at most 10), `Hold`, `Ended(CameraObservationEndReason)`. [Camera preview](modules.md#camera-preview). | `SampleCamera.cs` (`.shot preview`); unit test `CameraPreviewHoldTests` |
| `PluginCamera.CameraObservation` `Active` / `IsHeld` / `EndReason` / `SecondsLeft` / `Renew(seconds)` / `Hold()` / `Dispose()` / `MaxRenewSeconds` / `LeaveDistanceMeters` | landed; not live-verified | A renewable lease that always has a deadline: `Renew` sets it at most `MaxRenewSeconds` (600) from now, `Hold` is `Renew(600)`. It ends on Dispose, expiry, disconnect, the character going away or moving more than 10 m, another writer of the streaming origin, a newer `Observe` for the user, and unload. A user has one streaming origin: a lease another holder (another plugin, or Core's container preview) already runs at the same target (within 0.5 m) is shared, each holder keeping its own deadline, and the origin is restored when the last holder leaves; a lease at another point makes `Observe` throw. Sharing is game-free tested (`ContainerPreviewTests`), not live-verified. The game saves the streaming origin with the world (live 2026-10-17), so around every world save a lease puts the original back until the save is written, keeps a record in the mod save while it runs, and after a load (or at the next lease start) an origin left at a recorded lease point is restored; game-free tested, not live-verified ([Camera preview](modules.md#camera-preview)). | `SampleCamera.cs`; unit tests `CameraPreviewHoldTests`, `ContainerPreviewTests` |
| `CameraObservationEndReason` / `CameraObserveOptions` | landed | `None`, `Disposed`, `Expired`, `Disconnected`, `LeftArea`, `Superseded`, `Replaced`, `Unloaded` (plugin or Core unload, or the server world gone; no `Ended` call), `Failed`, `CharacterGone` (the character the lease started with is gone, a respawn for example). | unit test `CameraPreviewHoldTests` |

## Input

Client only. Contract, frame pipeline, user-safety rules and the acceptance procedure:
[input.md](input.md). Leases write the game's `EntityInput` on the input controller and the local
character, then read back and patch the outgoing input commands; they never touch the OS cursor.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.Input.TryAcquire(InputLeaseOptions, out InputLease?)` → `InputAcquireResult` | landed | Take `InputChannels` (`Aim`, `Hover`, `Move`, `Buttons`, `All`), all or nothing. `Busy` when any is held and `Preempt` is false (nothing changes), from inside an `Ended` handler, or for 2 s after the release key; `Preempt` ends the overlapping leases first (`Preempted`). `NoLocalPlayer` without one client world, local character and input controller; `LayoutRefused` / `Faulted` when injection is disabled; `ServerSide` on a server. The lease is tracked for this plugin's unload. | `SampleInput.cs`; unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLeaseOptions(channels, UserPolicy user = Yield, bool preempt = false, string? reason = null)` / `UserPolicy` `Yield` / `Override` | landed | `Yield`: movement keys pause `Move`, mouse movement or buttons pause `Aim`/`Hover`, ability keys (Q E R C T F Space) and mouse buttons pause `Buttons` (keys typed into chat or a text field do not count), until 1.5 s (`InputLease.YieldIdleSeconds`) without activity. Default key bindings only. `Override` keeps writing and is logged. | unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLease.AimAt(UnitVec3)` / `AimAt(Entity clientUnit)` / `ClearAim()` / `TargetLost` | landed | Aim at a floor point (x, character y, z) or a client-world unit re-resolved every frame (last point kept when it is gone). Writes `AimPosition`, `AimPositionPlane`, `AimDirection`, `AimLength` and the command's `RotationInput`. | `SampleInput.cs`; unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLease.Hover(Entity clientUnit)` / `ClearHover()` | landed | Hovered unit for targeted spells, feeding, interactions: `HoveredEntity` + the unit's `NetworkId`. Throws for an entity with no `NetworkId`; a unit that disappears is un-hovered. | `SampleInput.cs`; unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLease.Move(float x, float z)` / `Move(float2)` / `Stop()` | landed | World walk direction until changed; longer than 1 is normalized. A lease that also holds `Aim` with no aim set faces the walk. | `SampleInput.cs`; unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLease.Press(SyncedButtons, float? seconds)` / `Release` / `Tap` | landed | `SyncedButtons` mirrors `SyncedButtonInputAction` bits. Press/release edges go on the first new command of a frame only; a lease that ends or pauses sends the release edge (not after a fault). `Tap` presses on the next new command and releases on the next frame's; a tap asked for while the buttons are paused is dropped. | `SampleInput.cs`; unit test `InputTests` |
| `InputLease.OnFrame` / `Ended` / `EndReason` / `Active` / `Suspended` / `SuspendedChannels` / `LastSent` / `Dispose()` | landed | `OnFrame` runs once per frame before the write (steer there). `InputLeaseEnd`: `Released`, `PluginUnloaded`, `CoreUnloaded`, `WorldChanged`, `Disconnected`, `CharacterChanged`, `Preempted`, `UserPanic`, `LayoutRefused`, `Faulted`. Mutators are no-ops after the end. `LastSent` is an `InputReadback` of the newest outgoing command, with `SourceMatched` saying whether the game had copied the intent before the patch. | unit test `InputTests` |
| `ctx.Input.Status` (`InputStatus`) / `HeldChannels` / `HolderOf(channel)` | landed | Layout, fault, local player, `CommandsFlowing` (new commands in the last second while a lease exists; false on an unfocused normal client), `Focused`, leases, held and suspended channels, `LastSent`, `Issue`. | `SampleInput.cs` (`.input status`) |
| `InputLeases.TryAcquire(holder, options, out lease)` / `InputLeases.Status` | landed | The same lease for a holder with no plugin context (the Dev `bot` op, part of the [experimental headless bots](../tools/bots/README.md)); not tracked for any unload. Plugins use `ctx.Input`. | Dev `bot` op |

## Ui

Adding controls to a mod: start with the [UI style guide](ui-style.md) (where the UI goes, which control fits, layout, wording and color) and the controls sample it points to. The rows below are the reference.

The [Mods menu](mods-menu.md) is the player entry point. 

| New contract | Scope |
|---|---|
| `PluginMenu` / `ctx.Menu` | Tracked menu and explicit server-edit registration. `OpenPage(title)` → `bool` opens the Mods menu at this plugin's custom page (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified): in a world through the game's menu on its MODS tab, so the page shows on a later frame; at the title screen, or with the menu already open, at once. Use it from a keybind or button; `ModsMenu.OpenPage` draws the page without opening the game's menu, which leaves an in-world player on the spellbook page. False, opening nothing, for an unknown title and on the server. `Help(path)` → `IDisposable` names the mod's HTML help file relative to its own folder (or an embedded resource name) for the page's **Help** button (game-free tested, not live-verified; [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#help-button)). |
| `ModsMenu` / `ModMenuRegistration` | Mod directory, grouped settings and owned custom pages. |
| `ModMenuRegistration.Header(Action<IUiPanel>)` | SETTINGS1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. The mod's own header content on its page, drawn under the framework-owned status line in place of the default title and `by <author> · <one-liner>`. The builder receives a row-only panel: it can add rows and inline clusters, and its `Show` / `Hide` / `Dispose` / `Text` / `Visible` / `Footer` / `CaptureMode` writes are ignored, so it cannot change the status line or the page. An unclosed inline cluster is closed and the alignment restored after it; a builder that throws has its rows removed and the default is drawn. Once per registration. [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md). |
| `ServerSettings` / `ServerSettingResult` | Opt-in server-validated edits with conflict checks, confirmation and timeout. Authorized by the server admin list or adminauth, 10 requests per 60 s per sender, audited to `BepInEx/progenitor-settings.log` ([settings rows](mods-menu.md#settings-rows)). A text value is held to the setting's declared limit as the server declares it (`MaxLength`, else 256 for a field and 4,096 for a text area), at most 2,048 characters and 2,048 UTF-8 bytes; the client refuses longer before sending with the server's wording (TEXTLIMIT1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified; [limits table](mod-settings.md#server-wide-text-limits-textlimit1)). |
| `PluginDialogs` / `ctx.Dialogs` | DIALOGS1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. `Confirm(title, body, yes, onYes, no = "Cancel", onNo = null, danger = false, showModVersion = null)`, `Notice(title, body, ok = "OK", onClose = null, showModVersion = null)`, `Choose(title, body, options, Action<int> onPick, cancel = "Cancel", onCancel = null, showModVersion = null)`, `Prompt(title, body, initial, Action<string> onSubmit, placeholder = "", Func<string, string?> validate = null, submit = "OK", maxLength = 0, cancel = "Cancel", onCancel = null, showModVersion = null)`, each → `IDisposable` (closes, or unqueues, without a callback); `ShowModVersion` (default `true`). Enter never answers a `danger: true` Confirm; Choose takes 1–10 options; a Prompt validator's non-null result (a blank one shows `Not accepted.`) refuses. Layout, keys, queue, footer and unload rules: [ui.md](ui.md#dialogs). |
| `DialogFact` / `DialogFactTone` and the facts overloads | DIALOGS2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. `ctx.Dialogs.Confirm(title, IReadOnlyList<DialogFact> facts, yes, onYes, note = "", no = "Cancel", onNo = null, danger = false, showModVersion = null)` and `Notice(title, IReadOnlyList<DialogFact> facts, note = "", ok = "OK", onClose = null, showModVersion = null)` draw label/value rows in place of the body: `new DialogFact(label, value, tone = DialogFactTone.Normal)`; tones `Normal` / `Ok` / `Warn` / `Danger` color the value. Labels are muted and left-aligned in a column as wide as the widest label (at most 40 % of the block); values are bright, left-aligned and wrap to three lines. A fact with an empty value is left out; an empty label continues the fact above. `note` is one short muted sentence under the facts. The readback (`mods_ui status` `dialog.facts`) lists `{label, value, tone}`. Example: `SampleDialogs.Facts` (the Sample's **Dialogs → Facts dialog**). Layout: [ui.md](ui.md#facts). |
| `ctx.Dialogs.Form`; `DialogFormOptions`, `DialogCheck`, `DialogFormResult` | FORMDIALOG1, source-checked, built and game-free tested, not live-verified. `Form(string title, DialogFormOptions options, Action<DialogFormResult> onSave)` → `IDisposable`: one dialog with a captioned one-line field, then one to ten boxes (the game's Options checkbox) under a heading, then Cancel and Save. `DialogFormOptions` (all settable, all defaulted): `Caption`, `Initial`, `Placeholder`, `MaxLength`, `Heading`, `Checks` (`IReadOnlyList<DialogCheck>`), `RequireOne`, `NoneHint` (default `Turn on at least one.`), `Validate` (`Func<DialogFormResult, string?>`: an error under the field, or null), `Save` (`"Save"`), `Cancel` (`"Cancel"`), `OnCancel`, `ShowModVersion`. `DialogCheck` is a sealed class with init properties `Label`, `On`, an optional `Key` and an optional `Color` (`#RRGGBB`, the kit's hex form; empty by default: the normal label color; a color too dark for the dialog is lifted toward white until it reads 4.5:1). `DialogFormResult`: `Text`, `Count`, `IsOn(int index)`, `IsOn(string key)` (the box's `Key`, else its label, ignoring case), `States`. No box, more than ten, or a null `options` throws `ArgumentException` / `ArgumentNullException` (on the server too). A click anywhere on a box's line flips it; with `RequireOne` Save is grayed and the hint shows while no box is checked. Readback: `mods_ui status` `dialog.checks [{index, row, label, on, color}]`, `checksOk`, `checksHint`. Example: the Controls sample's **Form** dialog. Layout and keys: [ui.md](ui.md#form-dialog-formdialog1). |
| `UiWidgets.OrderedList(this IUiPanel, key, IEnumerable<UiListItem> items, UiOrderedListOptions? options = null)` → `IUiOrderedList`; `UiListItem`, `UiListReorder`, `UiOrderedListOptions` | P7, experimental: source-checked and game-free tested; live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller). Drag by the grip, up and down buttons, a drawer per row, remove; the list changes itself, then raises `OnReorder(UiListReorder {Id, From, To, Order})` / `OnRemove(UiListItem)` / `OnExpand(item, open)`. `Items`, `Capacity`, `Overflow`, `SetItems`, `Move`, `Remove`, `SetExpanded`, `IsExpanded`, `Describe()`. Items: `Id`, `Title`, `Note`, `Detail`, `Badge`, `Icon` / `ItemPrefab`, `Removable`, `Tooltip`; P7b `UiListItem.Term` (the caption explains a `UiTerms` glossary term in place of `Tooltip`; the marker follows the item). Rules: [ui.md](ui.md#ordered-list). Example: `ControlsSample/WidgetsDemo.cs` (Widgets page, Lists tab; `WidgetsDemo.Help.cs` for `Term`); unit tests `UiWidgetsTests`, `UiTermsTests`. |
| `UiWidgets.Picker(this IUiPanel, key, IEnumerable<UiPickerEntry> entries, UiPickerOptions? options = null)` → `IUiPicker`; `UiPickerEntry`, `UiPickerOptions` | P7, experimental: source-checked and game-free tested; live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller). Search with autofill (`Query`, `Matches`, `Suggestion`, `AcceptSuggestion()`), sections, icons (`Icon` / `ItemPrefab`), grayed entries with `DisabledReason` (shown in the note and the tooltip), single or `Multi` selection (`Selected`, `Select`, `ClearSelection`, `OnPicked`), `SetEntries`, `Describe()`. P7b `IUiPicker.SelectAllMatches()` → `int`: multi mode selects every pickable match (the status line's **Select all** link; the status line also counts the selection and offers **Clear**). Matching and ranking: [ui.md](ui.md#picker). Example: `WidgetsDemo.cs` (Input tab); unit tests `UiWidgetsTests`, `UiPickerMultiTests`. |
| `UiWidgets.Tabs(this IUiPanel, key, IReadOnlyList<string> captions, int selected = 0)` → `IUiTabs`; `UiBadge` | P7, experimental: source-checked and game-free tested; live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller). `Captions`, `Selected`, `Add(tab, handle)` (shown only on its tab), `SetBadge(tab, count)` / `SetBadge(tab, text, LabelTone)`, `SetTooltip`, `OnChanged`, `Describe()`; P7b `IUiTabs.SetTerm(int tab, string term)` (the caption explains a `UiTerms` glossary term; empty removes it). `UiBadge.Text(count)`: empty for 0, `99+` above 99. Example: `WidgetsDemo.cs` (the tab row; `WidgetsDemo.Help.cs` for `SetTerm`); unit tests `UiWidgetsTests`, `UiTermsTests`. |
| `UiWidgets.Tooltip(this IUiHandle handle, string text, string title = "")` → `IDisposable` | P7, experimental: source-checked; live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller). The kit tooltip box beside any kit control while the pointer is over it; owner-tagged, released with the plugin. Placement rules: [ui.md](ui.md#tooltips). Example: `WidgetsDemo.cs` (**Reset list**, **Pick in world**). |
| `UiWidgets.CodeArea(this IUiPanel, string text, string initial = "", int lines = 10)` → `IUiCodeArea`; `UiTextSpan`, `UiLineError` | P7, experimental: source-checked and game-free tested; live-verified on the test client 2026-10-14 through the bridge at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, plus a 2x supersample capture (layout only); not verified: native 4K pixel density, the feel of a real-mouse drag, and controller hardware (navigation was driven through the bridge's virtual pad, not a physical controller). Line numbers, caller colors (`SetHighlighter(Func<string, IReadOnlyList<UiTextSpan>>)`, `SetSpans`; `UiTextSpan(start, length, "#RRGGBB")`), line errors (`SetErrors(IReadOnlyList<UiLineError>)`, `Errors`; a red marker, a band and a summary row), `Value`, `OnChanged`, `LineCount`, `Describe()`. | `UiDataTableOptions.FillHeight`; `UiTableRow.BuildingPrefab` | HUB1, experimental: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified | `FillHeight`: the table's body uses as many rows (4 to 30) as fit its panel's tallest viewport with every other row on it, so the window grows to its cap and the table scrolls inside it; re-read every 10 frames. `BuildingPrefab`: a castle building's prefab id whose own blueprint icon (the one its build-menu cell shows) the row draws. **Limitation:** the sprite is found among the UI assets the game has loaded (the build menu need not be open), but the game streams a blueprint's icon in for its build menu, so a building whose icon the game has not loaded since the world was joined keeps an aligned blank. A waiting row redraws as soon as the icon is learned; scans run only while a row or lookup waits, backing off from 30 to 1800 frames after misses. A drawn row's building icon is checked every 60 frames and drawn again when its sprite was unloaded. The readback gives each slot's `buildingPrefab` and `iconSource` (`blueprint`, `none`). It is looked up by the blueprint's icon asset id (`ManagedBlueprintData.Icon`) among the UI assets the game has loaded, so the build menu need not be open. Build-menu cells are not read (a cell can show a stale sprite while its own streams in); a blueprint that names no icon asset has no icon. **Limitation:** the kit loads no game asset itself; null while the game has not loaded that icon since the world was joined (it streams blueprint icons in for its build menu); ask again later. A miss puts the building on a waiting list (left after about 2 minutes, 7200 frames, without a request) and may scan, at most once per 30 to 1800 frames (backing off after misses); a scan looks for at most 16 buildings with at most 512 game lookups, resuming where the last stopped. Client, main thread; null on the server. `BuildingIconSource`: where the cached icon came from, `"blueprint"` or `"none"` (reads the cache only; call `BuildingIcon` first). | `ControlsSample/GalleryHub.cs`; unit test `UiHubControlsTests` |

| `PluginSettings` / `ctx.Settings` / `SettingsPageBuilder` | SETTINGS1 stage 2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. `ctx.Settings.Page(page => page.Section(title).Toggle(entry, …).Number(entry, min, max, step, units, …).Slider(…).Dropdown(entry, choices, labels, …).Radio(…).MultiSelect(…).Text(entry, multiline, lines, …).MaxLength(characters).Color(entry, alpha, …).Setting(entry, …).Keybind(id, …).Button(caption, action, confirm, …, label).Info(label, value, refreshSeconds, …).Divider())`. Every setting method takes the common options `label`, `help`, `showIf`, `enableIf`, `needsRestart`, `needsServerPart`, `fallback`, `scope` (`SettingScope`). The builder runs once at the call; an entry it places appears only there (it wins over `.Settings(title, …)` groups and the automatic sections). A builder that throws registers nothing; a second page for the same plugin throws `ArgumentException`. `MaxLength(characters)` (TEXTAREA1) caps the `Text` row added last: the field stops typing and paste there and a longer write is refused; without it a text field holds **256** characters and a text area **4,096** (framework defaults; a stored value that is already longer is shown whole). On a server-wide row (`scope: Server`) the server enforces the same limit from its own copy of the page, capped at **2,048** characters and **2,048** UTF-8 bytes (the admin edit packet; ConfigSync's 4,096 escaped bytes then always fit), and refuses longer with `The value is longer than N characters (L); shorten it to N.`, and a value BepInEx's cfg escape would change (a backslash before `n`, `t` and the like) (TEXTLIMIT1, [server-wide limits](mod-settings.md#server-wide-text-limits-textlimit1)). Every text area has an expand button that opens the whole value in a large editor (TEXTEXPAND1, [expanded editor](mod-settings.md#the-expanded-editor-textexpand1)). A typed text value committed from the page (a Personal text field or text area, an admin's Apply or Done, and the server's own check) loses its leading and trailing spaces before the length check, because BepInEx's cfg read drops them; Tab and line breaks are kept, and the field then shows the stored value (TEXTUNDO1 polish, [commit trim](mod-settings.md#text-settings-limits-and-plain-text); `SettingsRow.TryWrite` and a plugin's own `ConfigEntry` writes are not trimmed). It throws `InvalidOperationException` after a non-text row and `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` below 1. Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. Contracts, drawing, limits and readback: [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#the-settings-page-builder-stage-2), [text limits](mod-settings.md#text-settings-limits-and-plain-text). |
| `SettingsPageBuilder.DateTime(ConfigEntry<string> entry, Func<bool>? seconds = null, Func<TimeZoneInfo>? zone = null, label, help, showIf, enableIf, needsRestart, needsServerPart, fallback, scope)` | JOINFLOW1, source-checked and game-free tested (`DateTimePickerTests`), not live-verified. A date-time row for a string entry holding ISO 8601 UTC (empty for none), drawn with `IUiPanel.DateTimeField`. `zone` is read when the row is drawn and when the picker opens (without it: the player's own zone on a personal row, UTC on a server-wide one); `seconds` is read when the picker opens (true adds the seconds stepper). Done on a personal row commits the ISO value through the page's validated write; on a synced `scope: Server` row an administrator's Done sends it with `ServerSettings.Request` (the Apply row's waiting, saved and refusal notes), and everyone else reads it as `Admin only · <wall-clock value>`. Readback `control: datetime`, `iso`, `display`, `zone`, `seconds` ([mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#the-settings-page-builder-stage-2)). |


Client HUD clone. **Not** a `ctx` property: call `Progenitor.Ui.Ui` statically and pass `owner` +
`pluginId`. The server returns disabled handles. Click handlers go through a Bootstrap-owned slot, so
a Ui change can owe a process restart rather than a Core swap. Full API: [`ui.md`](ui.md).

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Ui.Panel(title, owner = null, pluginId = null)` → `IUiPanel` | landed | One overlay canvas per panel, destroyed with it. Never null; check `Enabled`. The overlay copies a vanilla `CanvasScaler`, so a panel is measured in the game's design space and not in device pixels (verified live, evidence 165 / 173). | `SampleUi.cs` (`.ui demo` / F9), `SampleLogPanel.cs` (Ctrl+F9) |
| `IUiPanel` `Button` / `Toggle(text, initial)` / `Label` / `Slider(text, min, max, initial)` / `Input(text, initial)` / `Show` / `Hide` | landed | Clones vanilla widgets, with a Unity fallback when the catalog is empty. `Slider` has **no** default arguments. **You do not size or place a panel** — see the note under this table. | `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleLogPanel.cs`; `Slider` / `Input` **none** |
| `IUiHandle` `Text` / `Visible` / `Enabled` / `IsDisposed` / `State` / `PluginId` / `RootName` | landed | `UiHandleState` is `Disabled` / `Built` / `Shown` / `Disposed`. | `SampleUi.cs`, `SampleLogPanel.cs` |
| `IUiPanel.CaptureMode` → `UiCaptureMode` | landed (FUI19) | Per panel, and it beats both defaults. Settable on an open panel; it applies at once. | **none** |
| `IUiPanel.Footer` → `string` (get/set) | landed — source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified | One small, muted line centered at the bottom of the panel, below the last row, in a band the scrolling viewport is inset above, so it never pushes a button out of view. Empty draws nothing and is the default for a plugin's panel; every prompt Progenitor itself shows starts with `Ui.FrameworkFooter`. Opt in with `panel.Footer = Ui.FrameworkFooter`, or show your own version the same way (`panel.Footer = "MyMod " + MyPluginInfo.PLUGIN_VERSION`). Settable at any time; trimmed. See [ui.md](ui.md#version-footer). | **none** |
| `Ui.FrameworkVersion` / `Ui.FrameworkFooter` → `string` | landed — source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified | The running Core's version per [versioning.md](versioning.md) — `2026.9.58` for a release, `2026.9.58.3` for an unmerged build — read from the Core assembly's informational version with the `+<commit>` metadata removed; `FrameworkFooter` is `Progenitor <version>`. | **none** |
| `IUiPanel` `Dropdown(text, values, value, labels = null)` → `IUiDropdown` (OPTIONHOVER1 overload `Dropdown(text, values, value, labels, descriptions)`: each option's short explanation; a settings page's description column shows the hovered option's, see [option descriptions](mod-settings.md#option-descriptions), and anywhere else (a custom page, a floating window) a box beside the open list shows the highlighted option's (DROPHOVER1, 2026-10-11). The open list's highlight follows the pointer and a controller, drawn as a lit fill on that option; before, the highlight was kept but not drawn and the option buttons' tint could not show. Game-free tested in `UiControlPrimitiveTests`; live-verified 2026-10-11 on the controls sample's page with the pointer over an option) / `Number(text, value, min, max, step = 1, decimals = 0, units = "")` → `IUiNumber` / `SliderField(text, min, max, step, value)` → `IUiSliderField` / `ColorField(text, hex, alpha = false)` → `IUiColorField` / `KeyField(text, pluginId, bindId)` → `IUiKeyField` | landed (SETTINGS1) — source-checked and game-free tested, **not live-verified** | The settings kit. `Dropdown` on the settings grid is the game's dropdown (DROPDOWN1, 2026-10-06; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified): a click on the field opens the list of options under it with the current one marked and writes nothing; picking an option closes the list and commits once; Escape, a click outside the list or picking the current value closes it with no commit. Off the grid it is drawn as a selector (`<` value `>`): its arrows commit the previous/next value and a click on the value opens the same list. The value has its own area between the two arrows (each arrow 28 px plus a 6 px gap) and never draws under them: a caption wider than its area shrinks from 16 to 11 and then ends in an ellipsis; the dropdown bar and its list options fit the same way (2026-10-09, user report `< ilobal (Automatic >`). Readback `fit {text, area, control, prev, next, fontSize, truncated, preferredWidth, areaUnderArrow}`. A value outside the list is kept and shown `(not in list)`. `Number` is a field with `-`/`+` steppers: typed text commits on Enter/focus loss, clamped and snapped to `step`; a stepper click commits one step. `SliderField` is a slider plus an exact field: a drag previews and commits once the slider has rested 0.35 s (Unity reports no release, so a pause longer than that mid-drag commits the intermediate value; a drag the panel is disposed on inside the window is committed, not dropped), a typed value commits on Enter/focus loss. `ColorField` is a swatch plus a `#RRGGBB` / `#RRGGBBAA` field (the swatch repaints on the next page build). `KeyField` captures a chord for a bind registered with `ctx.Keybinds`; Escape cancels. Each `OnChanged` fires on a commit only, never for a programmatic `Value` set. The mod page's settings sections use these ([mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md)). | **none** |

A plain-panel field has no length limit unless one is set (settings rows get the [settings defaults](mod-settings.md#text-settings-limits-and-plain-text)); a text area scrolls to keep the caret's line in view, and three lines a notch with the mouse wheel over it until its text ends (TEXTEXPAND1), and a single-line field clips text past its right edge. Number, slider and color boxes filter what can be typed (digits and one leading sign, a point when the number has decimals; `#` and hex digits). Every framework field has Ctrl+Z undo and Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+Shift+Z redo, per field, word-sized steps, at most 100 steps and 65,536 characters, cleared on focus loss and on an outside change of the value; while a framework field has focus no `ctx.Keybinds` bind fires on a key it takes (printable keys, Ctrl+letter, editing keys; F10 and mouse buttons still do); a read-only field ignores undo and draws no caret (TEXTUNDO1, source-checked and game-free tested in `TextUndoTests`, not live-verified; [undo and redo](ui.md#undo-and-redo-textundo1)). `Radio` shows every option as a radio list (RADIO1, 2026-10-06): one option per line with a radio circle (filled and gold for the chosen option), the chosen line bright and the others dimmed; the overload `Radio(text, values, value, labels, descriptions)` adds a one-line muted explanation under each option. A click on a line chooses it and commits once (never a toggle); a click beside the lines does nothing. Readback `radio {current, currentLabel, options [{value, label, description, selected}]}`; bridge `mods_ui radio_choose {label, value}` (built and game-free tested, not live-verified). `MultiSelect` is a vertical list of check boxes; each click flips one option and commits the whole selection (`OnChanged(Action<IReadOnlyList<string>>)`). `Info` re-reads its function every `refreshSeconds` (clamped to 0.25–60; 0 reads once) while visible, logs a throwing function once and keeps the last value, and stops for good when disposed. `Divider` is a thin rule. `ConfirmButton` asks `question` in the kit dialog before its `OnClick` handler runs; the dialog closes with the button (panel disposed, page rebuilt, plugin unloaded) and a later Yes runs nothing; on an `IUiPanel` that is not a Progenitor panel it throws `NotSupportedException`. Dock replaces `Ui.TopPanel`'s and a wide game window's centered placement while docked; `UiDock.None` undocks. **What the panel lists**, in order: every registered keybind (with a press-a-key rebind button); then
every **non-synced** config entry of every plugin it knows about, grouped by plugin — sources are the
chainloader sweep plus every plugin that has touched `ctx.Keybinds`. Synced entries are shown
read-only and say `(set by the server)`; another mod's swept rows are read-only unless
`[Settings] EditOtherModsSettings` is set. Then the **Kept versions** group (when the version store keeps a copy), then Close. Binds are
polled once per `Plugin.Frame` from `UnityEngine.InputSystem.Keyboard.current` and suppressed by a
focused text field (a mouse-button bind excepted: a mouse button types nothing, so Mouse Back still navigates back while a field has focus), by a focused or unreadable chat box, and by an input-capturing mod panel.

**A press is derived, not read (evidence 161).** `ButtonControl.wasPressedThisFrame` is
`pressedStepCount == updateStepCount` — true only during the input update step that processed the
press — while the pump samples once per rendered frame, so an update step that advanced twice
between two samples hid the press entirely and every mod keybind needed two presses. Core derives
the edge from a held state it owns (the engine's flag is trusted when set; otherwise a key seen up
on the *immediately preceding* sample and down now counts as a press). None of that is public
surface — `KeyEdgeTracker` and `IKeyInputSource` are internal — but it is the reason a bind now
fires once, and `UnityKeyInputSource` logs once, with `InputSystem.settings.updateMode`, whenever a
press had to be recovered that way. **Do not write your own detector against the raw flag.**

**Placement and sizing are the framework's, not yours.** There is no size, position or layout
argument anywhere on `IUiPanel`, and `PanelLayout`, `PanelFlow` and `PanelSpot` are internal —
quotable as facts, not referenceable. What a mod author can rely on:

- **A panel sizes itself to its rows**, height only; the width is a constant. Rows sit in a clipped
  viewport under a scroll rect, so a panel with more rows than fit **scrolls** rather than drawing
  over the world, and the frame is capped at 80% of the canvas height. A panel that fits shows no
  scrollbar; one that does not, does.
- **Every row measures its own caption** — label, toggle, slider, input and button alike. Which
  control a row carries has nothing to do with how long the text on it is, and the framework does
  not get to decide how long somebody else's config description is. A pathological label truncates
  with an ellipsis instead of painting over the rows beneath it.
- **Shown panels tile into columns** (two on a 1920-wide design space), centered as a group and
  ordered so the longest-open panel keeps the leftmost column. Only a surplus beyond the column
  count cascades.
- **The stability guarantee is narrow and deliberate: a re-layout caused by *using* a panel never
  moves anything.** Rewriting a row's text under the player's own click recomputes the same
  position by construction. **`Show` and `Hide` re-flow every shown panel**, so opening or closing
  one *does* move the others — two side-by-side panels become one centered panel when one closes.
  The older promise that "a panel never moves while it is shown" was given up in FUI21, because
  with panels tiled it would leave a survivor off-center beside an empty column. Nothing is
  draggable, so an overlap the player cannot resolve costs them the panel.

Landed status: sizing, clipping, scrolling and the scaler copy are **verified live**; the tiling,
the no-scrollbar-when-it-fits case and the per-row measurement on control rows are **landed with a
live check owed** (evidence 173, 174).

**Escape closes panels and nothing else.** While capture is live, Bootstrap dispatches Escape to
Core, which hides **every shown Progenitor panel across all plugins** — it does not dispose them,
does not toggle, and never reaches a plugin key handler. It also cancels an in-flight rebind. The
vanilla system menu no longer opens underneath, but that is done by the input mask (the `Back` and
`Menu_Close` actions are blocked in every mask scope, including the close-key-only scope, which is
why Escape closes a `WhenHovered` panel from anywhere on screen), **not** by the key read. One
consequence worth knowing: if the `RotateCameraBlocker.GetConsumedInputs` detour cannot be resolved
for the build, the context still registers but the mask is not applied — and then Escape reaches the
vanilla menu again. One log line says which of the two happened. Landed (FUI16, FUI20); **live check
owed** — the defect was seen in a screenshot, the fix has not been screenshotted since.

| `IPatchApplier` | experimental | The HarmonyLib seam. Core supplies `HarmonyPatchApplier`; tests supply a fake. | unit test |
| `CorePatchFallbackPolicy.Decide(slotShapeFits, bootstrapPresent)` | landed | Job FHARM7: the one rule for Core's own fallback patches. Slot-shaped → the pool; not slot-shaped and Bootstrap present → refused; Bootstrap absent → direct, with one warning naming the pin and the generation. | unit test: `CorePatchFallbackTests.cs` (`With_Bootstrap_present_a_slot_shaped_site_routes_through_the_pool_and_the_others_are_refused`); Core: `Events/EventHooks.cs:255` |
| `UnsafeHarmonyUse.Note/RefusesSwap/Describe` | landed | The `ctx.UnsafeHarmony` register: once-per-plugin note, the reloader's swap refusal, `unsafe-harmony: …` status line. | unit test |

`ctx.UnsafeHarmony` = every patch through it pins the plugin and the Core generation for the life of
the process, so taking it costs this plugin hot reload. Everything else: `ctx.Patches` (tagged,
swept, slot-routed), a Bootstrap slot for a process-lifetime site, or do not patch.

## God mode

Server-only. `Add` refuses in a client process before it patches anything.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctx.GodMode.Add(entity)` → `bool` / `.Remove` / `.IsGuarded` / `.RemoveAll()` | experimental | Blocks `DeathUtilities::Kill` and both `StatChangeUtility::KillEntity` overloads — the three kill paths no component guards. **Check the return**: `Add` is `false` unless all three prefixes are live. Tagged with the plugin; dropped on unload. | `SampleGodModeDemo.cs` (`.godmode on\|off\|status`) |
| `GodMode.Add(entity, owner = null)` (static) | experimental | Same, untagged. Only for a caller that owns every removal path itself. | `SampleGodModeDemo.cs` (`.godmode static`): passes `ctx.Owner`, and the reply says why a private owner token is the shape that leaves an unkillable character behind |
| `GodModeGuard.ShouldBlock(key, site, out logLine)` | experimental | The prefix body, game-free. Blocked kills are logged once per entity. | unit test |
| `GodModeGuard.SweepStale()` | experimental | Drops every guard stamped in an older [world epoch](glossary.md#world-epoch). `IsGuarded` / `ShouldBlock` / `Count` also drop a stale entry lazily. | `SampleGodModeDemo.cs` (`.godmode sweep`): runs before the character check; the reply says zero is the normal answer and when the whole-set pass earns its place |
| `GodMode.DescribePins()` | experimental | `godmode: guarded=1 patched=3`. | `SampleGodModeDemo.cs` (`.godmode status`) |

## Native

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AddressLibrary.TryResolve(id, out address)` | verified live | Image base plus relative address. A missing table or id returns false, never throws. | unit test: `AddressLibraryTests.cs` (`TryResolve_adds_the_offset_to_the_attached_module_base`); Core: `Native/DetourSite.cs:26` |
| `DetourSite.Resolve(site, id, prefixFallback)` | verified live | Table first; the prefix scan only when there is **no** table for this build. | unit test: `AddressLibraryTests.cs` (`Resolve_uses_the_fake_table_before_the_prefix_fallback`); Core: `Patches/PatchSites.cs` |
| `Il2CppNative.MethodPointerByPrefix(type, name)` | verified live | Harmony cannot patch by-ref-struct IL2CPP originals, and this refuses an overloaded name. | Core: `Access/AccessResolver.cs` (behind every generated `<Method>Pointer()`), reached by `SampleAccessDemo.cs` (`.access method`) |
| `HarmonySlotHandoff.BindPlugin/TryBindPlugin/ClearPlugin` | verified live | A plugin fills a Bootstrap slot and never owns the trampoline. A second bind by a different owner is refused. Exception: on a client Core holds the `GameHelper.LaunchGame` slot (`GcBeforeLaunch`), and a plugin bind of that site is chained behind Core's dispatcher under the same one-owner rule (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified). | unit test: `HarmonySlotHandoffTests.cs` (`Slot_install_clear_reinstall`); Dev: `Ops/ChatObserve.cs:159`, `Ops/ExtraInstanceAuth.cs:230` |

## Hosted game (not plugin API)

Client-only. A Harmony prefix on `StunProcess.Start` stages Doorstop and BepInEx into the bundled
`VRising_Server` so a private game loads the same mods as a dedicated server. Consumer plugins do not
call this — Core installs it on the client process — and server-side code never branches on whether
the server is dedicated or launched by the client. `HostedServerLauncher.Enabled` / `DryRun` /
`Describe` and `HostedServerPluginsMode` are **verified live**, and the switches
(`Hosted.Enabled`, default true; `Hosted.DryRun`) are operator config, not plugin config.

## Update resilience and the compatibility rewriter

Not a facade — an operator- and author-facing report. There is **no** bridge op for it: it is one log
block per process start, headed `Resilience:`, so `grep Resilience:` in `LogOutput.log` (or the `logs`
op with `filter: "Resilience"`) is how a plugin author reads it.

| Member | Status | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| The `Resilience:` startup block | landed | `build`, `baseline`, `interop`, `addresslib`, `members`, `rewrite`, and a closing `note report only - nothing was blocked`. Three headlines: `game build unchanged`, `game build CHANGED` (the build, the bytes, the interop set, the address table or a previously binding reference moved), and `references DO NOT bind (game build unchanged)` for breakage with no game change. Core growing or shrinking its own reference set is a `members`-row detail (`895 references (+5 since baseline), all bind`) with a `core` row naming the new Core version — never a headline. | unit test: `ResilienceTests.cs` (`UnchangedReportIsTheOneLineBlock`); Core: `Resilience/ResilienceReport.cs` |
| `pwsh tools/resilience-report.ps1` | landed | The same checks offline against a game folder. Exit `0` unchanged, `2` changed but nothing of ours breaks, `1` breakage, `3` the tool failed. The **first** run on a healthy install exits `2` (no baseline). | unit test: `ResilienceTests.cs` (`UnchangedIsExactlyABaselineWithNoVerdictTermOnForEveryCombination`); the script compiles `src/Progenitor.Core/Resilience/*.cs` itself, so its exit code is that verdict |
| `[Compat] RewriteMode` `Off` / `DryRun` (default) / `Apply` | experimental | Retargets a plugin's metadata references through a per-build drift map. Every failure loads the original bytes; `Apply` needs the map on the allowlist. **Not verified in-game** — no V Rising patch has shipped since it landed. | unit test: `CompatRewriterTests.cs` (`A_dry_run_reports_everything_and_returns_the_original_array`); Core: `Compat/CompatRewriteHost.cs` |

It scans **Core's** references, not each plugin's. It cannot see behavior changes, native drift
inside a function body, or a numeric literal a plugin author copied out of the game's metadata.

## Dev bridge ops

`Progenitor.Dev` loopback JSON (`127.0.0.1`, token file). **Do not** ship the DLL; the public zip
omits it and `tools/package.ps1` asserts that. One main-thread op at a time. Call:
`node tools/mcp/call.mjs <client|server> <op> [json]`. Status: **experimental** for the whole
surface.

| Op | Intent | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `ping` / `ops` / `status` / `logs` | Liveness, op list, dump, ring buffer (`since`, `max`, `filter`, `level`). Ping also reports `gameIdentity` (the active Core generation's `PrefabEditWireApply.LocalIdentity`, same string as the prefab-edit wire, or null when unread / the bound Core is not Bootstrap's current `reload.alcName`; a retained older collectible Core is never used) and `images` (`[{name, sha256, version, generation?}]` for Core, Bootstrap and each reloaded plugin; `version` is the informational version `year.month.release+revision` from existing reload bookkeeping; `sha256` null if unknown). Source-checked, not live-verified. | tutorial section 8 |
| `bridge_status` | Socket thread. Never takes the one-op gate. Liveness probe after a main-thread timeout: `{entered, op, id, enteredAt, abandoned, elapsedMs, queued, delayed, lastDrainAt, lastDrainMs, frame, heartbeatSeconds, uiTickTiming, freezeWatchdog, freezeSeconds, freezeDumpCount, dumpInProgress, lastFreezeDump}`. `dumpInProgress` is true while a dedicated dump-writer thread is inside `MiniDumpWriteDump`. `lastFreezeDump` is null or `{path, at, frozenMs}` after that thread finishes a successful dump. Optional args `uiTickTiming` (bool), `heartbeatSeconds` (int, 0 disables), and `freezeSeconds` (int, default 10, 0 disables) toggle the removable Tick/Relayout stopwatch, the 5 s drain heartbeat, and the freeze-watch dump threshold. A freeze-watch poll from this op may start a writer thread; it never writes the minidump on the socket thread. Source-checked, not live-verified. | this row |
| `bridge_users` | Socket thread. Never takes the one-op gate. Who has been driving this bridge: `{current, recent}`, the current user and up to three distinct previous ones, each `{ai, name, id, client, tool, pid, model, firstSeen, lastSeen, requests, lastOp, ageSeconds}`. Callers identify themselves with an optional `who` object on each request; `ping` and `status` carry the same data as `bridgeUsers`, and any response carries `bridgeUser` when another user was seen in the last 10 minutes. Persisted beside the token as `progenitor-dev.users.json`. Source-checked and unit-tested, not live-verified. | [bridge.md](bridge.md#bridge-users) |
| `dump` | Socket thread. Never takes the one-op gate. Starts a dedicated short-lived background thread that writes one minidump of this process (`MiniDumpNormal \| MiniDumpWithHandleData \| MiniDumpWithThreadInfo`, no full memory) through dbghelp `MiniDumpWriteDump` to `BepInEx/progenitor-dumps/freeze-<utc>-frame<n>.dmp`. Returns immediately `{started: true, path}` (or `{started: false, error}` if the cap of 3 is reached or a dump is already in progress). Args `{reason}`. `bridge_status.dumpInProgress` is true until that thread finishes, then `lastFreezeDump` is `{path, at, frozenMs}`. The writer holds no framework lock around `MiniDumpWriteDump` (that call suspends other threads itself). Unload / `Stop` cancel the poller and do not join a dump in flight. Shares the cap of 3 dumps per process with the freeze watchdog (which polls the drain/frame counter every 2 s from a Dev background thread and starts the same kind of dump after `freezeSeconds` of a stuck frame). The watchdog also logs one `freeze began frozenForMs=… frame=… window=… userIdleS=… hint=…` line per stall (even after the cap) and `freeze ended frozenForMs=… frame=… window=…` on the first frame after it, with the game window's minimized/visible/cloaked/foreground state and the user's input idle time; a stall while the window is hidden is the engine waiting for the last presentation, not Progenitor code. Missing dbghelp is logged, not thrown. Source-checked and unit-tested; the began/ended lines are not yet live-verified. | this row |
| `reload` / `core_reload` / `reload_status` | Plugin folder scan; Core swap; `lastReload`. | tutorial section 8, [bridge-ops.md](bridge-ops.md#reload) |
| `time` | Server. World speed through `TimeScaleBridge` (the `ctx.Time.Freeze` / `SlowMotion` leases). `action` `status` (default), `speed` with `value` in [0.001, 1], `freeze`, `resume`, `release`. The bridge holds one lease (owner `progenitor.dev bridge`); `resume` and `release` drop it. Every action returns `{action, ok, refused, released, value, paused, engineScale, governing, bridgeHolds, leases:[{owner, kind, scale, governs}], readError}`. MCP `progenitor_time`. Source-checked and unit-tested, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#time) |
| `item_catalog` | Both sides, read-only: `ctx.Items.Catalog()` of this process. Args `filter` (GUID, or substring of the prefab or display name), `limit` (rows, default 20, at most 500), `rebuild` (drop the cache first to time a build). Returns `{side, built, count, withDisplayName, custom, buildMs, epoch, builds, filter, matches, entries:[{guid, prefab, display, maxAmount, category, type, custom}]}`. Source-checked and built, not live-verified. | [Item catalog](#item-catalog) |
| `photo` | Photo mode through `PhotoModeBridge`. `action` `status` (default; either side), `on` (client; `enable: true` first turns `[PhotoMode] Enabled` on), `off` (client; leaves and resumes a pause it asked for). Returns `{side, enabled, active, paused, pausePending, hudHidden, pose, speed, fov, holdsInput, serverPauses, action, ok, refused, changed}`. The pause itself is the `PhotoModePause` keybind (`keybind_press`). MCP `progenitor_photo`. Source-checked and unit-tested, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#photo) |
| `container_preview` | `ctx.Inventory.Preview` through `ContainerPreviewTestBridge`, for the live experiments E1 to E5. `status` (default, either side): on the client the running preview (`state`, `phase`, `closeReason`, `reason`, `networkId`, client `target` and `inventory` entities, `hooks`, `hiddenUi`, `droppedEvents`) plus the open HUD menu (`menu`, 3 = the container window), `mapperTarget` and `mapperInteractBuff`; on the server every server-side preview (`user`, `container`, `entity`, `secondsLeft`, `leaseHolders`, `syncInventory`, `syncSuspended`, and `synced`: each entity the preview holds a sync entry on, with `users` (the list length), `thisUser` (its copies of the user) and `inventory`), every shared streaming lease (`user`, `point`, `holders`), `saveGuard`, `saveSuspended` (a world save holds the entries out), `worldSaveHookArmed` (without it a synced preview is refused), `syncReleasing` (ended previews whose releases still retry), `syncSweepPending` and `syncRecords` (the startup sweep's records), `observationRecords` and `observationSweepPending` (the streaming-origin records), and per lease `proxySuspended`. Client: `open` (`id`, `keepOpen`, `seconds`, `hideBag`, `replicationTimeout`, `dropEvents`, `hudGate`: false, for E2 only, leaves the game's auto-close alone; `syncInventory`: false, for E1b only, leaves the chest's inventory unsynced), `close`, `bag` (`mode` `alpha` (default) or `inactive`, how the next preview hides the bag); server: `guard` (`on` false leaves the inventory sync entries in the next world save, for the E1b persistence read only; on again by itself after that save, after 5 minutes, and at every Core install), `proxy_reset` (`user`, a platform id: put that user's streaming origin back to zero, a one-off repair; refused while a lease holds the user). MCP `progenitor_container_preview`. Source-checked and unit-tested, not live-verified. | [modules.md](modules.md#container-preview) |
| `locate` | Client. `ctx.Hud.Locate` pings through `HudLocateBridge`: `pin` (world `x`, `y` up, `z`, `label`, `seconds`, `arrival`, `maps`), `status` (default; every ping's last drawn frame: `labelText`, `meters`, `from`, `character`, `onScreen`, `screen`, `viewport`, `pinholeViewport`, camera), `clear`. MCP `progenitor_locate`. Source-checked and unit-tested (`LocateArgsTests`), not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-ui.md](bridge-ops-ui.md#locate) |
| `camera_preview` | Client, read-only. The newest running `ctx.Camera.Preview` through `CameraPreviewBridge`: `active`, `held`, `point`, `anchor`, `yaw`, `screenOffset`, `wantedScreenX`, `screenX` (where the point projects on the live camera now), `viewport`, `error`, and the framing's readings (`measured`, `camera`, `fov`, `aspect`, `rect`, `depth`, `viewportTarget`, `lookAtViewportX`, `oneMeterRightViewportX`, `lateral`, `pinholeLateral`, `rightAgreement`). MCP `progenitor_camera_preview`. Source-checked and built, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-ui.md](bridge-ops-ui.md#camera_preview) |
| `users` / `players` / `worlds` | Users (including the handshake token), characters, world names. | [bridge-ops.md](bridge-ops.md#users) |
| `command` / `broadcast` / `chat_observe` | Inject `.…`; server chat send; watch `ChatMessageServerEvent`. | tutorial section 8 (`command`, `ghost`) |
| `query` / `get` / `set` / `components` / `spawn` / `buff` / `heal` | Entity inspect and mutate. `world` required. | [bridge-ops.md](bridge-ops.md#query) |
| `inspect` | Every component on one entity with decoded fields in one call. `depth` decodes buffer elements, `max` is the byte cap, `only` narrows. | tutorial section 8 |
| `teleport` / `give` | Server. Move a character to a point or onto another (`TeleportUtilityServer.Teleport`; the position-component fallback is size- and layout-guarded and refuses rather than writing blind); add an item stack (`ServerGameManager.TryAddInventoryItem`, reports `added` / `didNotFit`). | tutorial section 8 |
| `unlock` | Server, isolated test server only (refused under `C:\VRisingServer`). TEST SETUP: `status` (read only: unlock buffer counts, blueprint/recipe prefab totals, still-locked lists), `all` (every `BlueprintData` and `RecipeData` prefab), `prefabs` (listed names or hashes), `lock`, `revert` (removes the GUIDs recorded in `BepInEx/config/progenitor-unlock-<user>.json`), `dryRun`. Adds GUIDs to `UnlockedProgressionElement` and raises the game's `UnlockProgressionDebugEvent` so its handler rebuilds the derived buffers. Source-checked and unit-tested (`DevUnlockArgsTests`). `all` and `status` ran live on 2026-10-04: a connected client sees the bulk part after it rejoins. `lock` and `revert` not run. The record names the Progenitor mod that registered each GUID it lists (`owners`), and `status` lists `dangling` progression rows whose prefab is gone. | [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#unlock) |
| `build_search` | Client, main thread. The [build menu search](modules.md#build-menu-search) box through Core's internal `BuildSearchTestBridge` (reflection): `status` (open, focus, query, counts, each cell's names and whether the search hid it), `open` / `close` (the build menu itself, as the B key does, no input or focus), `hover` (a cell's tooltip without the mouse), `select` (a cell picked as a click does), `set`, `clear` (the x button), `focus`, `blur`, `type` (queued text and chords through the typed-key pump). Needs the build menu open for every action but `status`. Live 2026-10-04 on the Steam client. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#build_search) |
| `materials` | Either side, main thread. [Material sources](modules.md#material-sources) through Core's internal `MaterialsTestBridge` (reflection): `status` (the status line, hooks, payments, faults, shortfalls, views sent, recomputes, skips; client views; server open operations and cached views) and, on the server, `inject` (one-shot fault: move an item out of a container right after the next governed check) and `clear`. Reports `shortfalls`. Source-checked and built, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#materials) |
| `servants` | Server, main thread. A castle's servants and hunts through Core's public `ServantBridge` (the `ctx.Servants` code; not a plugin API), castle by `territory` or `heart`: `list` (servants and hunts), `zones` (discovered zones with a servant mission, active hunts, slots, hunt lengths), `send` (`ctx.Servants.Send` as `progenitor.dev bridge`; picks a zone when none is named), `empty` (move servants' inventories into castle containers with the inventory engine's `TransferAll`; reports moved and left, never destroys unless the test-only `discard`), `finish` (make hunts end now: method `timers`, the game's `ServantTimersDisabled` setting until they return, server-wide; or `row`, moves the rows' start back), `timers` (read or set that setting). Refused on the client. Built and game-free tested, **not run live**. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#servants) |
| `inventory_stamp` | Server, main thread, read only. `ctx.Inventory.ChangeStamp` of named containers (`index:version`, `#1` or `/out` for a station output) in one batch through Core's public `InventoryStampBridge` (not a plugin API): resolved inventory, stamp, clock, `AnyChangedSince` with `since`, batch time and the audit counters (`misses` must stay 0, fresh stamps, raw-read failures by check). Refused on the client. Source-checked, built and game-free tested, **not run live**. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#inventory_stamp) |
| `dye` | Client, main thread, read only. [Dye all](modules.md#dye-all) through Core's internal `DyeAllTestBridge` (reflection): the last scan step and its inputs (menu type, world, on, standing down), every known `TransmogContainer` with its HUD menu and whether it is active, the cells listed and watched, the hint, the hold phase and the last hold; `{"cells":true}` adds the shown cells' screen centers. See [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#dye). |
| `map` | Client, main thread. The world map, focus-free: `status`, `open` (idempotent; raises the full-screen menu with `HUDMenuManager.GoToMenu` and selects the map with `FullscreenMenuMapper.SetState(Map)`), `close` (`HUDMenuManager.CloseMenu`, only when the map is shown). Replies after Core's `WorldMap.IsOpen` agrees for 4 frames. Refuses with no world, with the Mods workspace open, over another HUD menu or a full-screen game view. Source-checked and built, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#map) |
| `key` | Client, main thread. Focus-free key input: `tap` / `hold` / `down` / `up` / `release` (`keys` such as `M` or `Ctrl+R`, `ms`) queue whole-keyboard state events through `InputSystem.QueueEvent`; `type` queues text events; `status`. Every press is released (deadline, `up`/`release`, unload). Reports focus, `backgroundBehavior` and whether the keyboard applied each key; `background:true` opts in to `IgnoreFocus` for the op. Source-checked and built, not live-verified. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#key) |
| `nav` | Client, main thread. NAV1 [controller and keyboard navigation](ui.md#controller-and-keyboard-navigation-nav1): `state` (the focus: surface, pane, marked, the focused control), `press {button}` (one step through the model), `pad {button, ms, background}` (a virtual gamepad's press through the real device read; the game sees a controller), `pad_remove`. Buttons `up`, `down`, `left`, `right`, `a`, `b`, `x`, `lt`, `rt`. Built and game-free tested; run live at the title screen 2026-10-10. | [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#nav) |
| `snapshot` / `restore` | One entity's unmanaged component blobs out (base64 plus decoded) and back. Each row carries a layout stamp (game build id + `TypeIndex` + `StableTypeHash` + size); restore refuses a size mismatch, a stamp mismatch, a managed/shared/buffer component, or one that is now missing. `strict` (default) writes nothing unless every entry passes; `strict: false` overrides only a stamp mismatch, with a `warning`. **Not a save** — use `world_save`. | tutorial section 8 |
| `prefab` | Prefab lookup and dump by guid or name. | [bridge-ops.md](bridge-ops.md#prefab) |
| `save` / `store` / `world_save` | `ModSave` flush; JSON bag; `TriggerSave`. | [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#save) |
| `connect` / `host` / `host_capture` / `disconnect` / `quit` | Client join / private game / quit (`Application.Quit`). | [bridge-ops-player.md](bridge-ops-player.md#connect), [bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#disconnect) |
